## Identity adjudication · `sec:identity:record`

**Record (Identity adjudication)** · `rec:identity:decision` Status: accepted. Scope: Proposed digests, provenance identities, exact comparisons and their admission discipline. Depends on: none. Does not establish: Matching hashes establish no authenticity, correctness, independence or deployment readiness.

This document lays down, self-containedly, a discipline for digests and identities in a corpus of validated typed objects, generated artifacts, evidence reports, deployment profiles, and releases. It is organized around one question — _is there a benefit from hashing this?_ — because a digest is justified only by a decision it makes possible or cheaper. One Formulation states the question, one Procedure walks every proposal through it, and a Case analysis receives the outcome, including the documented stop in which the right answer is no digest at all. The discipline prescribes no hash construction anywhere: it specifies the properties an identity must deliver, each for the benefit it provides, and leaves every scheme to the recipe record. It is generic: nothing here names a particular repository, tool, or algorithm. As adoption data, unlike the procedure, this repository's adoption data name the repository, its tools and its pin digest. This document is self-contained and cites only itself; where an adopting corpus uses it beside other disciplines, their alignment is fixed by the corpus's recorded adoption decisions, not by this text. Acceptance of this document presupposes acceptance of no other document: where another discipline's artifact is consumed — a labeling convention, a set of reserved kinds, a format's declared environment classes — this discipline consumes the artifact as adoption data and asks nothing of its provenance.

The document practices the labeling discipline it assumes: it is a source in the corpus it governs. The label at each heading or environment head is that environment's mint; a parenthesized label in running text is a same-owner citation; material in fenced blocks and double-backtick spans is displayed without participating. The document title is publication metadata, not an environment head; it mints nothing and participates in nothing. Every label here has area `identity`, each environment's kind names its genre, and environments carry no numbers: the mint at each head is the sole name of its environment, and every internal reference is a citation. A recipe _warrants_ a property in the ordinary sense of vouching for it; the word bears no relation to any labeling discipline's warrants.

## The question · `sec:identity:question`

**Formulation (The benefit question)** · `formul:identity:benefit-question` Given an object or artifact a contributor proposes to hash: is there a benefit from hashing it — which decision becomes possible or cheaper, and for which consumer — and only then, which class of identity should carry it? This document fixes the procedure that answers both questions for every typed object, generated artifact, evidence report, deployment profile, and release of an adopting corpus. Matching hashes establish none of authenticity, correctness, independence, or deployment readiness (`warn:identity:non-claims`).

**Model (Objects and boundaries)** · `model:identity:objects` The corpus holds authoritative typed objects; artifacts rendered from them; evidence reports about them; profiles aggregating requirements; and releases aggregating everything. As a running illustration, one adopting corpus's identities form a graph of immediate dependencies — the names below are that corpus's, not this document's — of which one path runs:

```text
ModelId → PlanId → BuildId → BundleId → InterfaceId → ProfileId → ReleaseId
```

An arrow points from dependency to dependent: the right side binds the left as an immediate identity dependency (`rule:identity:immediate-edges`), with fan-in where an object aggregates several — a release binds its profile, its required evidence reports, and its distributed artifacts alike. The boundaries that matter to identity are package, process, cache, publication, distribution, deployment, and signature. As adoption data, this repository builds no identity chain; the illustrated path admits no local identity by naming it. An identity earns its place only at such a boundary; inside one owner, the typed value itself is the comparison.

**Definition (Digest; recipe)** · `def:identity:recipe` A digest is the output of a fixed function over a fixed presentation of a value, and it evidences equality with respect to one recipe, under that recipe's collision assumptions — nothing else. Exact equality belongs to direct typed comparison and exact byte comparison; a digest buys their effect across a boundary at the price of a collision assumption, and the assurance class states whether that price is acceptable. A recipe names its projection (which content enters), its canonicalization (how that content is presented), its primitive (the digest function), its domain separator (which role it serves), and its recipe identifier (under which consumers recompute and migrations occur). A corpus's chosen constructions are named in recipe records and prescribed nowhere else. As adoption data, the first-party marker-policy identity is admitted under (`amend:identity:marker-policy-admission`); no general recipe convention is implied.

**Table (Identity properties and their benefits)** · `tab:identity:properties` The following are the properties an identity can be required to deliver, each demanded not for its own sake but for the benefit it provides:

| Property | Benefit it provides | Failure without it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Deterministic over meaning | consumers compare identities instead of re-deriving objects; cache and reuse become mechanical | equal objects hash apart; the digest decides nothing |
| Complete over semantic content | a change of meaning always changes the recipe input, and matching digests then fail except with the recipe's residual collision probability; staleness is detectable | silent semantic drift under a stable identity |
| Free of incidental content | re-serialization, reordering, and rebuilds change nothing; presentation invariance holds | false staleness; consumers learn to ignore the identity |
| Domain-separated by role | an identity cannot be replayed as a claim of another kind; evidence roles stay distinct | a digest quoted in one role masquerades in another |
| Recipe-identified | consumers know exactly how to recompute; recipes change only by explicit migration | ambiguous verification; silent redefinition |
| Collision- and second-preimage-resistant | a matched identity computationally pins the object the decision was about | substitution and cache poisoning under a matching digest |
| Recomputable by any consumer | verification without trusting the producer; recomputation is the check | the digest is decoration over a producer's claim |

Any construction delivering the required properties qualifies; which one a corpus chose is a fact of the recipe record (`red:identity:scheme-to-properties`).

**Table (Assurance classes and required properties)** · `tab:identity:class-properties` Each admitted identity belongs to one assurance class, and each class requires a stated subset of the properties of (`tab:identity:properties`): a cell reads _required_, optionally with the scope or mechanism over which the property is required; _not applicable_; or a condition, stated in the cell:

| Property | Semantic | Artifact | Provenance | Evidence | Release |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Deterministic | required | required, over exact bytes | required | required | required |
| Complete | over the semantic projection | over the exact bytes | over the named revision, tree, or input set | over the full typed report subject | over the entire canonical manifest |
| Free of incidentals | required | not applicable beyond path and role metadata | recipe-defined | required | required |
| Domain-separated | required | bound through the manifest role | required | required — load-bearing | required — load-bearing |
| Recipe-identified | required | required — an algorithm alone underspecifies projection, framing, and normalization | required | required | required |
| Collision-resistant | required | required | required where release-bound; per threat model otherwise | required | required — carries the signing benefit |
| Recomputable | required | required | required where release-bound | required | required |

A provenance identity that is only a locator — an internal name for a revision or input set, never release-bound — carries the weaker conditional row; release reachability forces the stronger.

**Table (Assurance mechanisms)** · `tab:identity:mechanisms` Distinct mechanisms establish distinct things, and none substitutes for another:

| Mechanism | Establishes | Does not establish |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Type | Representable shape | Cross-field validity |
| Validator | Declared constraints and invariants | Authenticity or implementation correctness |
| Test, proof, or execution | Evidence for a scoped claim | Identity or universal correctness |
| Semantic identity | Computational equality of a canonical typed projection | Validity or authenticity by itself |
| Artifact digest | Computational equality of exact bytes | Meaning or semantic correctness |
| Provenance identity | A named source revision, tree, or input set | Correctness of that source |
| Evidence-report identity | One typed report applies to named subjects | Honesty or implementation independence |
| Signature | A named authority approved an identity | Correctness of the signed object |
| Reproducible build | Independent builds produced equal bytes | An uncompromised toolchain |

A hash never replaces the owning type, the validator, or the evidence requirement.

**Example (Illustrative inventory)** · `ex:identity:inventory` For illustration only, a typical corpus might already hold the following. The Class column carries only the five assurance classes of (`sec:identity:cases`); an em dash marks an entry that has none, because no digest exists or because none has yet been admitted (`req:identity:admission-record`):

| Identity | Class | Standing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Revision and tree identifiers | provenance | retained; never a protocol identity |
| Publication metadata identifiers | provenance | retained; publication-only, contained by (`rule:identity:provenance-containment`) |
| An upstream anchor-set hash | semantic | retained |
| A model semantic hash | semantic | retained |
| A behavioural hash gating major versions | semantic | retained; not propagated as a runtime identity |
| Generated-file exact comparisons | — | freshness by exact bytes under (`case:identity:artifact`); no digest |
| A profile hash awaiting its consumer | — | pre-admission; no assurance until a named consumer decides from it |
| Raw hash fields in a profile schema | — | pre-admission; must gain owned recipes and typed references before release |

## The procedure · `sec:identity:procedure`

**Procedure (Adjudication)** · `alg:identity:adjudication` Every proposal walks one tree:

```text
proposed digest
    ↓
typed and validated?                  ── no → validate before any
    ↓ yes                                     identity is admitted
which decision would equality change?
    none                              ── → no identity: stop
    ↓ a named consumer's
is that equality already given on the path —
reviewed at this boundary · compared anyhow · parent-assured?
    yes                               ── → no identity: stop
    ↓ no
record the admission facts
    ↓
dispatch on class:
    semantic | artifact | provenance | evidence | release

artifact branch:  canonical renderer → artifact bytes
                  → exact freshness comparison
                  → byte digest only when independently
                    distributed or release-bound

evidence branch:  execution → typed report payload
                  → validated report envelope → report identity
                  → deployment profile or release manifest
```

The benefit nodes are decided by (`crit:identity:benefit`); an accepted walk records (`req:identity:admission-record`) before it dispatches; the two stopping branches terminate in (`case:identity:no-identity`).

**Criterion (Benefit)** · `crit:identity:benefit` A digest benefits the corpus if and only if a named consumer's decision becomes possible or cheaper through mechanical equality that nothing already on that path provides — where what a path can already provide is fixed by (`tab:identity:mechanisms`). Equality a standing review supersedes is no benefit at the review's own boundary: the review judges content, the digest only equality, and the weaker check cannot add to the stronger. Across a later boundary of (`model:identity:objects`) — cache, publication, distribution, deployment, signature — a digest may still bind what was reviewed to what arrives, and that binding is a distinct benefit the review does not provide. Equality a direct typed comparison already performs is no benefit, and equality the parent identity already carries is no benefit.

**Requirement (Admission record)** · `req:identity:admission-record` Admission is per identity class — one recipe, one role, one consumer-decision pattern; individual identity values flow through their class's record and are never admitted one by one. An accepted class records: the complete typed object or exact artifact bytes identified; the package owning the recipe; the producer; the present consumer, arriving in the same change set or within a recorded, deadline-bound migration — "the same implementation series" means exactly this; that consumer's exact accept, reject, cache, or reuse decision; the assurance class — semantic, artifact, provenance, evidence, or release, as fixed by (`sec:identity:cases`); the recipe, by identifier (`def:identity:recipe`), warranting every property its assurance class requires (`tab:identity:class-properties`); the exact stale conditions; the migration behavior; and the explicit non-claims. Fields are validated as parts of their owning object and are never independently hashed merely to detect changes.

**Requirement (Stop record)** · `req:identity:stop-record` A no-identity outcome is recorded: the proposal, the deciding branch of (`alg:identity:adjudication`), the date, and any condition under which the walk is retaken. The absence of a digest is then the corpus's documented state, and the same proposal is not re-adjudicated from nothing.

## Case analysis · `sec:identity:cases`

**Case (Semantic identity)** · `case:identity:semantic` One meaning with several possible encodings, or consumption across a package, process, cache, or publication boundary. The identity is computed over the canonical projection of the validated object under its recipe (`def:identity:recipe`) and delivers the full semantic column of (`tab:identity:class-properties`). No construction is prescribed; the recipe records the one in use. As adoption data, pn-records uses the marker-policy construction admitted under (`amend:identity:marker-policy-admission`), and pn-host uses the storage constructions admitted under (`amend:identity:storage-admission`).

**Case (Artifact digest)** · `case:identity:artifact` Exact bytes, independently distributed or release-bound. The required properties are the artifact column of (`tab:identity:class-properties`), and a release-manifest entry binds artifact role, canonical relative path, schema or media type, recipe identifier, and digest. An artifact digest does not become a semantic identity unless one reviewed canonical byte encoding is explicitly defined as the semantic object. Committed generated publications take the freshness sub-branch: exact expected-byte comparison decides them, and no digest is added — an implementation may realize exact comparison through a transient internal hash, which is an optimization, not an identity, and never persists or publishes. As adoption data, the documents engine performs the fixed-point byte comparisons directly. The correspondence gate in ci/design.py separately pins the normalized inventory, constants, crates, bites and questions projections with SHA-256 under its digest function; those comparison pins are not admitted semantic identities, and their computational equality retains the digest collision assumption. The host fixture's build receipt digest is a local comparison pin, not an admitted identity: its benefit is comparing the selected artifact across the build and test boundary without retaining an artifact copy, and its assurance is SHA-256 collision resistance alone, with no authenticity; its retention across that boundary is not the transient-hash freshness exemption. The schema-artifact admission of (`dec:api:schema`) uses four typed namespace/version coordinates, not four digests: org.peer-network.test.api-control-schema, org.peer-network.test.api-control-register, org.peer-network.test.api-node-schema and org.peer-network.test.api-node-register, each at major one and minor zero. Their content is public; there are no hidden fields, nonces, salts or signature message roles. Under (`req:identity:stop-record`), adjudication on 2026-11-13 stops at direct namespace/version and exact-byte comparison for source and generated freshness; an additional semantic hash would decide nothing those comparisons do not. The artifact-producing cut owes any release-manifest artifact digest only when a release consumer exists, under this case and (`case:identity:release`), with the role, path, media/schema type and recipe recorded there; no digest of an absent artifact is assigned. Revisit only for that independent distribution consumer. Transient control requests and responses retain the no-identity outcome of (`dec:api:encoding-boundary`). The control schema coordinate alone has an implemented artifact and exact-source carriage under ``INTERCHANGE::def:interchange:control-schema``; the other three remain reserved without artifacts. This status admits no executable schema, register parser or served edition.

**Case (Provenance identity)** · `case:identity:provenance` A named source: a revision, a tree, an exact canonical input set. The provenance column of (`tab:identity:class-properties`) carries it, locator-grade or release-bound as its reach requires. It names material, claims nothing about the material's correctness, and stays separate from semantic and artifact identity. As adoption data, this repository carries no paper or pack commit, tree or archive digest because those sources are not publicly fetchable.

**Case (Evidence identity)** · `case:identity:evidence` A validated typed report envelope binding at least report role, report schema, exact subject identities, producer or implementation identity, configuration where relevant, result status, and canonical payload or payload digest. The evidence column of (`tab:identity:class-properties`) applies in full, and domain separation by role is the load-bearing property: it is what keeps one report from being quoted as another kind of claim. A raw digest without role and subject binding is not evidence identity.

**Case (Release identity)** · `case:identity:release` The canonical release manifest aggregating the deployment profile, required evidence references, distributed artifacts with their byte digests, release policy, and explicit source revision and date. The release column of (`tab:identity:class-properties`) applies in full; domain separation and collision resistance carry the signing benefit. If signing is introduced, the release-manifest identity is the signing root, and internal objects are not signed separately unless they hold an independently defined authority boundary of their own, which then carries its own typed role and verification policy.

**Case (No identity)** · `case:identity:no-identity` The affirmative stop, as first-class as any admission. No digest is created when direct typed comparison suffices; when the parent identity already provides the assurance; when the value crosses no package, process, cache, publication, distribution, deployment, or signature boundary of (`model:identity:objects`) and has no independent lifecycle of its own; when importance is the only motive, importance being no consumer; when the value is ephemeral local evidence, such as ordinary CI logs nobody consumes as release evidence; or when the purpose never required byte-equal provenance, because every change is reviewed at that boundary and the review judges more than equality. Failing (`crit:identity:benefit`) is a result, not an omission: the walk ends here deliberately, the stop is recorded under (`req:identity:stop-record`), and the absence of a digest is then the corpus's documented state.

Under (`req:identity:stop-record`), adjudication on 2026-11-13 stops at the direct-typed-comparison branch of (`alg:identity:adjudication`) for the complete storage audit of (`amend:storage:tree-audit`). pn-host produces an immutable inventory of complete retained logical values for exact comparison by public rebuild and the storage re-admission dossier. Direct typed comparison supplies the required snapshot-consistency decision, so no audit digest, snapshot token or new serialization is admitted. Tips alone cannot bind unreferenced histories. The existing result-position recipe names positions only and is not repurposed as a whole-store commitment. Revisit this no-identity decision before introducing a separately transferable or authenticated audit certificate; importance or a smaller comparison value alone does not supply that need.

Under (`req:identity:stop-record`), adjudication on 2026-11-14 stops at the direct-typed-comparison branch of (`alg:identity:adjudication`) for the designed scalar binary64 carrier of (`rule:design:binary64-evidence`). pn-records owns the typed object and pn-host its separate canonical fragment codec; the consumer compares the complete original question, exact public inputs, domain, predicate and enclosures directly. No digest, signature role, document namespace or externally assigned tag is admitted: the codec's format edition and constructor numbers are local discriminators, not identities. A hash is set aside because it adds no decision to that comparison. A carrier is passed by value, or referred to by its enclosing record's ordinary array index without a persistent identity. Retake (`alg:identity:adjudication`) before a store persists a carrier or a published package or another record refers to one, as well as before a separately transferred, cached or authenticated certificate needs a named equality decision not supplied by complete typed comparison; the held publication cut must make that adjudication before adding a surrounding public envelope. These events reopen the walk rather than assigning an identity automatically.

Under (`req:identity:stop-record`), adjudication on 2026-11-14 follows the direct-typed-comparison branch for (`dec:design:rung-anchor-representation`) and (`dec:design:comparison-verdict-record`). The designed schema identities are the direct name/edition pairs (scalar-binary64, 2), (records-numerics-FloorComparison, 2), (records-ridge-ComparisonRecord, 2), (records-controller-Attempt, 2) and (records-controller-Record, 2). pn-records owns the typed values and pn-host their explicitly selected canonical fragment codecs; graph, records, epoch and controller consumers compare the complete question, original operands, evidence and result, not a digest. The scalar, FloorComparison, ridge ComparisonRecord and controller Attempt and Record edition-two fragments are implemented as explicitly selected typed codecs with supplied-case controls; the replacement publication edition remains designed. These local schema discriminators add no hash recipe, external document namespace, signature role or persistent carrier identity. A hash is set aside because it changes no equality decision at this boundary. Old schema meanings, bytes and publication identities remain unchanged. A publication consumer must revisit (`alg:identity:adjudication`) before assigning the surrounding replacement publication edition and binding its schema: no identity or digest of that absent artifact is claimed here. Standalone controls therefore do not change a byte of an existing published package, and no producer can label an old record with a new schema identity.

**Amendment (Retained result position identity)** · `amend:identity:result-position` As adoption data under (`crit:identity:benefit`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), admit the pn-host/result-position/v1 recipe for a public position in a retained result tree. Its owner and producer are pn-host's admitted carrier projection; its consumers are result commit, parent foreign-key lookup, tip-relative reads and public rebuild in the same tree implementation series. Public rebuild requires public-input restoration; the disposable first read-only instance instead requires fixture-premised restoration under the exception of (`req:storage:generation-authentication`) and (`req:storage:judgement-publication`) before its restricted executable persistence, with every other admission duty retained. The benefit is a bounded, independently recomputable parent and tip reference for alternative histories whose complete marker structures contain collections: the chain's scope/boundary tuple cannot distinguish siblings, a reusable marker label or per-history position cannot do so alone, and a complete structural key need not fit the store's key bound. A store-local serial cannot be recomputed from public records. Hashing complete package bytes would turn every conflict into a new position. Rejecting large marker structures merely to fit an index would narrow the admitted domain for storage convenience. Those alternatives are set aside. This is semantic computational equality of the projected position, not byte equality of packages, authenticity, finality, mathematical admission or a normative identity assigned by the paper. Hidden fields: none; every input is public, with no salt or nonce. This is a public, non-normative position identifier of this deployment; it selects among no valid objects. The residue never replaces complete equality or public replay.

Under (`alg:identity:adjudication`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), the initial acceptance member of (`def:identity:result-position-projection`) uses the general acceptance projection against an empty base. This fixes the recipe's acceptance interpretation without changing its role, tag, owner or benefit. One function projects every admitted marker and preserves each published acceptance constructor, while excluding only the named proof and equivalent opening evidence. A published initial commitment is public marker data carried nowhere else in C, so it participates in position identity and distinguishes contexts under one S for ContextConflict. A universal empty array is set aside because it introduces an index-dependent member shape; a universal [0, []] is set aside because it erases published commitments and leaves their encoding distinguishable only through the adjacent policy projection. This adjudication supplies a deterministic projection, not authority to admit a constructor that the policy or recognition refuses.

Under (`alg:identity:adjudication`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), the lineage and accepted members of (`def:identity:result-position-projection`) use the general difference rules for every marker, with empty bases for an initial marker; transition_initial is null for the initial marker in C as well as one nested in transition_initial. This fixes the recipe's general member forms without changing its identifier. An initial complete-list or commitment marker may retain nonempty lineage, so leaving its member form or ordering implicit would not determine a preimage. A separate initial lineage encoding or an inferred transition member is set aside: the same public fields and bases determine the same member values without an index-specific codec.

Under (`alg:identity:adjudication`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), the set arrays in (`def:identity:result-position-projection`) use UTF-8 text order, and C carries the unique admitted deployment/genesis declaration. This fixes the recipe's bytes without changing its identifier or admission predicates. Canonical map-key order and the publication writer's set order are set aside because they do not order these recipe arrays; selecting a latest genesis edition is set aside because the admitted family contains exactly one declaration.

The prior-identity search reads network-v0.37.4, ``def:comparator:epoch-marker``, ``cond:comparator:marker-lineage-succession``, ``cond:comparator:marker-network-acceptance``, ``cond:comparator:marker-causal-dependence``, ``def:network:conserved-standing-package`` and ``post:deployment:public-object-replay``. These define the structural object and replay duties, not a compact result-tree storage recipe. The paper's marker identity is used as the semantic subject, not redefined as its lookup name. Its explicit non-normativity of implementation hashes is preserved. The existing marker-policy, storage-payload and recognition-disclosure recipes identify different objects: policy, complete family payload and complete disclosure respectively. Reusing the last would make later disclosed candidates or proofs select a new result position. Their recipes remain unchanged. No almost-matching paper recipe is reinterpreted.

**Definition (Result position projection)** · `def:identity:result-position-projection` Let S be the original public context tuple [history identity, initial marker-policy identity, initial policy edition, sequence], taken from the initial admitted context and inherited unchanged by descendants, including either published policy-edition transition. Let C be [S, genesis declaration, initial policy, initial marker projection]: the complete deployment/genesis declaration and PolicyInput use their existing canonical publication projections, and the initial marker uses M below with index zero, null predecessor, acceptance projected against the empty set, an empty accepted set and an empty snapshot map. The genesis member of C is the unique admitted deployment/genesis declaration: marker-policy admission under (`amend:decomposition:marker-policy-content-binding`) binds exactly one and refuses a missing or second declaration with DeploymentBinding. No selection by effective point or carrier order occurs; the owning admission contract is unchanged. C is public starting context, not a newly hashed scope identifier. Equal S with different C is ContextConflict, never a way to join unrelated histories; a genuinely different context must have a distinct published S. The deployment declaration family's publication rule requires each realization to publish its own history identity in S; reuse across realizations is not permitted. A later realization field in that declaration enters this projection only under a new recipe, because its nested publication projection is pinned. The result projection is X = [C, null, M] at a root and [null, parent, M] at a non-root: the parent already binds the starting context, which is not repeated. Parent is null for the first result above that initial context, otherwise the exact 32-byte result-position residue of the explicitly named admitted parent. The root result still carries its terminal marker M, distinct from C's initial marker. Two first results with different terminal markers can share C; a later re-anchor or follow continuation is a child, with its new policy identity, edition and sequence in M, never an invented new root or an alteration of S. Its published continuity and the admitting layer's parent binding are prerequisites, not authority supplied by the hash. A current carrier lacking that continuity cannot be made a re-anchor by storage alone. The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the successor admission cut remains owed by this statement's owner.

M is the array [history, policy_identity, policy_edition, sequence, index, history_position, predecessor_position, lineage, acceptance, accepted, initial_snapshot, transition_initial]. Text is exact UTF-8, indices and positions are unsigned u64 values, and the predecessor position is null only for the initial marker. The validated parent binding fixes which predecessor that position denotes. When the current policy identity, edition or sequence differs from the parent's, transition_initial contains the new policy's complete initial-marker M, whose own transition_initial is null; otherwise it is null. That initial marker binds the transition while parent names the retained result whose state is continued. C supplies the initial marker for a root. The lineage and accepted bases are C's initial marker at a root and the validated parent's marker for an ordinary child. For a re-anchor both bases are transition_initial. For a follow child the lineage base is the parent's marker, whose lineage must be contained with equal immutable interpretation data; only the accepted base is transition_initial. The follow shape names the parent as predecessor, so lineage containment crosses the edition change; accepted-set continuity instead follows the published ledger-continuity decision and does not inherit the old edition's acceptance bound. Transition_initial remains bound for the new edition in both transition shapes. An initial marker nested in C or transition_initial is projected against empty lineage and accepted bases, with its complete lineage, acceptance under the general constructor rule, an empty accepted set, index zero, null predecessor, an empty snapshot map and null transition_initial. Against those empty bases, lineage uses the same member form, UTF-8 identity order and duplicate refusal as every M, and accepted is the same sorted set difference, hence empty.

For every M, lineage contains only records newly present relative to the lineage base, sorted by record identity's UTF-8 bytes, with one [record_identity, destruction_identity, address, amount] per member and no duplicate identity. Address and exact nonnegative amount use their existing canonical publication value projections. Every inherited lineage member must remain with equal immutable interpretation data; loss or substitution refuses InvalidPosition. For every M, accepted contains only the recovered accepted-record set difference from the accepted base, unique exact text ordered by its UTF-8 bytes. Before projection the complete recovered set must extend the accepted base's accepted set and satisfy acceptance and lineage bounds.

One UTF-8 ordering rule governs lineage identities, accepted members and complete-list acceptance members inside M. These members are array elements of this recipe, not map keys: canonical map-key order does not govern them, and the publication writer's set order belongs to another recipe and is not reused.

For every M, acceptance is [0, members] for CompleteList, with members the unique difference of the complete acceptance list from the accepted base's recovered accepted set, ordered by the text's UTF-8 bytes; [1, commitment_bytes] for Commitment; or [2] for WholePrefix. A commitment's uniquely verified complete opening members must equal the complete recovered accepted set; its proof bytes and equivalent opening evidence do not enter M. The initial case applies this general rule against an empty accepted base: [0, []] for CompleteList, [1, commitment_bytes] for Commitment with its published bytes and a uniquely verified complete opening equal to the empty set, or [2] for WholePrefix; its accepted member is the empty sorted set and its snapshot the empty map. Which constructor is admitted remains the policy and recognition rule, with no additional predicate imposed by projection. Initial_snapshot is null for a non-initial marker. Every component comes from the admitted public record and its validated causes, never a database-generated value. Complete cumulative sets at every node with a declared refusal bound are set aside: they repeat equality carried by the parent and turn branch length into a representation limit. The per-node identity preimage is proportional to new lineage and acceptance members and fixed marker fields, plus the initial context at a root or the new initial marker at a transition; validating the supplied complete public record remains separate work.

The parent-binding predicate has three child shapes. An ordinary child has the same history, policy identity, edition and sequence as its parent, index exactly the parent's plus one, predecessor_position equal to the parent's history_position, and complete lineage and accepted set extending the parent's with the increments above. A re-anchor child has a changed policy edition, the new policy's transition_initial in initial shape, index one above that initial marker and predecessor_position equal to its history_position; its increments extend that initial marker. A follow child has a changed policy edition, index exactly the parent's plus one, predecessor_position equal to the parent's marker position, and transition_initial equal to the new edition's initial marker in initial shape; its lineage increment extends the parent's marker and its accepted and acceptance-member increments extend that initial marker under the published ledger-continuity decision. Both transitions require the published continuity and parent binding of their respective deployment response; a changed policy identity or sequence without the published edition transition is not an ordinary child. A root has index one above C's initial marker, predecessor_position equal to that initial marker's position and increments extending it. Checked index overflow, a missing parent, a non-extending increment or a failed shape predicate refuses InvalidPosition. Storage checks these structural predicates; it supplies no authority to choose or publish a breach response.

A third party recomputes a position from the complete ancestry back to the public starting context. A store cannot retain only a suffix, and public rebuild starts at a root. Keeping placement coordinates makes a same-edition re-placement of one structure a sibling rather than a conflict; the admitting layer, which does not reissue closed epochs, refuses that re-placement.

| Member | Identity or retained content | Reason and alternative set aside |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Original S, full genesis declaration, initial policy and initial marker structure | Identity through C at a root, inherited through parent otherwise | Bind the public starting context; terminal policy edition alone would split shared ancestry at re-anchor. |
| Parent result-position residue | Identity | One fixed-size recursive reference binds ancestry; expanding a composite parent key and discriminator at every generation grows with branch length. |
| Current marker history, policy identity/edition, sequence and index | Identity | Fix the current interpretation, including a new edition on a child; inheriting only the root edition would erase a transition. |
| Required disclosed history position and predecessor position | Identity, together with complete M and parent | These are fixed placement attributes of this admitted marker, not globally unique locators. Re-inclusion at a different position is a different placement; position alone cannot distinguish alternative histories. |
| New lineage records' immutable record/destruction identities, amounts and attribution | Identity as increments relative to the validated base | These are causal and interpretation data; a name-only lineage permits substitution under an unchanged key. |
| Acceptance constructor, list increment or fixed commitment; recovered accepted increment | Identity relative to the validated base | Preserve both policy interpretation and accepted membership. Omitting accepted membership would identify distinct snapshots; hashing proof bytes instead would identify the evidence presentation. |
| Marker lookup name | Content | Reusable lookup labels do not distinguish a new causal marker; renaming alone cannot evade equal-position conflict. The immutable policy's initial-name rule still applies. |
| Opening proofs, burn proofs, extra disclosed candidates and unrelated records | Content | Later or alternate evidence does not change the fixed marker; changing retained package bytes still conflicts at an equal position. |
| Settled prefix height and block hash, immutable buffer and linking evidence | Content | The prefix fixes this deployment's author-authority admission view under (`rule:identity:author-chain-view`); it is content only under the finality premise stated below. Complete retained evidence is compared, never upgraded in place. A fixture with no underlying chain has the same definition of X and claims no settlement. |
| Price, acts, debits, outputs, annotations and the complete package bytes | Content | The result at a position must compare all these bytes for retry; their differences must not manufacture siblings. |

The reused declaration, PolicyInput, address and amount projections are exactly those of org.peer-network.test.publication 1.0.0; a later codec cannot silently change this recipe's nested arrays or enum forms. Those four nested projections are retained as code for the lifetime of this recipe. The projection uses semantic equality of these validated public values and canonical set order, not equality of all evidence bytes. A coordinate that commits to a complete realization history remains verification evidence here: substituting that coordinate for M would bind unrelated history outside the marker's causal scope and leave the no-chain instance undefined. It cannot override failed lineage or predecessor validation. Required marker placement coordinates do not change merely because more evidence is disclosed; a changed placement is a new M. An unchanged identity with changed proof, label or package bytes is a conflict, not a proof-upgrade write. The store remains append-only.

The settled height and block hash are the epoch's author-check prefix, not merely evidence of marker disclosure, under (`rule:identity:author-chain-view`) and (`rule:design:marker-recognition`). Their exclusion from position identity relies on the finality premise: a different settled prefix at an equal settled marker signals replacement of that prefix and halts the run without rewriting closed epochs. The halt and the distinct reanchor and follow responses remain this admitted local position contract; network-v0.37.4 ``rem:comparator:finality-breach-continuity`` instead distinguishes a changed oracle projection from an unchanged completed calculation. The same breach signal applies to different immutable buffer or linking evidence at an equal height and hash: that evidence extends strictly beyond the settlement depth, so the disagreement also replaces blocks beyond that depth. This recipe chooses neither response. The observer's current tip is neither identity nor retained content: the stable prefix ends at the settled height and hash, and a later observing tip cannot turn a retry into a conflict.

**Amendment (Result position recipe and refusals)** · `amend:identity:result-position-recipe` Use the framing construction F of (`amend:identity:act-framing`) with r = pn-host/result-position/v1 and role tag PN-result-position-v1: SHA-256 of UTF-8 r, one zero byte, then pn-interchange's strict canonical encoding of [PN-result-position-v1,X]. The result is exactly 32 bytes; textual display, when required, is lowercase hexadecimal and is not an additional hash input. Arrays, nulls, byte strings, preferred integer heads and exact text follow the single canonical codec. No new envelope namespace or alternative canonical parser is allocated. SHA-256 collision and second-preimage resistance, including approximately 128-bit generic classical collision work, are the same assumptions as (`amend:identity:act-framing`). The caller supplies projection/reconstruction limits; the reconstruction limit is sized to the package's own complete sets, not a fixed per-node constant, since validating inherited sets still reconstructs their complete content; checked arithmetic and reservation before allocation apply, with no guessed multiplier or total-package default. The host constructs the preimage value through pn-interchange's reserved bytes, text and array transfers and its empty-map transfer, from buffers admitted by reserve_value_buffer under the caller's ceiling and fallible meter; plain unsigned, boolean and null scalars need no buffers. It encodes that value only through to_canonical_bytes_budgeted under the same cumulative meter and releases complete values and partial children with dispose on success, refusal and callback unwinding. The canonical bytes are identical to the strict legacy encoder's language. Representation preserves the distinct Length, Limit, Reservation and Allocation causes and transfer refusals, including spare capacity after an allocator grants more than requested; no refusal is repaired by shrinking. Admitted-request usage is reported on every outcome, including a panicking callback at the earlier admitted requests. The projection needs no tag value: its role tag is text and its constructor discriminants are unsigned array members. Its only map is the initial empty snapshot, so no nonempty map transfer is needed. Position construction borrows the admitted package; the retaining carrier's separate constructor still clones and re-encodes the complete disclosure outside this projection budget. No allocator census, separate checked sufficiency of the writing pass, observation budget, protected-route budget or production resource default is claimed. Existing individual storage key/edition and signed-coordinate representation limits remain. Collections are not squeezed into the structural-key text bound: only the fixed residue is used as the result lookup component. Invalid projection shape, inconsistent accepted membership, missing required causal data or an inconsistent parent binding refuses InvalidPosition; exceeded caller limits or representation ranges refuse Representation. Unsupported recipe refuses UnsupportedPositionRecipe. None returns a usable identity or an admitted result on refusal.

An equal identity with equal complete canonical package, references and vintages is an idempotent retry and reports inserted=false. An equal identity with different settled-prefix evidence (height, block hash, immutable buffer or linking evidence) is SettledPrefixConflict and writes nothing: the admitting layer receives a finality-breach signal, not an equivocation of acts or prices. This comparison takes precedence over other retained-content differences. With equal complete settled-prefix evidence, different retained content is EpochConflict and writes nothing, including when a hypothetical hash collision is detected by the complete comparison; the admitting layer receives a content conflict at the held position. One conflict with a classified cause is set aside because a caller could skip that field and mistake the breach signal for ordinary content equivocation. A distinct identity with the same admitted parent is a sibling candidate and is not rejected merely for that relation; after all ordinary admission, reference and transaction checks it reports inserted=true. These are storage outcomes delivered to the admitting layer; a sibling is not selected over another branch and an identity is not permission to publish. Same public C, parent and M recompute the same value on every rebuilt store, independent of insertion order, physical branch descriptors, host clock and database placement. Changing a projected member makes the identity stale. Changing projection, tag, framing or primitive requires a new recipe under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`), with replacement rebuild and no reinterpretation of old residues. The borrowed root and ordinary-child position projection and result-qualified retaining carrier are implemented in pn-host. Seven distinct unsorted independent preimage/residue fixtures, changed-member controls, parent and acceptance refusals, reservation-site controls and complete-retention controls cover the present carrier. Runtime mutants omitting parent, lineage or accepted membership, and replacing the lineage increment by its cumulative set, each fail the independent-vector assertion. These are finite conformance, robustness and test-quality witnesses, not collision or settlement evidence. The current admitted carrier expresses neither published edition transition, so re-anchor and follow projection integration and their parent controls remain held until public continuity exists; no continuity is fabricated. Content conflicts, settled-prefix outcomes, transaction, tree reads, persistence and public rebuild remain the later storage cuts' obligations.

**Table (Result identity register)** · `tab:identity:result-position-register` This row is the identity-class register for (`amend:identity:result-position`), not executed evidence or an interchange envelope assignment.

| Recipe and role | Owner and producer | Consumers and decision | Assurance and status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| pn-host/result-position/v1; PN-result-position-v1 | pn-host; admitted public carrier projection | commit, parent lookup, tip-relative read and public rebuild; bounded position equality followed by complete content comparison | semantic computational equality; public fields only; complete content comparison and replay remain required; implemented for roots and ordinary children; public transition continuity and store consumers remain held |

## Reductions · `sec:identity:reductions`

**Reduction (Scheme to properties)** · `red:identity:scheme-to-properties` Every construction question reduces to the property table. A scheme is adequate exactly when it delivers the properties of (`tab:identity:properties`) that its class requires; which adequate scheme a corpus chose is a fact of its recipe record; and the discipline prescribes none. Schemes therefore migrate freely under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`) while every benefit the properties buy stands still.

**Reduction (Mesh to chain)** · `red:identity:mesh-to-chain` All-to-all identity binding reduces to immediate typed edges. Binding every object to every transitive dependency duplicates what the chain already carries and erects a quadratic consistency mesh; binding each object to its immediate dependencies alone (`rule:identity:immediate-edges`) preserves transitive assurance by composition — the composition grounded by (`rule:identity:well-founded-graph`) — and gives each edge one owner. Human-readable manifests may display a whole chain; authoritative validation follows the edges.

**Reduction (Field hashes to object validation)** · `red:identity:fields-to-object` Hashing a field reduces to validating its object. Fields have no independent lifecycle, cross-field validity is a property of the whole, and a field digest evidences nothing the owning validator does not already establish (`tab:identity:mechanisms`).

## Rules of the procedure · `sec:identity:rules`

**Rule (Validation before admission)** · `rule:identity:admission-order` No semantic, evidence, or release identity — a deployment profile's identity falling under whichever of these its class record names — is admitted or published before the owning validator has run. Bytes may be hashed earlier — for transport, lookup, streaming, or content addressing — but such a value has no assurance standing until the applicable validation succeeds (`tab:identity:mechanisms`): a self-consistent invalid object rehashes perfectly, and a digest downstream of no validation binds garbage exactly.

**Rule (No incidental content)** · `rule:identity:no-incidentals` No graph-library index, source order, path, line number, solver variable number, matrix position, traversal order, thread schedule, temporary path, or floating-point working value enters a semantic identity. This enforces the free-of-incidentals property of (`tab:identity:properties`) at the source: processing accidents must not masquerade as meaning.

**Rule (Recipe permanence)** · `rule:identity:recipe-permanence` A published recipe (`def:identity:recipe`) is never silently redefined. Changing its projection — the fields it includes and the rules by which it excludes — its canonicalization, its primitive, or its domain separator creates a new recipe identifier. A migration records old and new recipes, the reason, whether meaning changed or only measurement, old and new identities where applicable, and the consumer transition policy. A recipe migration does not itself decide semantic versioning; the owning versioning rule does.

**Rule (Producer and consumer duties)** · `rule:identity:duties` The owning producer validates the complete typed object (`rule:identity:admission-order`), derives its canonical projection, computes its identity, and publishes object and recipe identifier together wherever external consumption exists. An immediate consumer parses external bytes into a typed value where necessary, rejects unknown fields and unsupported schemas, runs the owner's validator, recomputes the identity, compares the required immediate dependency identity (`rule:identity:immediate-edges`), and only then consumes the typed value.

**Rule (Immediate edges)** · `rule:identity:immediate-edges` An independently consumed parent binds only its immediate identity dependencies, and a child receives an identity of its own only when it has an independent lifecycle; otherwise the parent includes the canonical typed child value directly.

**Rule (Well-founded graph)** · `rule:identity:well-founded-graph` The authoritative identity-dependency graph is finite and acyclic: every release-reachable identity terminates, along the immediate edges of (`rule:identity:immediate-edges`), in directly validated typed values or exact artifact bytes. As adoption data, no first-party identity chain, evidence envelope, release manifest or release validator is built here; each corresponding implementation obligation activates with its first admitted consumer. A cycle at the model level is broken in the identity projection before any identity on it is admitted.

**Rule (Delegated release validation)** · `rule:identity:delegation` The release validator traverses the typed identity graph (`rule:identity:well-founded-graph`), delegates to package-owned validators (`rule:identity:duties`), verifies immediate edges (`rule:identity:immediate-edges`), required evidence roles, and artifact bytes, and reimplements nothing.

**Rule (Provenance containment)** · `rule:identity:provenance-containment` Publication provenance identifiers never enter semantic, interface, or protocol identity. Before production release, deployment calibration binds the exact final bundle and interface, and evidence fields bind typed report roles and subjects (`case:identity:evidence`) rather than bare digest arrays.

## Myths · `sec:identity:myths`

**Myth (Hashes validate)** · `myth:identity:hashes-validate` Corrected: admission awaits validation, always (`rule:identity:admission-order`). A hash of an invalid object is a fast way to remember the mistake.

**Myth (A digest authenticates)** · `myth:identity:digest-authenticates` Corrected: an unkeyed digest evidences equality under one recipe, never authenticity. Authenticity requires an independently trusted expected identity, or a signature over the accepted release root (`case:identity:release`) — nothing smaller, except an object holding an independently defined authority boundary of its own.

**Myth (Inequality is independence)** · `myth:identity:inequality-independence` Corrected: different bytes do not prove independent implementation or judgment. Independence is a reviewed provenance claim recording implementation identity, shared code and dependencies, operator, and execution environment where relevant. Equal report hashes are not rejected to manufacture an appearance of independence; distinct report roles are distinguished by typed envelopes and domain separation (`case:identity:evidence`).

**Myth (Important values deserve hashes)** · `myth:identity:importance` Corrected: importance is not a consumer (`crit:identity:benefit`). A value's weight argues for validation and review — the mechanisms that judge content — not for another digest.

**Myth (Reviewed objects still need digests)** · `myth:identity:reviewed-anyhow` Corrected: review judges content, a digest only equality. Under a standing review, at the review's own decision point, the digest adds a maintenance surface, not assurance, and fails (`crit:identity:benefit`) on its own terms. Binding the reviewed object across a later boundary of (`model:identity:objects`) is a different proposal, walked separately.

**Myth (The scheme is the security)** · `myth:identity:scheme-worship` Corrected: benefits flow from the properties a recipe warrants, not from any particular concatenation (`red:identity:scheme-to-properties`). A familiar-looking scheme without the properties is false comfort; an unfamiliar one with them is sound.

**Myth (One hash can rule them all)** · `myth:identity:one-hash` Corrected: semantic meaning, artifact bytes, provenance, evidence, and release aggregation have different stale conditions and different consumers (`sec:identity:cases`); one undifferentiated hash serves none of them.

**Myth (More digests, more assurance)** · `myth:identity:more-is-safer` Corrected: each digest is a standing obligation — a recipe, stale conditions, a migration path, a consumer (`req:identity:admission-record`). Proliferation multiplies obligations while assurance stays where it always was: with types, validators, and evidence.

**Warning (Non-claims)** · `warn:identity:non-claims` A matching identity evidences equality under one recipe (`def:identity:recipe`), computationally and nothing else: no logical identity, no authenticity, no correctness, no independence, no deployment readiness. Every admission record states its own non-claims.

## Moral · `sec:identity:moral`

**Moral (Types validate; hashes bind)** · `moral:identity:types-validate-hashes-bind` One aggregate identity per independently meaningful object; one byte digest per independently distributed artifact; one root for future authentication; typed evidence references instead of ambiguous raw hashes; immediate rather than all-to-all binding (`rule:identity:immediate-edges`); no field-level proliferation. Types and validators remain the correctness mechanism; hashes remain the comparison and binding mechanism; and no digest without a decision it changes (`crit:identity:benefit`) — the documented stop (`case:identity:no-identity`) being as sound an outcome as any admission.

## Implementation gate · `sec:identity:gate`

**Gate (Implementation)** · `gate:identity:implementation` Implementation is blocked until all of the following hold:

- every existing digest is classified by object, owner, producer, consumer, decision, assurance class, stale condition, migration, and non-claims;
- no new digest enters without passing (`crit:identity:benefit`) and recording its class under (`req:identity:admission-record`);
- every no-identity outcome is recorded with its deciding branch and revisit condition (`req:identity:stop-record`);
- every admitted recipe demonstrably delivers the properties its assurance class requires (`tab:identity:class-properties`), and no scheme is prescribed outside a recipe record;
- every admission record explicitly declares its hidden fields or none; every nonce- or salt-bearing recipe carries that declaration, its computational-hiding assumption, permitted disclosure, custody and generation obligations, scoped witnesses and any held evidence under (`amend:identity:hiding-property`);
- semantic, artifact, provenance, evidence, and release identities use distinct typed roles;
- committed generated publications keep exact freshness comparisons and acquire no redundant digests (`case:identity:artifact`);
- identity graphs bind immediate dependencies only (`rule:identity:immediate-edges`), and the graph is acyclic, every release-reachable identity terminating in validated values or exact bytes (`rule:identity:well-founded-graph`);
- local handles and other incidental content remain absent from semantic identity (`rule:identity:no-incidentals`);
- evidence reports bind typed roles and exact subjects (`case:identity:evidence`);
- deployment calibration binds the final bundle and interface before production (`rule:identity:provenance-containment`);
- producers and consumers discharge their duties (`rule:identity:duties`), and release validation delegates to package-owned validators (`rule:identity:delegation`);
- any future signature authenticates the release-manifest identity, or the identity of an object holding a declared independent authority boundary (`case:identity:release`);
- the gate is dischargeable from this document and the corpus's adoption data alone; no check consults another document;
- the corpus's full checks pass and leave no uncommitted generated changes.

**Rule (Hash-citation audit)** · `rule:identity:hash-citation-audit` As adoption data, every hexadecimal value in the tracked tree is accounted for in ci/hash-citation-families.toml, which names its group, what it measures, its writing program and how a reader establishes it as recomputable, resolvable or defined. The hash-citations step of ci/gate.sh asks Git for the tracked set on every run and uses the representation boundary documented in the families table. It refuses a value no rule describes, a rule whose claimed establishment its reader cannot establish, and a stale rule describing no tracked value. The audit places values rather than recomputing their digests: the correspondence gate owns the projection computations, Cargo's package locators identify its archive checksums, and Git provenance requires a fetchable source repository. Authored literals have a specification or visible pattern as their definition; an unestablished identifier is not admitted by calling it an authored constant. In valid JSON files, a number token is a quantity rather than a hash candidate: its optional sign, integer part, optional fraction and optional signed exponent are recognized outside strings before candidate extraction. JSON string contents retain the ordinary audit, including full hexadecimal identities and runs containing the letter e; they still need an established family. Invalid JSON gains no numeric exclusion, and Markdown and other formats keep their existing treatment. For a public-git family, a hexadecimal abbreviation of at least nine digits resolves only when it prefixes exactly one full public commit that the same family already resolves in the same file. The full identity anchors the abbreviation in public provenance; another file, an ambiguous prefix, or fewer than nine digits provides no such warrant. The hash-accounting witness refuses those three missing warrants and accepts the anchored compiler abbreviation. The hash-accounting witness exercises exponent numbers, unresolved string identities, exponent-looking strings, escaped string contents and the unchanged Markdown boundary. Family path matching is indexed once per file, candidate spans once per line, and line identity context once per line rather than once per numeric sample. Every candidate still reaches every applicable reader; indexing grants no new family or exemption. The indexed-hash-scan witness preserves line-local spans and refuses an unresolved string beside numeric samples. Raw committed bytes remain audited on every invocation.

**Amendment (Release support)** · `amend:identity:pre-one` As adoption data, the consumer transition and migration fields of (`req:identity:admission-record`) follow (`rule:compatibility:pre-one`). Before 1.0 their disposition is replacement and rebuild from public inputs, not migration of stored data or retention of obsolete consumers. Recipe permanence still forbids silently redefining an identifier: a replacement records its new recipe and the reason under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`). Recording that provenance is not a cross-release compatibility path.

**Amendment (Marker-policy identity admission)** · `amend:identity:marker-policy-admission` As adoption data under (`req:identity:admission-record`), the object is the complete validated marker PolicyInput, owned by pn-records; the producer is marker_policy_declaration, which validates the complete policy and returns DeclarationContent::MarkerPolicy with recipe and digest together, and the present consumer is bind_marker_policy. Storage and replay must recompute this same recipe when they first consume the declaration family. The decision is to refuse a policy whose content differs from the separately published declaration commitment, even at the same edition and publication boundary. This passes (`crit:identity:benefit`): the publication carries a commitment rather than the policy itself, no parent identity binds it, and no direct typed comparison already establishes equality across that boundary. The assurance class is semantic, under SHA-256 collision and second-preimage resistance. Recipe identifier `pn-records/marker-policy/v1` travels beside the digest in DeclarationContent::MarkerPolicy; an unsupported identifier refuses. Its input begins with that UTF-8 identifier and one zero byte, followed in order by identity, edition, sequence, initial_marker, placement_procedure, cadence and verification_profile as exact UTF-8 strings prefixed by u64 big-endian byte lengths; published_at, genesis_at and stability_buffer as u64 big-endian integers; then open_placement as false=0 or true=1, encoding as CompleteList=0, PublicCommitment=1 or WholePrefixAcceptAll=2, and candidate_rule as FirstValidInHistoryOrder=0 or UniquePerIndex=1. Fixed widths, length prefixes and explicit tags make framing unambiguous and independently recomputable; these are structural values with no path, traversal order or other incidental content. Exhaustive field destructuring makes a new policy field a compile error requiring a recipe decision. Any field change makes the commitment stale; any projection, framing, primitive or domain change requires a new recipe identifier under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`). Before 1.0, replacement rebuilds declarations from public inputs under (`amend:identity:pre-one`); old recipes are never silently reinterpreted. This is internal commitment framing, not a protocol document encoding. Matching identities establish computational equality only, not authenticity, policy validity, finality, independent evidence or deployment readiness; validation still precedes admission under (`rule:identity:admission-order`). The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the marker and burn replacement within (`gate:design:bite-2`) remains owed by its records, host and development-profile owners.

**Amendment (Cryptographic library selection)** · `amend:identity:crypto-library-choice` As adoption data, (`rec:crypto:decision`) selects an implementation library and divides primitive responsibilities. It introduces no digest field, canonical projection, signed-object presentation, authority boundary or recipe identifier, so there is no new identity to admit under (`gate:identity:implementation`) at that choice alone. Selecting a signature algorithm is not admission of its consumer's recipe. Before a later slice constructs or consumes a new digest or signed object, it must state the benefit, object, producer, consumers, assurance, recipe, stale conditions, migration and non-claims under (`crit:identity:benefit`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), and discharge (`rule:identity:duties`) and (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`). A library choice cannot waive that gate.

**Amendment (Policy publication producer)** · `amend:identity:policy-publication-producer` (`amend:identity:marker-policy-admission`) uses marker_policy_declaration as its validating publication producer. MARKER_POLICY_RECIPE is the one code constant for the domain separation and declaration recipe; marker_policy_digest is only a raw recomputation helper, not publication admission. An unsupported declared recipe refuses with UnsupportedPolicyRecipe, separately from PolicyDigest for mismatched content. The producer returns no declaration for an invalid policy, and recipe and digest travel together under (`rule:identity:duties`) and (`rule:identity:admission-order`).

**Amendment (Permanent recipes, implementing line)** · `amend:identity:sha-two-line` Under (`gate:identity:implementation`), (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`) and (`rule:identity:hash-citation-audit`), first-party SHA-256 moves from sha2 0.10.9 to the already admitted exact 0.11.1 line with default features disabled and zeroize enabled. This changes no identity class, domain separator, framing, output byte order or artifact recipe. The marker-policy producer retains pn-records/marker-policy/v1; six fixed vectors cover all encoding and candidate-rule discriminants, using independently framed public inputs and byte arrays, before and after the move. The observation artifact reader independently recomputes SHA-256 over each reconstructed object, preserving the existing content-address contract. A registered fixed-payload vector retains the literal lowercase address captured from the old implementing line before adaptation and checks both the digest field and the objects/sha256 filename; its expected text is never formatted by the library under test. The hash-citation reader recomputes that vector from its declared JSON-string payload. These are implementation correspondence witnesses, not a new digest admission. The unused direct pn-host zeroize edge is removed; secret carriers and cryptographic primitives retain their required zeroization through their existing dependencies.


**Amendment (Storage identity admission)** · `amend:identity:storage-admission` As adoption data under (`gate:identity:implementation`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), pn-host owns two identities at its durable public-input boundary. Payload constructors produce pn-host/storage-payload/v1, consumed by append, load, rebuild and replay, to detect substitution or damage to the complete canonical stored package. Recognition construction also produces pn-host/recognition-disclosure/v1, consumed by stored-recognition lookup and replay, to distinguish complete validated disclosures that may share a recognized prefix. Neither identity promotes recognition to settlement. No parent identity already supplies either durable binding. Direct byte equality still decides same-edition idempotence and conflict; digests never replace that exact comparison. The assurance class is semantic computational equality under SHA-256 collision and second-preimage resistance.

**Amendment (Permanent storage recipes)** · `amend:identity:storage-recipes` For each recipe in (`amend:identity:storage-admission`), SHA-256 consumes the UTF-8 recipe identifier, one zero byte, and the exact pn-interchange canonical value bytes. The payload projection is [recipe, family, complete public carrier projection]. The validated-disclosure projection is [history identity, records sorted by identity, candidates sorted by disclosed position, complete bound policy, complete burn publication]. The fixed field ordering and enum spellings are the exhaustive v1 projections in pn-host storage_payload; arrays preserve source definitions and public openings, sets and maps have canonical structural ordering, and neither secrets nor clocks nor observation nor database layout enters a projection. All referring records, failed candidates and their proof bytes remain. The recognition package carries its disclosure recipe, digest, complete projected input and complete prefix/refusal output, explicitly unsettled. Any input change makes its digest stale; any projection, order, enum spelling, primitive or domain change requires a new identifier under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`). A new carrier field requires a projection review before use. Before 1.0 replacement rebuilds public history under (`amend:identity:pre-one`), never silently reinterpreting old bytes. Matching identities establish neither authenticity, correctness, independent evidence, finality nor readiness. Recipe vectors and source-citation audit discharge (`rule:identity:hash-citation-audit`); typed validation and external evidence duties remain separate.


**Amendment (Storage projection correspondence)** · `amend:identity:storage-projection-vectors` The permanent recipes of (`amend:identity:storage-recipes`) are pinned by complete canonical-byte and digest vectors for all six storage families and the validated recognition disclosure. Fixed validated fixtures are projected by Rust; an independent restricted canonical encoder and SHA-256 reader construct their expected arrays and bytes. The rule fixture is an opaque public value and claims no domain validity. Field reordering or enum respelling within those fixed projections must break correspondence, even when a round-trip through the implementation would succeed. The fixed roster includes ridge-image constants, public-commitment openings, whole-prefix and first-valid spellings, the operator bound, and a refused recognition with its cause and competitors. A component table pins all twenty-eight cause spellings without declaring a new family. These witnesses strengthen permanence without changing either recipe or its input.

**Amendment (Graph dependency notice identity)** · `amend:identity:graph-dependency-notice` Under (`rule:identity:hash-citation-audit`), the graph notice repeats the existing Cargo registry archive identity for petgraph 0.8.3 to associate the retained MIT permission text with its exact dependency. Cargo lock resolution supplies the checksum; the package name and version resolve the archive, and the cargo-archive reader checks the citation against Cargo.lock. The object is the unmodified registry archive, the owner is the graph package, and the consumers are dependency attribution and the citation audit. The checksum has artifact computational-equality assurance only, not authenticity, licence validity, implementation correctness or canonical graph identity. A package, version or archive change makes the notice stale and requires replacing its attribution with the corresponding lock resolution; no first-party digest recipe or binding graph serialization is introduced.

**Amendment (Declared computational hiding)** · `amend:identity:hiding-property` Every admission record declares the fields it hides or explicitly declares none. Every recipe with a nonce or salt input must make that declaration regardless of whether its consumer claims privacy. This adds a conditional property to (`tab:identity:properties`) and every class in (`tab:identity:class-properties`): computational hiding of the declared fields, conditioned on the public projection, lengths and expressly disclosed metadata. An admission names adversary knowledge, the primitive and entropy assumptions, entropy floor, freshness, custody, disclosure transition and non-claims. Collision resistance alone does not establish hiding. Evidence pins the construction, independently reviews the assumption, witnesses entropy-failure and reuse refusals when generation is implemented, checks secret custody and removal, and refuses unintended disclosure. Fixed vectors and statistical output tests prove neither entropy nor cryptographic hardness. (`gate:identity:implementation`) checks the declaration, assumptions, scoped witnesses and held obligations, not a computational-hardness theorem. The existing marker-policy, storage-payload, recognition-disclosure, graph archive-notice and public observation-artifact identities declare hidden fields: none; they commit public inputs or bytes and use no concealment nonce or salt. The Actor document also declares hidden fields: none under (`rule:identity:actor-document-hiding`); its public blinder is a prefix-search variable, not a concealment nonce or salt. A later concealing identity cannot inherit that declaration merely by reusing their primitive.


**Amendment (Shared act profile)** · `amend:identity:act-profile` The deterministic act profile is owned by pn-act-profile, depending on pn-records and pn-interchange, never on pn-host. Records retains distinct opaque P, C_Y and C_D carriers, each with its recipe identifier and exactly 32 bytes, outside removable carriage. The Actor computes P through the shared package; the host computes C_Y and C_D through it. The present pure consumers compare a supplied opening with a supplied recorded commitment. Equality authenticates neither the commitment nor its producer, approval, finalization or anchor. A substituted anchor and substituted opening can match. This slice uses fixed test nonces and salts only. The test namespace reservation is (`amend:design:test-publication-profile`); complete theory and external interchange envelopes remain held; production entropy, counter allocation and custody remain held; signing and anchor authentication remain under (`open:crypto:dependency-admission`). The act-and-epoch document concept (`spec:actepoch:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the document subjects stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted rule until its owner admits the successor document cut, and current fixtures witness only their existing scope.

**Amendment (Act framing and permanence)** · `amend:identity:act-framing` For the three semantic recipes below, F(r,t,x) is the UTF-8 recipe identifier r, one zero byte, then pn-interchange's canonical encoding of the two-element array [t,x]. No other encoding-version string participates. Exact arrays, constructor tags, byte-string lengths and preferred canonical heads make framing injective. The primitive is SHA-256 on the exact sha2 0.11.1 line with zeroize and disabled defaults. Binding assumes collision and second-preimage resistance, with approximately 128-bit generic classical collision work. The profile's addresses, constructor vocabulary, field projection, ordering, bounds, nonce and salt formats, canonical encoding, role tags, framing and primitive form one immutable edition. Changing any binding component assigns new identifiers to all three recipes; a different implementation producing identical bytes does not. Input changes make the corresponding residue stale. Before 1.0 replacement rebuilds from available inputs under (`amend:identity:pre-one`); missing private material cannot be migrated from a digest, and old identifiers are never reinterpreted. The act-and-epoch document concept (`spec:actepoch:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the document subjects stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted rule until its owner admits the successor document cut, and current fixtures witness only their existing scope.
