**Amendment (Proposal admission)** · `amend:identity:act-proposal-admission` Under (`req:identity:admission-record`), pn-act-profile/act-proposal/v1 is semantic over the exact typed tuple (B,N,Y,D), produced by the Actor through pn-act-profile. The present consumer proposal_matches_supplied_commitment recomputes and compares P with a supplied recorded proposal under the admitted historical local construction. It detects substitution across the earlier Actor-proposal boundary, which later host-salted residues cannot establish. Its two private intermediates are SHA-256 of F(r,PN-content-pre-v1,[I,N,Y]) and F(r,PN-dependency-pre-v1,[I,N,D]); P is SHA-256 of F(r,PN-act-proposal-v1,[B,content_pre_digest,dependency_pre_digest]). Neither intermediate is retained or published: the host recomputes both at proposal to check P. This is the preceding reference construction, implemented with one nonce and version-one tags. It is not evidence for the changed construction of network-v0.37.4, ``def:graph:verified-act`` and ``def:graph:author-commitment-preparation``; that conformance remains held under (`open:identity:act-realization-admission`). The verified header publishes B, P, its Actor signature and both host-salted commitments. For this implemented version-one construction, P has no host salt and neither pre-digest is retained after reduction. Any holder of that legacy record can jointly search its single nonce, payload and dependency list against P; its concealment therefore depends on that nonce's entropy. The separately admitted local design uses independent N_D and N_Y, so disclosing one does not disclose the other; its host-dependent construction is not the current paper’s author-only preparation. In the implemented version-one construction, a field-by-field search requires a private pre-digest, held only by the host at proposal, and adds nothing to that host's existing access. B keeps exact authored integer pairs by records' structural equality: 1/2 and 2/4 remain different authored inputs. Hidden fields are Y and D, conditional on the Actor's published nonce specification and the computational-hiding assumption of (`amend:identity:act-concealment`). The version-one host sees N at proposal to recompute both intermediates, then custodies and removes it with the payload. Matching P establishes no Actor authenticity, genuine anchor, entropy, valid signature or information-theoretic hiding. The retirement field is a new design admission, pn-act-profile/act-proposal/v2, under (`gate:identity:implementation`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`); v1's ten-field body and all existing bytes remain immutable. B in v2 is exactly the eleven-element canonical CBOR array [act_identifier, retirement_point, layout, legs, quantities, license, asserted_parents, protocol_references, settlement, content_request, dependency_request]. Position 0 keeps the existing ["act",author,sequence,family] value unchanged. Position 1 is a required byte string of exactly 32 bytes: the transaction identifier in display order, exactly the byte reverse of its 32-byte consensus transaction hash, following ``[WALLET-def:wallet:settlement-evidence]``. Version two of the qualification marker uses the issuance prevout transaction hash in consensus byte order in its output script, not this display-order locator; conversion is explicit and performed once at that boundary. The marker appends the output index in four little-endian bytes, without issuance or peg-in flag bits, under (`rule:devnet:author-token`). Original positions 1 through 9 shift to 2 through 10 without changing their value encodings; protocol_references at position 7 is still the empty array. No author spelling, act identifier, sequence, graph target or identity equality relation changes. This pairs author identity with its retirement statement without making the statement part of identity. Absent, extra, reordered, wrongly typed or wrong-length fields refuse as malformed body before authentication; a well-formed identifier naming no transaction of that author's path is not malformed and has only the no-effect interpretation of (`rule:identity:retirement-order`). Every act carries the field, irrespective of family or payload state. An author with no effective retirement names its qualified token's creation transaction. A fourth segment of ActId.author would change equality, ordering and all identity consumers; a family-specific reference or removable payload would omit ordinary acts or lose the instruction on removal. Those alternatives are not selected. At network-v0.37.4, premise{``def:graph:act-identifier``; act identity is author, local sequence and family}, premise{``def:graph:identifier-algebra``; grounded and minted identities use those fixed atoms} and premise{``def:graph:node-taxonomy``; grounded identity does not rebind}, the field is not part of any identity constructor. It is authentication data of the admission handshake, not a new structural or semantic act field, admitted under premise{``rem:graph:authentication-realization-out-of-scope``; authentication serialization and key management are deployment concerns}. The admitted local envelope places the retirement point within its authentication material; the current paper’s act envelope does not prescribe that field or this handshake construction. Removing position 1 from B version two yields the historical local structural-body projection; the unsigned author preparation of ``def:graph:author-commitment-preparation`` does not establish equivalence of these byte recipes. B version two is the deployment's encoding of the pair (structural body, retirement point), flattened in the eleven-element order above; its exact local meaning is unchanged by the current paper’s different preparation form. No host salt, epoch or logical time enters that structural body. This is the repository's realization, not a claim that the paper names a retirement field. Its retained bytes survive payload and dependency removal under premise{``itm:graph:act-authentication-requirements-projection-binding``; removal preserves immutable binding}. For the separately implemented new recipe, r = pn-act-profile/act-proposal/v2, use independent N_Y and N_D in the existing nonce form: hY = SHA-256(F(r,PN-content-pre-v2,[I,N_Y,Y])), hD = SHA-256(F(r,PN-dependency-pre-v2,[I,N_D,D])) and P = SHA-256(F(r,PN-act-proposal-v2,[B,hY,hD])). These are the repository’s retained local tags, retaining the historical local transcript independently of network-v0.37.4, ``def:graph:courtesy-handshake``. The host receives hY and recomputes hD from D,N_D; hD is not a proposal field. The optional content pair is (Y,N_Y), never Y alone. The new B field is retirement_point in the position and form above. Recipe identities and independent vectors remain subject to (`open:identity:act-realization-admission`). The object, producer and consumers remain the Actor proposal and host/replay recomputation described for v1. Benefit: a publicly retained retirement point bound by all ordinary act signatures. Assurance class: semantic binding of the authored tuple, not authority, cutoff membership, inclusion or finality. A failed shape, recipe or recomputation refuses the proposal; a valid but ineffective retirement point does not. There are no new hidden fields; the existing conditional nonce/concealment premises remain. Changed recipe, tag, retirement point, body or opening makes the result inapplicable. Reserve pn-act-authentication/author-token-bip340/v2 for this future operation domain, with V key 3 naming proposal/v2 and the material and frame edition matching it; the exact V version is [2,0,0] under the version-two definition of (`def:identity:verified-header-name`), V keys 5 and 6 name content/v2 and dependency/v2, and seal/approval framing algorithms are unchanged. The content and dependency edition texts in the designed V are 2 and 2. The repository reads an edition as identifying the projection's whole binding construction: commitment formula, opening format and the verification rule joining them, not only the projection state's shape, because this local construction moves the content and dependency domains together while the seal's domain stays unchanged; the reference transcript is courtesy material under network-v0.37.4, ``def:graph:courtesy-handshake``. The content opening now includes N_Y and is checked through hY; both commitment domains use their second strings. The seal's own construction and PN-verified-act-v1 domain stay unchanged. The implemented header's edition texts remain immutable version-one evidence until the coordinated code cut. All three semantic recipes receive fresh identifiers as required by (`amend:identity:act-framing`); retaining content/v1 or dependency/v1 would also violate their required proposal/v1 anchor. This edition changes neither token identity nor host binding. The v1 operations and their golden evidence remain the implemented scope until the replacement cut provides independent v2 vectors and typed refusals and moves all producer/consumer paths together. Nothing withdrawn has been deployed; there is no conversion of a signed v1 message into v2 and no migration of live records. (`amend:identity:act-concealment`) retains the local entropy and custody premise. realizes{``amend:identity:act-concealment``; The Actor nonce and proposal format uses the declared construction; the particular hiding premise is named separately.} realizes{``open:identity:act-realization-admission``; This retains the coordinated new-tag implementation hold, not evidence of private entropy or custody.} The separate pn-act-profile v2 surface implements this eleven-field projection and the three admitted tags, with finite independent vectors under (`verif:identity:authentication-encoding`). Host recomputation consumes secret-owned hY and D,N_D without requiring Y,N_Y; the separate optional-pair comparison returns no act-admission verdict. The coordinated retained-carriage and production holds remain. The existing FullEmpty, Full, Reduced and Tombstoned body spellings remain local edition values, including their emptiness assertions; they establish no requested publication intent at network-v0.37.4. The adopted courtesy requests are wishes, never assertions that an opening exists; separate emptiness representation, new body edition and receipts remain held under (`open:identity:carriage-result-recipe`). The exact hash equations above are this repository’s local construction; the release’s reference transcript belongs to ``def:graph:courtesy-handshake``, not to the closure definition of the proposal. In the local construction N_Y and N_D are author blinding values and the host’s salts are host blinding values; identifiers, literal recipes and historical bytes retain their existing spellings until the separate terminology cut. The separately selected third formation is admitted as design by realizes{``amend:identity:formation-three``; This uses the selected public format and implementation hold, not private entropy or custody.}, with distinct body, proposal, content and dependency records and independent vectors. The separately selected third formation's pure producers and comparison consumers implement the admitted body, proposal, content and dependency records with independent finite evidence; production enablement remains held. Its opaque signed extension is authenticated without interpreting requests. Empty content or dependencies are established by authenticated openings, never by an intent. Old bodies keep their old request meanings and acquire no implicit five-verb intent. The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the successor admission cut remains owed by this statement's owner. The payload family (`conv:devnet:wire-boundary`) designs an unbuilt successor that holds the successor signed-message network binding instead of shortening an existing signed Actor spelling; this statement retains its landed or admitted meaning until replacement, and the identity and development-profile owners owe the coordinated payload and authority admission cut.

**Amendment (Content admission)** · `amend:identity:act-content-admission` Under (`req:identity:admission-record`), pn-act-profile/act-content/v1 is semantic over the exact typed tuple (I,P,S_Y,Y), with Y an opaque byte string. The host produces it through pn-act-profile. The present consumer content_matches_supplied_commitment decides whether supplied removable content matches this supplied recorded residue; no direct comparison with removed bytes or other parent identity supplies that decision. C_Y is SHA-256 of F(r,PN-content-v1,[I,P,S_Y,Y]), requiring the exact proposal/v1 anchor recipe and its 32 raw bytes. This implemented version-one content recipe is evidence of the preceding raw-content construction only. Its salt-based hiding claim is conditional on (`amend:identity:act-concealment`); it does not implement the content pre-digest construction of network-v0.37.4, ``subsec:deployment:concealment-entropy``. This is a deployment obligation, not evidence that this fixed-input slice generates entropy or implements custody. Matching C_Y establishes computational equality only, not authenticity, formation, anchor genuineness, deployment readiness or information-theoretic hiding. For the designed authentication v2 edition, admit pn-act-profile/act-content/v2 with C_Y = SHA-256(F(r,PN-content-v2,[I,P,S_Y,hY])), r that new recipe, and P the exact proposal/v2 residue. This is a semantic recipe under (`req:identity:admission-record`) whose benefit is the same supplied-opening comparison with the new admitted anchor. The object, host producer and comparison consumers remain; the version-two content opening recomputes hY from Y,N_Y, before comparing C_Y; a wrong anchor recipe, malformed opening or unequal digest refuses the supplied evidence. Changes to I, P, salt, content or recipe make the result stale. Independent v2 vectors and a coordinated producer/consumer replacement precede enablement; v1 bytes and their applicability never change. (`amend:identity:act-concealment`) retains the local entropy and custody premise. realizes{``amend:identity:act-concealment``; The legacy format and held new content recipe use the declared construction; actual entropy is not established.} The separate v2 surface implements C_Y over secret-owned hY and compares a supplied Y,N_Y opening under (`verif:identity:authentication-encoding`); absence of carriage is not act refusal. Freshness, entropy and custody remain unestablished by these finite fixtures. The separately selected third recipe is admitted as design by (`amend:identity:content-three-admission`); the pure supplied-input producer and comparison consumer are implemented; entropy, custody and production enablement remain held. No existing recipe accepts its new anchor by reinterpretation. **Amendment (Dependency admission)** · `amend:identity:act-dependency-admission` Under (`req:identity:admission-record`), pn-act-profile/act-dependency/v1, the implemented preceding-reference recipe, is semantic over the exact typed tuple (I,P,S_D,D), produced by the host through pn-act-profile. The present consumer dependencies_match_supplied_commitment decides whether the supplied complete opening matches this supplied retained residue after dependency tombstoning; no empty replacement or unavailable earlier list supplies that decision. C_D is SHA-256 of F(r,PN-dependency-v1,[I,P,S_D,D]), requiring proposal/v1's exact recipe and raw residue. D uses records' strict ActId order, never CBOR string-length order. Opening deliberately releases the whole list and salt; it restores no carriage and changes no finalized order, recognition or title. Representation and binding refusals reject supplied evidence, while absent, self or later opened dependencies remain host-fault evidence. Matching establishes neither authenticity of P or C_D nor genuine finalized anchors, signatures, entropy or information-theoretic hiding. For the designed authentication v2 edition, admit pn-act-profile/act-dependency/v2 with C_D = SHA-256(F(r,PN-dependency-v2,[I,P,S_D,D])), r that new recipe, and P the exact proposal/v2 residue. This is a semantic recipe under (`req:identity:admission-record`) whose benefit is the same complete-opening comparison with the new admitted anchor. The object, host producer and comparison consumers remain; the dependency opening remains encode(D); a wrong anchor recipe, malformed opening or unequal digest refuses the supplied evidence. Changes to I, P, salt, dependency list or recipe make the result stale. Independent v2 vectors and a coordinated producer/consumer replacement precede enablement; v1 bytes and their applicability never change. The separate v2 surface implements the admitted tag and exact three-part opening against supplied P,C_D under (`verif:identity:authentication-encoding`). It authenticates neither anchor nor finalized placement; retained records/graph carriage remains a later cut. **Amendment (Act concealment specification)** · `amend:identity:act-concealment` Under this repository’s admitted local construction, independent fresh N_Y and N_D separate content concealment from dependency verification. Each nonce has the existing form: u64 big-endian author sequence followed by an independent unpredictable 256-bit random suffix. The prefix equals I.sequence; independent suffixes distinguish attempts at one public slot. The Actor keeps the content nonce beside Y; D and N_D are disclosed to the host and submission path. The host receives N_Y only in the optional content pair, not as dependency-verification input. It custodies and co-removes a received pair; absent the pair it holds no N_Y. P has no host salt; a holder of P, B, D and N_D can jointly search Y,N_Y, so a weak content nonce exposes that Actor's content even when the host salts remain strong. A supplied hY is not a proof that an opening exists. The hiding assumption is domain-separated SHA-256 with sufficient private entropy, conditioned on public B, I, recipes, requested states, lengths and expressly disclosed metadata; collision resistance and fixed vectors do not prove hiding. The client-generated rule and carrier checks remain this repository’s local duties; actual entropy and custody remain premises, not conclusions of the pure checks. Each host salt retains u128 big-endian allocation counter plus 32 independent random bytes. One durable per-run counter reserves before issuance across both roles, permits gaps, refuses exhaustion and uncertain rollback, and retains no old salt journal. The host generates salts in both historical carriage modes under this local admission: its audited generator owns the salt-quality obligation and content-nonce generation is the client’s separate obligation. The current paper imposes neither host entropy nor an entropy-audit duty. The named erasure floor of ``rem:graph:erasure-floor`` retains an inert binding residue, cannot erase observers' copies, does not prove payload authorship, and leaves private entropy quality with its assigned owner. Production witnesses must cover entropy failure, reuse, rollback, custody and removal; the pure shared package checks only supplied forms and equality. The implemented one-nonce, raw-content version-one recipes retain their immutable bytes and finite witnesses of the preceding reference construction; they do not discharge the separately held local production obligation under (`open:identity:act-realization-admission`). Authorized disclosure ends the corresponding hiding claim and cannot erase recipients' copies. realizes{``open:identity:act-realization-admission``; This retains the coordinated new-tag implementation hold, not evidence of private entropy or custody.} For the designed separated path, the formation edition fixes the verifiable author_content_blinding_value, author_dependency_blinding_value, host_content_blinding_value and host_dependency_blinding_value forms. Actual entropy, durable allocation freshness and private handling remain producer premises; optional custody is a courtesy undertaking, not an act-eligibility premise. The historical author_proposal_blinding_value remains one value. The Rust identifiers for these values in pn-act-profile and pn-act-authentication now name blinding values by owner and projection; serialized keys, PN domains, golden fixtures and their legacy nonce/salt names stay byte-identical. Closure projection binding rests on collision and second-preimage resistance of the admitted hash, not possession, custody or an assertion of sufficient private entropy. The admitted third formation retains these blinding-value formats. Production entropy, allocation, freshness and custody remain held; admitting its pure recipes does not discharge those duties. At network-v0.37.4 the courtesy statements ``subsec:deployment:concealment-entropy``, ``case:deployment:concealment-entropy-operator-generated``, ``case:deployment:concealment-entropy-client-generated`` and ``case:deployment:concealment-entropy-unassigned-custody`` carry no obligation in the claim register; no local check or production hold is cancelled by that classification. Author-only commitments and one complete-act approval are the separate successor form, which remains unbuilt under (`open:identity:act-realization-admission`). The act-and-epoch document concept (`spec:actepoch:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the document subjects stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted rule until its owner admits the successor document cut, and current fixtures witness only their existing scope.

**Amendment (Act identity stops)** · `amend:identity:act-stops` Under (`req:identity:stop-record`), an identifier digest stops at direct typed equality: act(author, sequence, family) already decides act identity without a hash assumption. Revisit only for a separately justified distribution consumer. A separate opening digest stops because recomputing C_D already decides the supplied-opening comparison; no independent object lifecycle or decision has been identified. The supported authentication profile spends the slot (author, sequence), where author includes the selected run and unchanged token identity, independently of family. Two families at that slot have different ActId values but compete for one admitted act; family is neither removed from ActId nor a second allocation space. This realizes network-v0.37.4, premise{``def:graph:act-identifier``; the Actor chooses a unique author-local sequence before submission}. Freshness means unused, not strictly next: any representable unused sequence is admissible subject to ordinary checks. The client generator selects the least unused unsigned 64-bit value, starting at zero, from complete authenticated ordered history for that author, within scan and arithmetic bounds whose parameter and admission remain held inputs of the public slot-generation cut; exhaustion or incomplete evidence refuses generation. A separate one-time proposal value would add a signed field and a second uniqueness register without removing the author/sequence link already public in ActId, so it is not selected. No identifier, proposal byte or signature frame changes for slot selection. A slot is spent only when its act becomes part of the committed authoritative epoch order, not by proposal, host seal, approval or temporary candidate selection. The final all-writer ordering decision checks prior history and the complete candidate epoch atomically with committing that order. It admits at most one act per slot and returns SpentActSlot carrying the public winning ActId and its (epoch, position) for every later attempt, identical or different. A proposal-time check may return that same refusal for an already ordered slot, but makes no reservation; concurrent pending attempts may both be sealed and approved. A reply alone is not public evidence: the client resolves the named winner in the authenticated order. Candidate conflicts cause re-selection or PendingSlotConflict before commitment, never two published winners or a partial extra debit. PendingSlotConflict does not claim a spent slot or authorize advancing a retry to another slot; only the committed winner supports SpentActSlot. The ordinary immutable ordered record supplies the spent set; a rebuilt index is only a projection of it, not a journal of intentions. Two authenticated ordered entries sharing a slot are a public duplicate-slot conformance fault without a private opening, even across families or epochs. This is distinct from network-v0.37.4, premise{``def:graph:ordering-fraud-proof``; the named proof opens a dependency projection and proves a placement violation}, whose success condition is not duplication. The accountability principle of premise{``itm:graph:act-authentication-requirements-ordering``; dependency violations are publicly provable without rewriting history} does not widen that proof definition. Finalized fault evidence preserves the record; no replay deletes acts, refunds debits or reverses title. Authenticity of the order remains (`open:design:dependency-opening-recipe`). SpentActSlot is the ordering refusal carrying the winning ActId and placement for (author, sequence). It is distinct from pn-records’ existing DuplicateAct(ActId), which detects formation duplication over the full author/sequence/family triple. Neither a proposal nor a seal reserves a slot, and a different pending body alone is not a refusal. The act-and-epoch document concept (`spec:actepoch:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the document subjects stated here; this carrier keeps all its required material and admitted meaning until its owners admit the replacement in the successor document admission cut.

**Decision (Authentication key binding)** · `dec:identity:authentication-keys` The author-token binding in (`amend:identity:author-token-binding`) supersedes the direct verification-key target of this decision. An Actor is the permanent run-qualified asset identity, and signing keys are replaceable delegates selected by its public token history and prospective network retirements. No accepted identifier, burn or history is reassigned. Key loss or disclosure is a tolerated delegate fault when the token remains controllable; loss of the token control key has no recovery promise. The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the successor admission cut remains owed by this statement's owner.

**Decision (Host authority binding)** · `dec:identity:authentication-host` A host identity is pn-host-bip340-v1/ followed by the lowercase hexadecimal x-only host key. The selected run's public deployment configuration names exactly one expected host identity; callers must supply that selection independently of a candidate handshake. The disclosed host key must equal that identity, and the run in the authored identifier must equal the selected run. A seal cannot nominate its own trusted host. The public record retains that run configuration with the authentication evidence so an offline reader can repeat the comparison; choosing which run configuration to trust remains the deployment premise, not a consequence of a signature by its own key. This chooses a fixed host per run over an online certificate hierarchy or mutable host registry. Host-key rotation requires a later explicit deployment transition; this edition supplies neither rotation nor authentication of finalized anchors, and a valid seal promises no inclusion. Host and Actor keys have distinct roles even if their public bytes happen to coincide; the three signed messages remain role-separated. A designed courtesy receipt uses the independently selected receiving host identity with a separately admitted request/receipt role; it is neither a host seal nor an approval witness. Another carrier requires independently selected party identity and admitted key-selection policy, never a key chosen by the candidate receipt. The payload family (`conv:devnet:wire-boundary`) designs an unbuilt successor that uses (`rule:identity:actor-document-context`): the namespace separates test from production and, within a namespace, the token’s chain supplies context under (`assum:identity:actor-chain-separation`), with no signed network or run context and operation admission retained under (`open:identity:chain-actor-binding`); this statement retains its landed or admitted meaning until replacement, and the identity and development-profile owners owe the coordinated payload and authority admission cut. The act-and-epoch document concept (`spec:actepoch:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the document subjects stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted rule until its owner admits the successor document cut, and current fixtures witness only their existing scope.

**Definition (Verified header name)** · `def:identity:verified-header-name` Let B be exactly the canonical array projection of pn-act-profile/act-proposal/v1 under (`amend:identity:act-proposal-admission`), including its existing bounds, family vocabulary and empty protocol-reference list. This describes the implemented v1 header; the designed retirement extension and its fresh recipe and edition are specified by (`amend:identity:act-proposal-admission`) and are not accepted by this decoder yet. V is pn-interchange's canonical unsigned-key CBOR document: key 0 is the text org.peer-network.test.verified-act, key 1 its version, key 2 B, key 3 [proposal_recipe,P], key 4 actor_proposal_signature, key 5 [content_recipe,C_Y], key 6 [dependency_recipe,C_D], key 7 content_edition and key 8 dependency_edition. All keys are required. The repository maintainers reserve this test namespace at major 1, minor 0 for this header, separately from the complete act-envelope namespace. For this authentication edition both producer and verifier require exactly [1,0,0]; any other version is an Edition refusal. General interchange acceptance excludes patch from semantic identity, but this authentication operation restricts the representation to one version spelling so one header value has one signed encoding. Structural interchange acceptance of another version does not admit its authentication operation. Recipe identifiers and editions are CBOR text; residues are byte strings of exactly 32 bytes; the signature is a byte string of exactly 64 bytes. The three recipe strings are exactly pn-act-profile/act-proposal/v1, pn-act-profile/act-content/v1 and pn-act-profile/act-dependency/v1. This authentication edition admits the content and dependency edition texts 1 and 1 only. B preserves authored integer pairs, canonical structural list order and both legs, never a reduced rational or a host-local handle. The complete header theory and independent vectors must be published before the first external producer is enabled under (`schema:interchange:global`) and (`sig:interchange:theory-assignment`); the enclosing complete act document remains separately held under (`open:identity:act-envelope-allocation`). No unknown content key is in the initial closed theory. This reservation records the design, not a registry implementation or completed allocation evidence. This uses one encoder and refusing decoder under (`req:xchg:determinism`) and (`conv:xchg:refuse-never-repair`), rather than JSON, a second CBOR codec or signing a repaired representation. No epoch, position, time, salt, nonce, payload, dependency opening or approval is inside V. This schema assigns no extra header digest: the admitted seal and approval messages bind the complete V bytes. Under (`req:identity:stop-record`), adjudication on 2026-11-20 stops at the parent-provides branch for a separate V digest; retake (`alg:identity:adjudication`) before a persisted, transferred, cached or authenticated header has an independent lifecycle or consumer decision outside the admitted seal and approval bindings that exact byte comparison does not supply. It has no separate digest assurance class under (`case:identity:no-identity`): canonical representation is checked directly and the signed parent roles supply their own provenance assurance. Hidden fields: none beyond the conditional hidden preimages of its residues. Input and output are at most 2,097,152 bytes; the B3 ceiling plus fewer than 512 fixed header bytes fits inside that bound. Refusal order is byte ceiling (Bound), canonical encoding and complete consumption (Canonical), exact closed map/key shape (Shape), namespace and version (Edition), B3 validation excluding its final expected-body equality check, then keys 3 through 8 whole in key order (pair shape Shape, recipe Recipe, residue or signature length Shape, content edition ContentEdition, dependency edition DependencyEdition); the comparison then checks the independently expected body, P, C_Y, C_D, Actor signature, content edition and dependency edition in that order with causes Body, Proposal, Content, Dependency, ActorSignature, ContentEdition and DependencyEdition. Finally, after all those comparisons, an operation given canonical supplied header bytes compares them with the canonical re-encoding of the complete header assembled from the independently expected values (HeaderBytes); this check applies to those supplied bytes, not to a typed-only construction, and follows DependencyEdition. The earlier Canonical check refuses malformed or noncanonical input before this comparison is reached. No equality comparison substitutes for signature verification. Any changed value, signed extension, coordinate, recipe or formation edition invalidates the old comparison and signatures. The deadline and v1/v2 refusal policy are realizes{``amend:identity:formation-three``; This uses the specified public format and held implementation boundary, without asserting private entropy or custody.}. The exact theory is this closed map plus the B3 schema and canonical rules; it assigns no surrounding act or epoch envelope. V contains no epoch, position, opening, host blinding value, receipt or approval. Its vectors are (`amend:identity:formation-three-vectors`); production authentication, oracle case binding and complete publication remain held. The admitted construction and declaration identifiers remain part of this historical representation. The current paper’s omission of a separate construction-version field does not omit an admitted member; successor membership belongs to its own representation admission. The act-and-epoch document concept (`spec:actepoch:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the document subjects stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted rule until its owner admits the successor document cut, and current fixtures witness only their existing scope.

**Amendment (Third formation signed roles)** · `amend:identity:formation-three-roles` Under (`req:identity:admission-record`), admit pn-act-authentication/actor-proposal/v3 as the distinct provenance message operation over the exact computed P of proposal/v3. pn-act-authentication owns it, the Actor signs it and host proposal verification and public replay consume it to refuse unauthenticated authorship. **Amendment (Closure act carrier admission)** · `amend:identity:closure-act-carrier` The complete schema (records-ClosureAct, 2) is the eight-element canonical array [2,V,material,host_seal,actor_approval,epoch,position,logical_time]. V and material are byte strings containing exactly the canonical major-three header and third authentication material of (`amend:identity:header-three-admission`) and (`amend:identity:material-three-admission`); they are not nested CBOR values or opaque unvalidated substitutes. The two signatures are exactly 64-byte strings. The last three fields are unsigned 64-bit integers, retaining the authoritative epoch-qualified position and attached logical time without certifying their derivation. The typed identity, structural body, legs, protocol references, P, C_Y, C_D, proposal signature and formation editions are the exact projections of V, with no second independently mutable copy. Material retains the authentication edition, run and disclosed keys. Thus every historical required structural and authentication value is retained or exactly projected; no D, content opening, projection status, tombstone or removal member exists. B3's asserted_parents remain structural record data and are not reinterpreted as D. The entire fragment is at most 2,097,152 bytes; V retains its own 2,097,152-byte input guard, its B3 ceiling and fixed overhead, and material its 512-byte ceiling, so every admissible complete value fits the fragment ceiling. Host assembly produces the candidate after formation; records validation and graph/epoch reconstruction consume its immutable structural projection, and host restoration compares it against independently supplied expected header, material, signatures and placement. The consumer can decide exact representation and binding without any courtesy state; it cannot infer signature validity, accepted inclusion or dependency-derived time from equality. Exact refusal order is whole-fragment byte ceiling (Bound), canonical encoding and complete consumption (Canonical), eight-element shape and member types, signature lengths and integer ranges in array order (Shape), local edition 2 (Edition), then the full nested header order with the expected-body comparison in its admitted position (Header with the nested cause), then the full material order under the independently selected run and host (Material with the nested cause), then equality of host_seal (Seal), actor_approval (Approval), epoch (Epoch), position (Position) and logical_time (Time) against the independently expected values in that order. The stage for a material differing only in its actor key remains open for the identity record owner; meanwhile the comparison refuses with Material(Host) after nested material and before Seal, without assigning a separate admitted cause. A typed-only construction checks the corresponding encoded sizes and structural conditions and has no supplied-byte Canonical or HeaderBytes comparison. A fragment decoder that has no independent expectation performs the representation checks only and returns an unchecked value, never an admitted act. Signature verification and finalized inclusion remain the separate authentication and boundary obligations. For the proposal to add a carrier digest, adjudication on 2026-11-20 stops at the equality-already-given branch under (`req:identity:stop-record`) and (`case:identity:no-identity`): direct comparison of the complete value already supplies the named consumer’s equality decision, and the schema has no independent digest assurance class. Retake (`alg:identity:adjudication`) before an independently persisted, transferred, cached or authenticated carrier requires a named equality decision not supplied by the complete value and its admitted bindings. Hidden fields of this carrier: none; the commitments retain their existing hidden-preimage premises. A changed V, material, signature, placement or expected context makes an old comparison inapplicable. This admission implements no validator, switches no producer and assigns no complete publication encoding. **Amendment (Dependency opening proof carrier admission)** · `amend:identity:dependency-proof-carrier` The complete schema (records-DependencyOpening, 2) is the eight-element canonical array [2,I,epoch,position,P,C_D,S_D,D]. I is the exact ActId value of (`amend:identity:dependency-three-admission`); epoch and position are unsigned 64-bit integers; P and C_D are exact 32-byte strings; S_D is the admitted 48-byte host dependency blinding value; D is the complete canonical array value of strictly ordered, duplicate-free ActIds, never a byte string wrapping that array or a selected subset. The encoded D has a 262,144-byte ceiling and at most 1,024 identifiers. The whole proof fragment has a 262,453-byte ceiling: 262,144 for D plus 309 for the other members and framing. I uses at most 171 bytes: one array head, four bytes for text act, 144 for the 142-byte author text and its head, nine for the unsigned sequence, and thirteen for the longest family text Registration. The author spelling has 13 prefix bytes, two 64-byte hexadecimal components and one separating slash; the family vocabulary is the unchanged third-formation vocabulary. The remaining overhead is one array head, one edition byte, two nine-byte placements, two 34-byte residues and one 50-byte blinding value: 1 + 1 + 171 + 9 + 9 + 34 + 34 + 50 = 309. Thus every admissible complete value fits the fragment ceiling. Under the current identifier vocabulary the maximum admissible D is actually 3 + 1,024 * 171 = 175,107 bytes; the independent 262,144-byte guard remains part of the order and can refuse structurally encoded candidates before their member-identity checks. These representation limits are independent of a caller's smaller work allowance. A supplier with the full opening produces it; the records/graph opening consumer checks it against an independently retained closure act and its authenticated epoch-qualified anchor, and only then supplies D to the existing dependency-time and placement reasoning. The benefit is one exact association and complete-list check across that supply boundary, using C_D already retained by the act rather than a new opening digest. Exact refusal order for a present fragment is whole-fragment byte ceiling (Bound), canonical encoding and complete consumption (Canonical), eight-element shape and scalar/array types and epoch/position ranges in array order (Shape), local edition 2 (Edition), I's third-formation representation (Identity), P then C_D lengths (Digest), S_D length (Blinding), D's encoded-byte and member-count ceilings in that order (DependenciesBound), each D member's ActId representation in array order (Identity), then strict order and uniqueness (Dependencies). Finish each member before the next. With these structural checks complete, compare I, epoch, position, P and C_D against the independently authenticated retained anchor in that order (AnchorIdentity, AnchorEpoch, AnchorPosition, AnchorProposal, AnchorCommitment), then recompute pn-act-profile/act-dependency/v3 over [I,P,S_D,D] under (`amend:identity:dependency-three-admission`) and compare its residue to C_D (OpeningMismatch). After those anchor comparisons and before hashing, the nested recipe's full framed-preimage ceiling applies (Bound); OpeningMismatch is reached only after it passes. Self, absent and non-earlier members of an authentic D are placement-fault evidence, not a reason to repair the list or reject its opening; the separate authoritative history determines those results. Authentication of the anchor means that the exact retained closure object, including its I, P, C_D and placement, has been selected from authenticated finalized order under the mandatory deployment authority case, not nominated by this proof. The verifier must obtain that premise independently; the declaration/account representation is admitted by (`amend:identity:local-authority-boundary`), while complete publication binding and finalized inclusion remain held for C7P and its implementation, and no flag, key, URI, digest or opaque authority blob inside this proof supplies it. This premise states no new boundary representation. After the structural checks, if that authenticated anchor is unavailable, return UnavailableAnchor before the association comparisons, not verified, failed or empty. Without a supplied proof the result is UnavailableOpening before any proof decoding, not a malformed empty fragment and not an act refusal. A malformed or mismatching supplied proof is an evidence refusal only, never a change to the retained act or its order. Only a successful comparison of the complete canonical empty D against the authenticated anchor proves emptiness. Successful proof matching establishes only that opening; downstream time or placement verification still needs its own complete authenticated history and otherwise remains unavailable. The new proof is applicable to any retained new closure act at the matching anchor, without a tombstone prerequisite. A historical replay_openings call retains its historical target restriction and recipes; neither path upgrades the other. Under (`req:identity:stop-record`), adjudication on 2026-11-20 stops at the existing-parent/direct-comparison branch for a separate proof digest: recomputing retained C_D and checking the full anchor already supply the decision, under (`case:identity:no-identity`), with no separate digest assurance class. Retake (`alg:identity:adjudication`) before an independent proof lifecycle or certificate consumer needs a named identity or authentication not supplied by this anchor and complete comparison. Hidden fields of this proof carrier: none. Supplying D and S_D discloses them to the verifier; this carrier claims no hiding from its recipient, no entropy, authorship, custody or signature evidence. Changing I, placement, P, C_D, S_D, D or the selected anchor makes the previous comparison inapplicable. The C6 migration deadline and absence semantics remain (`amend:identity:closure-components`); publication of a proof is never required for an act to retain its identity. The act-and-epoch document concept (`spec:actepoch:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the document subjects stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted rule until its owner admits the successor document cut, and current fixtures witness only their existing scope.

**Amendment (Closure component independent vectors)** · `amend:identity:closure-component-vectors` The public fixture packages/act-authentication/tests/carrier-golden.json and its independent encoder packages/act-authentication/tests/carrier_vectors.py give complete third-formation inputs, all preimages and residues, the closure fragment, the separate proof fragment and the expected anchor tuple. The latter two contain an overlong author and are structural controls, not admissible dependency lists: at the limit the byte guard passes and Identity refuses; one byte above, DependenciesBound refuses first while the whole fragment remains within its ceiling. Their sizes and SHA-256 checksums are recomputable from the descriptor inputs and encoder; these checksums identify fixture bytes only and assign no carrier digest. Patterned signatures, blinding values and trusted-anchor assumptions are explicit fixture inputs, never authenticated facts.
