**Amendment (Case closure admission)** · `amend:identity:case-closure-admission` Under (`req:identity:admission-record`) and (`rule:identity:hash-citation-audit`), the existing calibration case-closure recipe is named same-file-case-closure/v1. Its owner is the CI case-identity implementation in ci/devperf.py. The validated object is a unique named Python or Rust case, its exact body and attributes, its transitive same-file references, its own class fixtures and referenced same-file assignments, constants and statics. The existing producer sorts the reached definition keys and serializes their key-and-source pairs as compact Unicode JSON, encodes UTF-8 and computes SHA-256; this admission changes no recipe bytes or calibration digest. The assurance is source-provenance computational equality under collision and second-preimage resistance, not semantic equivalence or evidence identity. Hidden fields: none. External library code, inherited fixtures from other files, environment and executed results remain outside the declared closure. The review carrier binds operation, path, claim, gloss, label assessments and a closure identity whose recipe, function and digest fields are exhaustive, with only an optional public_commit locator. Unknown fields, recipes, unsafe paths and ambiguous roots refuse before comparison. The validating producer uses the existing case_identity function; witness_binding validates the complete carrier and witness_currency establishes recomputability before the tracker consumes its currency. The present consumers are calibration currency and assertion-review currency: only a current reviewed closure supports a witnessed-label route. The digest is compared only within this typed role and recipe, never as a protocol or report identity. A current review recomputes from its carrying tree, which remains public after import re-signing. A stale review must instead recompute from a named public ancestor and supplies no witness; an unrecomputable review refuses. Any closure change requires re-review or an explicit stale historical binding. Any projection or primitive change requires a new recipe identifier and re-review, never silent reinterpretation. Equality proves neither correctness, entailment, authenticity nor complete dependency coverage.

**Definition (Author token)** · `def:identity:author-token` An author token is the qualified chain asset whose run-qualified identifier is an Actor’s permanent identity; control of the output holding it is the authority to declare that Actor’s signing delegates. The payload family (`conv:devnet:wire-boundary`) designs an unbuilt successor that retains permanent token identity and control/delegate distinctions while replacing marked qualification with eligible single-unit assets and assigning the burn's asset identifier within the selected network, with the successor signed Actor name following (`dec:identity:actor-document-name`) and its admission owed under (`open:identity:chain-actor-binding`); this statement retains its landed or admitted meaning until replacement, and the identity and development-profile owners owe the coordinated payload and authority admission cut.

**Argument (Author token)** · `arg:identity:author-token` Consensus follows the creation-fixed asset through every move, so identity survives any change of delegates; control of the holding output is the one power the public chain shows along that path, so it is the authority whose declarations a reader can follow. This definition gathers (`dec:identity:authentication-keys`) and (`amend:identity:author-token-binding`) without changing their qualification, recipes or admitted identities. The concept and its consequences are (`spec:author:concept`).

**Amendment (Author token binding)** · `amend:identity:author-token-binding` This withdraws the direct-key identity and no-delegate-rotation interpretation of (`dec:identity:authentication-keys`) and the target/key equality in (`def:identity:authentication-material`). The implemented authentication edition is pn-act-authentication/author-token-bip340/v1; its designed v2 successor under (`amend:identity:act-proposal-admission`) preserves every token-binding choice here; pn-act-authentication/bip340/v1 remains a distinct historical design, never an alias. The target in pn-devnet-v1/run/target is the Elements asset identifier, in the lowercase hexadecimal display order of the admitted codec, while run remains the existing 32-byte run atom. Binary asset identifiers in this contract are the 32 bytes obtained by decoding that display spelling; consensus serialization byte order is converted explicitly, never guessed. The asset identifier is the creation-fixed value that consensus follows through transfers. An issuance transaction hash identifies a container that may create several assets and changes with transaction details; an issuance outpoint needs the issuance derivation to identify the asset; another digest adds a recipe without adding authority. The asset identifier is therefore selected without an extra repository hash. Its external derivation is admitted by (`amend:identity:author-asset-recipe`). Control of the output holding the token is the root authority over this author; a named signing key is a delegate, not that authority and not the identity. The creation and qualification evidence is (`rule:devnet:author-token`). The same target is the permanent burn destination under (`amend:devnet:burn-targets`) and (`rule:harness:burn-targets`); a supported token move can add declared delegates for the same author but alone retires none, with the epoch’s delegate set determined from the effective cutoff onward under (`rule:identity:author-key-history`) and (`rule:identity:retirement-order`), never moving burn between addresses. The concept is (`spec:author:concept`). From this authentication edition, a new author target is a qualified asset identity and nothing else; no new arbitrary or direct-key target can be assigned. Previously accepted arbitrary targets, including direct-key targets, retain their old meaning. Assignment under another binding means historical assignment in the accepted record as of the admitting epoch. A token whose asset identifier collides with such an assignment refuses admission rather than claiming the old identifier or its burn. Fresh qualified targets must be unassigned in that view before funding; a predictable asset identifier cannot be reserved by creating a new non-token target. Creating a token is not Registration, standing, title, human identity or uniqueness of a person. Loss of its controlling key has no recovery mechanism; a later recovery or algorithm transition belongs in this record and must preserve old verdicts. realizes{``amend:identity:act-proposal-admission``; The binding selects the designed edition while preserving identity; it asserts no private entropy.} The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the successor admission cut remains owed by this statement's owner. The payload family (`conv:devnet:wire-boundary`) designs an unbuilt successor that retains creation-fixed asset derivation and byte order while assigning contextual asset32 only to the successor burn and leaving admission of the actor-document name of (`dec:identity:actor-document-name`) in signed acts and frames owed under (`open:identity:chain-actor-binding`); this statement retains its landed or admitted meaning until replacement, and the identity and development-profile owners owe the coordinated payload and authority admission cut.

**Amendment (Author asset locator admission)** · `amend:identity:author-asset-recipe` Admit the external design recipe elements/initial-asset-id/v1 under (`gate:identity:implementation`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), not its implementation. The object is the issuance prevout (transaction identifier and output index) together with the initial issuance contract hash, identifying the asset consensus follows through transfers. The recipe owner is pn-act-authentication; pn-wallet's bounded chain-layout adapter consumes its derivation, and the issuing wallet produces the identifier, while token qualification and public authentication replay recompute it and refuse a mismatched claimed asset or author target. The benefit is correspondence with the chain's existing asset locator, rather than an extra repository digest or a transaction identifier that conflates several issuances. Assurance class: artifact binding of the exact issuance inputs under the external SHA-256 construction's binding assumption, not authenticated provenance or qualification by itself. Serialize the prevout as its 32 transaction-identifier bytes in consensus order followed by the unsigned output index in four little-endian bytes, without the transaction input's issuance or pegin flag bits. Compute H = SHA-256(SHA-256(those 36 bytes)), with ordinary finalized hashes. Let M(left,right) initialize SHA-256 at its standard initial state, compress the single 64-byte block left || right, and return the resulting eight state words as 32 big-endian bytes, without padding or finalization. The entropy is M(H,C), where C is exactly the initial issuance contract field's 32 bytes, not a fresh hash of that field or its display spelling. The asset's internal bytes are M(entropy,Z), where Z is 32 zero bytes; this is the two-leaf fast Merkle construction, not finalized SHA-256 or SHA-256d of either concatenation. Reverse those asset bytes for the lowercase hexadecimal display spelling and for the decoded display-order binary identity of (`amend:identity:author-token-binding`). The producer follows the admitted Elements codec; independent conformance vectors and replay recomputation use the reviewed RustCrypto SHA-256 route, including its compression primitive with the state and no-padding rule above, not a second call to the Elements asset helper. The first token-evidence cut implements producer and consuming comparison together, with independent vectors and wrong-prevout, contract, byte-order and finalization controls before operation admission. A changed prevout, contract field, serialization, compression rule or display conversion makes the result inapplicable; an unsupported recipe or missing recomputation evidence refuses the claimed binding. A changed external derivation requires a new recipe and authentication edition with explicit replacement from public evidence, never reinterpretation or reassignment of old identifiers and burn. There are no hidden fields. The token-evidence status names Qualified, Moved and Unsupported are conditional on the supplied input claims; they do not establish that those claims are chain truth. The result alone proves no unique human, token qualification, control, delegate authority, Registration, standing, title, inclusion or chain finality. This is the fixed external chain-locator use of (`amend:crypto:wallet-codec-features`), not permission for a new repository semantic digest; primitive and operation evidence remain required before implementation. The payload family (`conv:devnet:wire-boundary`) designs an unbuilt successor that keeps this external derivation unchanged and adds no issuance-contract hash or sole-issuance restriction; this statement retains its landed or admitted meaning until replacement, and the identity and development-profile owners owe the coordinated payload and authority admission cut.

**Amendment (Signing key hash admission)** · `amend:identity:signing-key-hash` Admit the design recipe pn-act-authentication/signing-key-hash/v1 under (`gate:identity:implementation`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), not its implementation. Its exact preimage is the ASCII recipe text, one zero byte, then exactly 32 opaque bytes intended as a BIP-340 x-only key encoding; its digest is RustCrypto SHA-256. Parse and validate the point before using it as a verification key. The token holder produces the digest for the on-chain key declaration; the shared authentication verifier and offline replay recompute it from disclosed keys to decide delegate membership. The benefit is compact public delegation without placing a raw key in the chain declaration; direct key storage is an alternative but does not implement the selected hash-only contract. Assurance class: artifact binding of the canonical key encoding under collision resistance, not identity or proof of possession. The hash operation refuses any length other than 32 but does not parse a curve point. Membership and signature verification first parse the disclosed key through the admitted base library and refuse an invalid point before testing membership. A declaration of bytes that are not a key can authorize nothing. A recomputed hash absent from the active set refuses ActorBinding and grants no delegate authority. A malformed declaration has only the effect specified by (`rule:devnet:author-key-output`), not authority inferred from its tag. The hash contains no run or author: shared delegation is intentional, and the act messages bind each author separately. A changed key, encoding, hash or domain is inapplicable; a different recipe requires a new authentication edition and declaration tag. Before external use the producer, decoder and verifier migrate together with independent vectors and wrong-key/domain/length controls under (`amend:crypto:authentication-entry`); missing operation admission refuses. Old declarations are never reinterpreted. No secrecy, token control, chain finality, Registration or inclusion follows from a digest. There are no hidden fields in this recipe. The payload family (`conv:devnet:wire-boundary`) designs an unbuilt successor that keeps this digest recipe and disclosed-point validation unchanged while replacing its successor declaration carrier; this statement retains its landed or admitted meaning until replacement, and the identity and development-profile owners owe the coordinated payload and authority admission cut.

**Rule (Author key history)** · `rule:identity:author-key-history` Begin at the qualified issuance, which is the first transaction of the token history and may name keys. Follow only the unique explicit unit of that asset through its spends, ordered by chain block height and transaction position, to the selected prefix. For each transaction on this path collect every valid declaration under (`rule:devnet:author-key-output`); output position orders the evidence but membership is a set. The active set is their union from the currently effective retirement transaction inclusive. With no retirement it starts at issuance. A transfer with no key output preserves the set; repeat declarations add no duplicate authority. When several qualified tokens move in one transaction, every valid declaration applies to each token whose input meets the all-outputs signature commitment of (`rule:devnet:author-token`). A spend outside that admitted script requirement makes only that token's path unsupported: its later authority views are unavailable, even if a subsequent output is explicit, while old keys and accepted verdicts are not deleted and other tokens remain independently trackable. Confidential outputs unrelated to the token unit or tagged declarations do not break its path under the consensus-validity premise. Later paths separate: neither another token's earlier history nor its later transactions are inherited. This is not transitive union of author sets. A key occurring both before and at or after the retirement boundary remains active through its later declaration. The holder may move the token to a new address to add delegates; that move alone retires none. Retiring all delegates requires a later history transaction naming no keys, then an ordinary act naming it, with no later declarations in the selected prefix. The act naming the point is itself authorized by the pre-effect set; the resulting empty set from the following epoch authorizes no act until a subsequent token move names a key. Its identity and prior history remain intact. The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the successor admission cut remains owed by this statement's owner.

**Rule (Authentication chain view)** · `rule:identity:author-chain-view` This deployment's admission rule for an act in epoch k uses the mandatory author-authority projection of (`dec:devnet:author-authority-case`) over the one settled chain prefix bound to that epoch's recognized marker snapshot, under (`amend:storage:marker-settlement`) and (`rule:design:marker-recognition`), plus retirements effective for that epoch from acts ordered in strictly earlier epochs under (`rule:identity:retirement-order`). The deployment marker interpretation must identify the exact block hash and height of its chain prefix; if it cannot, token authentication is unavailable, not a read of the current tip. Token transfers need not be accepted burn events: scan the chain prefix, not the burn accepted set. Check the declared stability buffer and finality premises for that prefix; missing or contradictory evidence prevents readiness. Proposals and seals may be checked provisionally against a named earlier view, but ordering rechecks the admitted epoch view. At one fixed prefix and retirement state the verdict is deterministic. Extending a valid token history can add authority, never remove it: no-key transfers, duplicate keys and unrelated transactions cannot turn a valid delegate into an invalid one. New key declarations can change an earlier absent-key refusal into membership for a later admission view. Retirement can remove authority only prospectively; a later explicit re-declaration can restore it. An unsupported confidential or destroyed token path makes later views unavailable, not a deletion of old keys or old verdicts. A reorganization of an unpinned candidate changes only provisional results; replacement of the settled prefix violates the inherited finality premise and halts the run without rewriting accepted acts. Availability is a deployment premise, not a consequence of inclusion or a signature. Author-key authorization is oracle material of this realization: the signed Elements chain supplies public rules and data under (`rule:devnet:author-key-output`), and a changed history can change the local authority result without changing the recorded author or completed calculation; whether that result permits further local admission remains the deployment’s policy. The deployment therefore requires a named mandatory author-authority case, binding at pin; closure verifies each recorded proposal and approval signature under the key the record maps to its covered bytes; it does not establish that the key speaks for the stated Actor. The deployment's mandatory author-authority check is separate and conditional on its declared rules and pinned account. This remains a required deployment authorization extension; network-v0.37.4 separately defines an authority oracle over public history, and neither its result nor this local admission policy is a predicate of the paper’s completed calculation. The case semantics are (`dec:devnet:author-authority-case`). In the authentication admissions, authorship, unauthorized proposals and the declared authority case refer to that mandatory deployment check, while the paper’s closure verifies the recorded signatures only; this qualification changes no admitted recipe or identity meaning. Identity, development-profile and publication designers own the still-held declaration representation, required boundary binding, conditional-claim review and execution evidence; this route cannot be enabled until those admissions close. The class is settled; separately selected component representation and conditional comparison are implemented with finite controls, while authenticated case evaluation and production admission remain unimplemented. realizes{``def:graph:author-authority-oracle``; This compares the recorded-signature and conditional authority scopes; it does not bind the local extension as a paper case or assume external authority.} The component representation of this same local check is admitted by (`amend:identity:local-authority-declaration`), (`amend:identity:authority-account`) and (`amend:identity:local-authority-boundary`); its consensus-validity premise, required complete publication binding and implementation holds are unchanged. The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the successor admission cut remains owed by this statement's owner.

**Amendment (Network key retirement)** · `amend:identity:key-retirement` Every act pairs its unchanged author identifier with its own retirement_point field under (`amend:identity:act-proposal-admission`). Identity remains the run-qualified creation-fixed asset identifier of (`amend:identity:author-token-binding`); the field is never part of ActId.author or ActId equality. Standing, burn destinations, author-local sequence, same-author comparisons and every author-indexed map keep their existing keys. An edge targeting an Actor or Profile names only that identity and carries no retirement point for its target: the source cannot assert the target author's key-management state. Two acts of one author may carry different points without becoming different authors. No family is specialized, no additional act is required, and no extra price is charged for the field; each ordinary act still pays its ordinary price and satisfies ordinary admission. A dedicated Registration would make recovery depend on buying an additional profile update and on interpreting its frame-binding clause; Withdraw/Rescind concern Offer cancellation and Publish concerns Content. Carrying the field on every act avoids those family interpretations. The standalone deployment record, pn-act-authentication/key-retirement/v1 recipe and frame remain withdrawn and were never deployed. Ordinary three-signature authentication binds the field, so there is no separate retirement signer, record list or migration. realizes{``amend:identity:act-proposal-admission``; The retirement rule uses the signed body field and unchanged identity, not proposal concealment.} The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the successor admission cut remains owed by this statement's owner.

**Rule (Retirement order and effect)** · `rule:identity:retirement-order` All acts of epoch k use the key set in force for k under (`rule:identity:author-chain-view`), including acts that name a new retirement point. Judge each well-formed field exactly once against epoch k's fixed settled chain prefix and the boundary effective at its start. Valid means the transaction occurs on that author's own supported token path in that view; latest means its position along that path (chain height and transaction position), never the order or identifier of the act naming it. A transaction absent from that path, beyond the selected prefix or earlier than the effective boundary has no effect. It is ignored for retirement, not grounds to refuse the otherwise valid act or wait for another chain view. Missing chain evidence remains unavailable, not proof of absence. A no-effect field never activates later from that old act; another act must name it in an applicable view. Set the boundary for epoch k+1 to the greatest of the starting boundary and every valid non-backward point named by that author's ordered acts of k. With no such act the boundary persists; at the first epoch for an author it starts at qualified issuance. Equal points are idempotent, different points combine by path maximum, and the named transaction's own declarations remain active. Earlier acts and all acts of k retain their verdicts. This avoids circular same-epoch authority and preserves monotonicity. Every act retains its field, including ineffective fields; no payload, Profile display head, act family or standalone list filters the computation. Public replay needs complete prior ordered acts and the applicable chain prefixes: restating a point is useful but a single later act with a stale or unknown field cannot replace the earlier maximum. The retired standalone signature recipe is not reinterpreted. Publication authentication and complete closure remain (`open:design:dependency-opening-recipe`). An active delegate authenticates the ordinary act through proposal, seal and approval. With one lost delegate, another active delegate can name the desired point. With every delegate lost, the token holder moves the token naming a fresh key, waits for that transfer's settled authority view, and puts the new point in its next ordinary act; no special act or extra writing price is needed. The ordinary act still requires funds, capacity and admission, so recovery has no time guarantee. A disclosed delegate can name at worst the latest transaction on that token's path in the judging prefix, potentially emptying the next epoch's active set; it cannot name an effective future cutoff, move the token, change identity or erase old verdicts. A later token move can introduce a key beyond that cutoff. The author's first act normally names the creation transaction and uses its declared delegate set; an older ineffective field in a later act never restores retired keys. The public claim is deterministic active-set and declared-authority reconstruction from authenticated ordered acts and the selected pinned chain evidence; availability, finality and off-record inclusion are not proved by that record. Published deployment policy: when choosing among an author's candidate acts with differing points, the host prefers those carrying the more recent valid path position over older points. This is a fault-tolerance preference against crowding out during the epoch when old delegates remain active, subordinate to ordinary formation and admission. It does not alter authoritative sequence validity or guarantee inclusion. The verifier never rejects an epoch for violating this preference and no conformance claim is made for it: an act the host did not include leaves no public record by which a third party could check the preference. The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the successor admission cut remains owed by this statement's owner.

**Amendment (Shared-key signed objects)** · `amend:identity:shared-key-messages` This amends (`amend:identity:authentication-recipe-gate`), (`amend:identity:proposal-signature`), (`amend:identity:host-seal`) and (`amend:identity:approval-signature`) for the token edition: replace their direct-key authorization prerequisite with the selected public token-history membership, without changing their message bytes or recipe identifiers. In particular the proposal retains Sign(P) as specified in (`amend:identity:proposal-signature`), with the explicit signer-side key-use restriction of (`amend:identity:authentication-recipe-gate`); public verification alone does not establish that restriction. P already commits B including the complete ActId.author, so two authors sharing a key have different proposal messages under the admitted binding assumptions. V includes B and P; the host seal signs those exact V bytes, and approval signs V and that seal. No author is inferred solely from a key or its hash, and no signature can be transplanted between authors without failing the message binding. Every act binds both the unchanged author identifier and its retirement point through B, P and V; sharing a delegate cannot transplant either the act or its statement to another author. The initial token edition changed authority interpretation without changing the three existing message functions. Its designed v2 successor additionally selects proposal/v2 and actor-proposal/v2 under (`amend:identity:act-proposal-admission`) and (`amend:identity:proposal-signature`); the host-seal and approval frame functions remain unchanged. The host remains the explicitly configured run key of (`dec:identity:authentication-host`): making host authority transferable by an author token would add a separate deployment-governance transition without solving this Actor fault. Old edition records are not silently upgraded; new ordering supports only the explicitly selected token edition after its admission evidence exists. realizes{``amend:identity:authentication-recipe-gate``; The token edition changes authorization while preserving message bytes and the signer restriction, not claiming private entropy.} realizes{``amend:identity:act-proposal-admission``; Shared-key separation uses the framed body and edition, not an entropy guarantee.} The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the successor admission cut remains owed by this statement's owner.

**Verification (Public token fixtures)** · `verif:identity:token-fixtures` The public authentication fixture suite supplies daemon asset identifiers and independently computed key hashes for the admitted recipes; the wallet token fixture suites supply conditional per-transaction interpretation, supported witness type bytes and allocation-boundary controls. (`claim:discipline:authentication-values-have-independent-byte-checks`) independently recomputes the key digests and script bytes in the check suite. This narrows only the public byte prerequisite of (`open:identity:act-production-concealment`); signature operations, live complete history, entropy, custody, durable salt reservation and retry state remain held. No hash match claims point validity or proof of possession.

**Amendment (Verification operation evidence boundary)** · `amend:identity:verification-operation-evidence` (`verif:crypto:public-verification-fixtures`) supplies the public key parser, fixed-message BIP-340 operation and parse-before-hash membership candidate, pending independent integration review. It does not close (`open:identity:act-production-concealment`): canonical header encoding, framed messages, the three-signature act verifier, authenticated token histories, signing, custody and durable concealment remain outstanding. No signed-object recipe or digest preimage changes.

**Verification (Authentication encoding fixtures)** · `verif:identity:authentication-encoding` pn-act-authentication supplies canonical header and material encoding, strict decoding, three role-specific message producers and field-by-field comparison under (`def:identity:verified-header-name`), (`def:identity:authentication-material`) and (`amend:identity:authentication-recipe-gate`). Independent golden values, canonical-form refusals and deliberately broken message and comparison controls establish the bounded public encoding scope. The operation neither signs nor verifies a signature and does not close (`open:identity:act-production-concealment`) or (`open:crypto:dependency-admission`). Public authority and contextual admission remain later duties. The withdrawn standalone retirement producer and its marker wrapper are absent; these version-one operations do not implement the designed per-act retirement field. realizes{``amend:identity:authentication-recipe-gate``; The finite encoding fixtures exercise public bytes and comparison, not private entropy or custody.} Every cited claim remains finite version-one evidence of the preceding construction. The new-tag conformance obligation is held on (`open:identity:act-realization-admission`) until the coordinated reference authentication code cut; passed fixture execution is not v2 implementation. realizes{``open:identity:act-realization-admission``; The evidence scope retains the new-tag implementation hold, not an entropy claim.} Separate version-two evidence is carried by the claim obligation for this statement: golden recipes, representation refusals, the three-part dependency proof, per-field comparison refusals and namespace/recipe refusals; the hash audit alone regenerates the independent corpus. This narrows the recipe/header portion only; retained carriage, signature integration, private entropy, custody and live authority remain held. The separately selected third formation has finite independent body, proposal, content, dependency, header, material and role values and refusal-order evidence in the two owning packages; its size-only carriage and private final-byte controls have their narrower stated claims. These pure comparisons establish no authenticated opening or production enablement. The lineage concept (`spec:lineage:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the token, authority, burn or marker subject stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted scope until that successor is admitted, and the successor admission cut remains owed by this statement's owner.

**Amendment (Canonical publication-core digest)** · `amend:identity:publication-core-digest` This amendment retains the recipe of a design admission under (`req:identity:admission-record`) and (`gate:identity:implementation`); its consumer is withdrawn and its class is stopped under (`rec:identity:publication-core-digest-status`), with the recipe below retained verbatim for a retake and no operation enabled. Its typed object is the complete Work-free core of a document accepted by the independently selected publication operation under the reserved org.peer-network.test.publication major-three, minor-zero coordinate of (`amend:design:publication-version-three`), once assigned under (`sig:interchange:theory-assignment`). No admissible input exists before that assignment. This recipe deliberately applies to minor zero alone: the independently selected operation and this typed object both select exactly 3.0.0 under (`amend:design:publication-version-three`). One canonical spelling would also be possible for each later assigned minor, and retaining the minor in the projected core distinguishes those values; that fact alone does not widen this recipe. Restricting applicability keeps the admitted object class aligned with that operation at the cost of a new recipe identifier and corresponding comparison selection when a later minor is assigned. Widening now would avoid that later identifier but commit this recipe to inputs of other assigned minors; this admission takes the bounded class instead. A reader of a later minor must compute its separately admitted recipe from that minor’s complete validated core, never apply or relabel pn-host/publication-core/v1; that assignment owes the new identifier, applicability and independent vectors even if its projection and framing rule are otherwise identical. This admission specifies no section schema, member list, example document or whole-theory coordinate assignment. The owner is pn-host's publication operation and the designed producer is its canonical core projection. The previously named reproduction consumer is withdrawn with (`open:identity:reproduction-binding`); no present consumer or deadline-bound migration remains. (`rec:identity:publication-core-digest-status`) records this amendment and its stop, without changing the admitted object or recipe. No signing operation accepts a caller-chosen core digest: authentication keys sign only messages computed inside admitted recipe operations under (`amend:identity:authentication-recipe-gate`). The proposed independent publication-to-reproduction authority boundary is withdrawn with (`open:identity:reproduction-binding`); no signature role or key-selection policy was assigned. The act-and-epoch document concept (`spec:actepoch:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the document subjects stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted rule until its owner admits the successor document cut, and current fixtures witness only their existing scope.

The walk of (`alg:identity:adjudication`) requires the complete document already fully decoded and validated by that independently selected operation, with exactly the version spelling [3,0,0]. Validation is a premise, not an effect of hashing. Freedom from incidental content is also a premise on the assigned theory: its canonical encoding and field removals must satisfy (`rule:identity:no-incidentals`); excluding annotations alone does not establish it. The original benefit under (`crit:identity:benefit`) was a compact core comparison across the proposed reproduction authority boundary, not supplied by an act residue, seal, account or existing parent identity. With that consumer and its key-9 binding withdrawn, that benefit no longer supplies a present consumer’s decision; the stop under (`rec:identity:publication-core-digest-status`) applies. The class is semantic: canonical projection supplies determinism and completeness over that projection, with freedom from incidental content supplied by the assigned-theory premise; a fixed role prefix supplies domain separation and recipe identification; SHA-256 supplies collision and second-preimage resistance, not exact equality; all inputs and framing are public and recomputable. No hidden field, blinding value, salt, nonce or secret enters this recipe.

The recipe identifier is pn-host/publication-core/v1. Let C be the bytes of the same canonical envelope with annotation key 9 absent, as the rule of (`amend:design:implementation-annotations`) states: encode the head of the remaining map entries and retain their canonical keys and values, never a hole or a null replacement. The projection is this rule, not an enumeration of section members. The preimage is ASCII(pn-host/publication-core/v1) followed by one zero octet followed by C, with no further array, length field or terminator; its digest is SHA-256 of that complete preimage, an exact 32-octet residue identified by this recipe. Canonical full-consumption CBOR and the fixed zero-terminated ASCII prefix make the framing unambiguous. Against the repository-owned SHA-256 framings admitted here, distinct zero-free recipe identifiers terminated by one zero octet form a prefix-free set, so distinct identifiers cannot yield the same preimage: this covers F for act, seal, approval and result-position recipes under (`amend:identity:act-framing`) and (`amend:identity:result-position-recipe`), storage framing under (`amend:identity:storage-recipes`), the length-prefixed marker-policy recipe under (`amend:identity:marker-policy-admission`), and key-hash framing under (`amend:identity:signing-key-hash`). The resulting residues remain subject to the hash assumptions. BIP-340 internal tagged hashes belong to the base signature operation under (`amend:identity:authentication-recipe-gate`); their separation rests on the hash assumption, not this zero-terminated-prefix argument. This argument does not cover external chain derivations by pretending they use repository recipe prefixes. There is no additional run, host, history or epoch argument: later reproduction and publication messages bind their own context; any such values already belonging to the complete core are retained by the projection. There is no generic digest entry point for signers and no serialization of a digest reference is assigned here.

The selected publication operation first applies its own input bounds, canonical refusing decoder, complete schema and semantic checks in their assigned order; malformed or noncanonical input is never repaired or hashed. The coordinate and version checks guard that validated-input premise: (`amend:design:publication-version-three`) already assigns Edition for an unsupported namespace, major or minor and Patch for a nonzero patch, with no patch normalization. They are unreachable for input satisfying this recipe’s exact 3.0.0 premise, not additional stages in the publication operation’s refusal order; that order remains the assigning cut’s. The fixture guard preserves Edition before Patch. Next PreimageBound refuses if the projected core plus the 28-octet prefix exceeds 2^61-1 octets, the whole-octet SHA-256 message limit; hence C is at most 2^61-29 octets. Checked arithmetic precedes hashing. This is an intrinsic primitive bound, not a publication allocation allowance, annotation bound or whole-package limit; the assigning cut must publish the tighter enclosing resource bounds and show that every admitted complete document fits. Only after these checks does the operation compute the residue. An unknown recipe is inapplicable, never equal by bare residue; absent or unvalidated core is unavailable, never an empty core or a failed comparison. A matching recipe and residue establish computational equality of the canonical projection only. They establish no validity, authenticity, completeness of history, order or finality, and no signature operation is enabled.

The identity becomes stale when any retained canonical core byte changes, subject to the hash assumptions; adding, removing or changing optional annotation content alone leaves it unchanged. Changing the projection to a member list, adding a context input, changing the version-spelling rule, moving the annotation key, or changing canonicalization, primitive or domain separator requires a new recipe identifier under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`). Assignment of the complete theory supplies the typed inputs, not a redefinition of this rule. Earlier identities, recipes and meanings remain unchanged; migration recomputes from available complete validated inputs, never relabels a residue. Under (`req:identity:stop-record`), on 2026-11-21 additional per-section and per-member digests stop at the parent-assured branch: the complete core identity already binds those retained values; retake only for a separately transferred or authenticated component with a distinct equality decision not supplied by its parent. A digest of optional annotation bytes for this core-comparison purpose stops at the no named consumer's decision branch of (`alg:identity:adjudication`); retake only for a separately admitted annotation consumer and its own authority boundary, never by expanding this core recipe.

Independent projection fixtures are packages/act-authentication/tests/publication_core-golden.json, recomputed by the standard-library encoder publication_core_vectors.py in that directory. They are not documents of the theory and no fixture digest is a pre-assignment publication identity. Their two coordinate keys and seven opaque section placeholders exercise only the projection rule and framing: annotation presence and absence, the remaining-entry map head, one retained-value change in each key, refusing noncanonical input, coordinate and patch refusals, five illustrations of distinct F-framed domains and the intrinsic preimage-limit arithmetic; the structural separation argument above covers the admitted framings beyond those illustrations. Arithmetic limit fixtures allocate no enormous input and claim no successful boundary-size hash execution. Complete real-document vectors, validation/refusal integration and enclosing-bound evidence are owed at assignment and implementation. Successful fixture recomputation admits no implementation or enablement. Reproduction and publication message recipes, finalized-order mechanism, floor disposition, numerical route, annotation bound and whole-theory assignment remain their existing owners' work.

Let hY = H_Y(I,N_Y,Y), hD = H_D(I,N_D,encode(D)) and P = H_P(B,hY,hD), with independently fresh nonces. The repository maps H_Y, H_D and H_P through its framing F to PN-content-pre-v2, PN-dependency-pre-v2 and PN-act-proposal-v2 under proposal/v2. The host checks authority/signature, committed act-identifier uniqueness, canonical formation, hyper-edge A/T consistency, public protocol references, bounded canonical dependencies, the dependency nonce's form, absence of self-dependency and recomputed P. Pending attempts reserve no slot. With independent fresh salts it computes C_Y = H_CY(I,P,S_Y,hY) under PN-content-v2 and C_D = H_CD(I,P,S_D,encode(D)) under PN-dependency-v2. The latter deliberately retains canonical dependencies rather than hD: the three-part fraud proof opens exactly that projection; symmetry with content supplies no reason to change it. V retains B,P,ActorSignature,C_Y,C_D and their editions; neither pre-digest nor private opening is retained in the reduced structural record. Host receipt of a content pair adds checks of the content nonce's form and its opening as carriage checks, not eligibility conditions. The Actor verifies its retained openings, exact returned fields and all common hyper-edge-leg fields before approving. Ordering requires valid approval, unchanged header/legs, a matching complete dependency opening and satisfiable placement.

| Requirement | Design argument; not executed new-tag evidence |
|---|---|
| (i) Signature verification | The disclosed actor key maps the recorded proposal and approval signatures to their covered bytes. Proposal verification checks the signature over P, whose binding to B and both pre-digests covers the stated author and ActId; approval verification checks exact V and seal under the recorded key. This establishes signature well-formedness, not authority for that Actor. |
| (ii) Proposal integrity | Injective framing and binding retain the exact body, projections and requested states; the Actor checks the returned commitments. |
| (iii) Approval coverage of host additions | The recorded approval covers exact V and seal, including all host-added finalizable fields retained by the admitted V and commitment association; returning them before approval alone is not the argument. |
| (iv) Exact approval | Approval binds exact V and seal; carriage acceptance cannot replace it. |
| (v) Hyper-act unity | Formation and approval check A/T consistency and agreement across all act-level fields of both legs. |
| (vi) Opening-independent projection binding | C_Y binds hY and its private opening; C_D binds encode(D). Removal cannot furnish a different opening under the stated collision and second-preimage assumptions. |
| (vii) Ordering accountability | (I,D,S_D) recomputes C_D using retained P and exhibits the violated dependency placement, without revealing N_Y or changing the finalized record. |

Actor authorization is supplied separately by this deployment's mandatory author-authority check under (`dec:devnet:author-authority-case`); it is not inferred from a signature verifying under a disclosed key. The proof of P's formation remains conditional on the admitted binding construction and required supplied openings; a signature alone does not recompute hidden preimages.

The five v2 tags denote these five constructions only. The implemented pure content/v2 recipe commits the pre-digest; proposal/v2 uses independent supplied nonces, and dependency/v2 opens encode(D). Version-one recipes, decoders and golden bytes remain immutable. The seal and approval tags remain PN-verified-act-v1 and PN-act-approval-v1: v2 commitment recipes inside the exact signed V exclude a mixed reading, so a new message-role edition is unnecessary. Authentication v2 and the major-two header remain distinct from the implemented version-one closed theory. New recipes change P, commitments, V and signatures without changing the framing algorithms. The producer-only ComputedProposalCommitment path stays distinct from an unopened digest. Independent vectors, mixed-recipe refusals, both carriage paths, removal and public dependency-proof checks precede enablement; no fixed vector proves entropy or opening existence. The pure new-tag recipe and header prerequisite is implemented; dependent retirement and publication enablement still require retained carriage and authenticated integration, which these fixtures do not establish. Missing private openings cannot be recovered from a residue.

**Open Question (Optional centralized content carriage, closed)** · `open:identity:optional-act-carriage` Closed by network-v0.37.4, premise{``def:graph:verified-act``; Act acceptance is independent of optional content carriage.}, premise{``rem:graph:act-payload-custody-phases``; Centralized append permits decentralized carriage.} and premise{``def:graph:act-payload-state``; A pair held only by its author is full; availability belongs to an accepting carrier.}. No paper answer remains outstanding. Repository enablement remains under (`open:identity:act-realization-admission`) and (`open:identity:carriage-result-recipe`); closure of the paper question is not implementation evidence. realizes{``open:identity:act-realization-admission``; This retains the coordinated new-tag implementation hold, not evidence of private entropy or custody.}

The optional field requests carriage within the same act process. A carrier checks the aggregate encoded locked payload bound, the content nonce's format, length, freshness and non-reuse under the published generation rule, the hY opening and the C_Y opening after salting. It owns custody, declared service and atomic removal of Y,N_Y and content opening material. The host owns these duties only when accepting carriage. A wrong opening or unavailable service refuses carriage, not the act. Transport resource failure can still stop the handshake operationally. The absent-pair path keeps no invented opening at Layer 1. After approval the Actor supplies the host salt to another accepting carrier when needed. A full pair held only by its author is full, with no Layer 1 availability promise; absence is neither empty content nor removal, and reduced state never reverses.

Both paths disclose B,D,N_D,hY,P and the messages and salts, so the submission observer can reconstruct hD and retain the full dependency opening. A sent pair also discloses Y,N_Y. Confidentiality of the path remains a deployment premise; reduction cannot erase earlier observers' knowledge. A party can strip the optional pair without changing V or seal, and a host can seal and order while declining carriage. The authenticated carriage result, separate from the seal, lets the Actor distinguish an acceptance it can authenticate from unknown service; suppression cannot forge acceptance.

**Open Question (Authenticated carriage-result recipe)** · `open:identity:carriage-result-recipe` Accepted carriage is attested to the Actor by an authenticated carriage result separate from the act seal. This new carriage-result message role names the accepted act/content commitment, requested service and state, and the independently expected accepting carrier, with an unambiguous request context; its replay/stale conditions, authority selection, framing, edition, exact bytes and independent vectors remain held under (`gate:identity:implementation`), (`req:identity:admission-record`) and (`amend:identity:authentication-recipe-gate`). A missing result is neither acceptance nor refusal: suppression leaves carriage unknown. The Actor keeps its pair, retries the same carrier or requests another carrier. An exact retry to the same carrier reuses its request context; a different carrier or changed requested service uses a new context bound to the same act/content commitment. Stale acceptance after removal must not attest a new service request; the exact freshness and replay recipe remains held. Suppression cannot forge acceptance. A result attests acceptance, not perpetual availability or erasure of prior copies, and cannot authorize an act or alter ordering. Reusing the act seal to attest carriage would make it differ between the two paths; giving no attestation would leave silent denial. Both alternatives are set aside. This role is not a generic signature permission, and no byte is assigned here. Under the adopted network-v0.37.4 courtesy edition, the held authenticated receipt binds request, sequence, party, projection and object; acceptance is separate from the act seal. The initial intent’s sequence representation and transition to later requests, authority selection, framing, edition and exact bytes remain held; no numeric initial sequence is inferred. An identical retry returns the one issued receipt as persisted identical bytes. No legacy request field or present pair implies a five-verb intent; an adapter needs explicit edition-specific interpretation and controller authorization without rewriting the historical signature. premise{``def:graph:requested-publication-intent``; The five request verbs and authenticated receipt need their own body and receipt design.} The designed request context binds run, act and retained formation/object context, projection, controller, intended party, courtesy edition, verb and controller-local sequence. The signed initial request is the origin of its act/projection/party sequence, and its receipt binds that origin; later sequences exceed it. C9 admits a representation and comparison for that origin, not a choice whether it exists. An identical retry in the receipt-bound values returns the one issued receipt; this deployment persists and returns identical bytes. A tagged Initial(P) representation remains possible only with an admitted ordering relation before all Later values; no wire value is assigned here. Later requests cannot amend the signed act. The author controls both projections in this deployment; a later request selects its own authenticated authority view and cannot modify the original act pin. Persist receipt bytes, service result and sequence high-water mark before returning acceptance; changed bytes at one sequence and older requests refuse, exhaustion never wraps, and uncertain durable completion is unavailable rather than acceptance. Forget retains no opening in its sequence bookkeeping. Receipt, request and initial extension each need their own admitted schema and role before implementation. These are designed service rules with no executed claim.

**Open Question (Optional host contribution to a content nonce)** · `open:identity:host-contribution-option` The client may choose an optional host contribution to its content-nonce derivation, before or after the option's recipe is admitted, or proceed on its own entropy alone. This is never a submission, carriage or act-acceptance condition, a default or a successor to client generation. The proposed recipe derives the content nonce's random suffix from independent host and client contributions with a domain-separated hash and injective framing. On the client's request the host may supply a fresh contribution before the Actor signs; the client generates its own independent random contribution and never transmits that contribution as such. Bind both contributions, the independently selected run and host, the complete ActId I and canonical authored body B, including the deployment retirement field and requested projection states. network-v0.37.4, premise{``def:graph:author-commitment-preparation``; I and the Actor-chosen B exist before the commitments.}, supplies that pre-nonce context: neither P, hY, C_Y, seal, epoch nor logical time can be an input to this derivation. I and B bind the submission's author, slot and authored meaning; fresh contributions distinguish attempts at the same slot, without inventing a proposal identifier that would depend on the result. The existing author-sequence prefix remains outside the derived random suffix. N_D is independently client-generated and never uses either contribution or N_Y. Under a reviewed hash-combiner and private-entropy assumption, one adequate independent contribution suffices against an observer missing it; this is a proposed hiding argument, not evidence that either generator is adequate. Plain exclusive-or is set aside because a reflecting client cancels the host value. Host key agreement is set aside because one weak private key breaks that construction and gives the host every derived nonce. The optional exchange costs one round before signing and makes that submission wait on the host; a client that trusts its own generator owes neither. A carrier cannot tell from the nonce whether the option was used, so such a requirement would not be checkable. It alone gives the host no derived nonce: the client's contribution is not sent. If the client subsequently sends the content pair, the host receives N_Y in that pair and checks, custodies and removes it as carrier; when the pair is absent the host never holds N_Y. The option requires that no retained host contribution can reconstruct a removed nonce. The host forgets its contribution immediately after sending it on either path: keeping it would permit a search over a weak client's contribution after removal. That erasure does not erase a separately received N_Y, which remains under carriage custody until removal. The host contribution protects only against parties that did not observe it; an unprotected path gains no protection from that contribution, although later record holders may. With a failed client generator the entropy is the host's alone and concealment rests on the host, without claiming implementation of the paper's operator-generated case. Host or path copies are not erased by an application request. Admission is held under (`gate:identity:implementation`), (`req:identity:admission-record`) and (`amend:identity:hiding-property`): owner identity/profile implementers must supply the distinct semantic digest recipe, exact domain and framing, output form, entropy floors, failure and retry rules, hidden-field declaration, independent vectors and hiding review for the public pre-digest and contribution views before the code cut may enable it. Hidden fields are the client contribution and derived nonce, and the host contribution against later record holders after erasure; public context and deliberately disclosed values are excluded. The generation recipe is a new identity crossing the client/host exchange boundary, not one of the historical reference's five commitment tags; no recipe byte is assigned here. The local option additionally requires custody and failure controls, including contribution retention, cancellation, reuse and accidental dependency-nonce reuse. Client generation under (`rule:devnet:content-nonce`) remains the profile's rule. Under this local option, nonce generation and custody stay with the client: the client computes N_Y and alone holds it until choosing to transfer its content pair. The host supplies only auxiliary input and forgets it; that contribution does not give the host custody of N_Y. Recipe admission enables a repository implementation of the option and never makes its use mandatory. (`amend:identity:act-concealment`) retains the local entropy and custody premise. The optional derivation retains the client nonce form and entropy obligation of (`rule:devnet:content-nonce`); it does not replace client generation. The current paper requires no host entropy or seal in the complete act; the successor removes that construction under a new admitted form, while historical host values and the local production duties of (`amend:identity:act-concealment`) remain unchanged. The act-and-epoch document concept (`spec:actepoch:concept`) decides the designed and unbuilt successor for the document subjects stated here; this statement retains its landed or admitted rule until its owner admits the successor document cut, and current fixtures witness only their existing scope.

## Chain payload admission holds · `sec:identity:chain-payloads`

**Decision (Actor document name)** · `dec:identity:actor-document-name` The designed successor has the chain name the token and the epoch name the Actor. The successor burn carries the raw asset identifier under (`conv:devnet:wire-actor`), while an act's author and an epoch frame's Actor name are the 32-byte hash of the Actor's actor document. That document holds the asset identifier and a public 32-byte blinder, varied until the hash carries the three-byte prefix assigned to the document's namespace and version, so a name carries its kind under the layer boundary of (`rule:identity:actor-document-hiding`). The actor document is an ordinary document with a full namespace and no byte budget; it never goes on the chain and is no member of an epoch's committed set. The Actor gives its document to its host for publication as public oracle-side material, including the blinder; within a network the oracle accepts one actor document for a token at any time; a change of that document is the oracle's, through a flow it admits, with the earlier name retained beside the new under (`amend:identity:actor-document-succession`), and no such flow is admitted yet. The table from prefix to namespace and version is recorded with that document's design and stated in no epoch document, so an epoch's static check reads a name as opaque. The host publishes the exact document bytes this host's receipt accepted, by name through (`conv:actepoch:interface`) before an external successor act or frame uses that name. Publication means that a third party can retrieve those bytes from the announced interface and recompute the name; a private cache entry or a published schema alone is insufficient. The document supplies the oracle's explicit token binding under (`rule:identity:actor-document-hiding`). Neither the public-chain asset nor the public blinder is a secret under (`rule:observe:privacy`); the blinder is neither nonce material nor a blinding factor under (`rule:crypto:secret-integration`), but a public search variable. No observer is promised ignorance of the binding. The blinder is retained as the variable of prefix search; it is not a confidentiality mechanism, and the layer boundary is (`rule:identity:actor-document-hiding`). This keeps the raw asset identifier out of the Actor names that acts and frames carry and makes those names document hashes; a later actor-document version changes the document and its prefix without changing a carried name's shape. The test namespace and theory design, candidate exact-byte recipe and prefix assignment are stated in (`schema:identity:actor-document`), (`def:identity:actor-document-recipe`) and (`tab:identity:actor-prefixes`); production has only a reserved prefix under (`open:identity:actor-document-namespaces`). The context policy is (`rule:identity:actor-document-context`), subject to (`assum:identity:actor-chain-separation`), with no signed network or run context. Registry publication, recipe and operation admission and independent evidence remain (`open:identity:actor-document-admission`), with the burn-to-name binding and run-to-lineage question under (`open:identity:chain-actor-binding`). This decision and the representation design are designed and unbuilt; no implemented identity or admitted digest recipe follows.

**Table (Chain payload dossiers)** · `tab:identity:chain-payload-dossiers` The five classes below are held admission dossiers under (`req:identity:admission-record`) and (`gate:identity:implementation`), not accepted recipes or published theories. The family design is (`conv:devnet:wire-boundary`); existing asset and signing-key recipes retain their own admissions. The existing split in (`amend:decomposition:act-authentication`) places public fixed-size carriers, strict qualification and key scripts, the signing-key hash and initial-asset derivation in pn-act-authentication, decoded-transaction interpretation in pn-wallet, and canonical encoding in pn-interchange; (`amend:decomposition:infrastructure`) keeps chain integration and wallet effects in pn-wallet. This split extends to the successors of those responsibilities, the key document and strict eligibility, without a new package or edge admission; it does not admit their successor operations. The successor producer and consumer operations, coordinated implementation change or deadline-bound migration, and independent evidence still must be stated before completion; the remaining burn-reader placement is (`open:identity:chain-payload-owner`). None of these rows admits a digest merely to detect changes; none has a hidden field or hiding claim.

| Class | Designed object, recipe and consumer decision | Assurance, stale conditions, migration and non-claims | Hold and owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Burn document | Exact canonical three-key value and minimal push of (`def:devnet:wire-burn`), with its output amount and selected asset; pn-wallet constructs the transaction under (`rule:harness:burn-targets`) and (`amend:decomposition:infrastructure`); the bounded burn reader validates and attributes only a recognized burn | Exact artifact comparison without a new digest; wrong coordinate, 32-byte asset identifier at position 2, push or output contract gives no effect, incomplete evidence is unavailable; no old-target conversion, syntax proves neither chain truth nor Actor authority | Held: identity and development-profile owners owe the burn-reader placement, exact successor producer and consumer and their coordinated cut under (`open:identity:chain-payload-owner`), with theory and independent vectors |
| Key document | Exact (`def:devnet:wire-key`) and zero fee-asset output/null nonce; (`amend:identity:signing-key-hash`) unchanged; the carrier and hash fall to pn-act-authentication by the extended split under (`amend:decomposition:act-authentication`), pn-wallet constructs and interprets the decoded transaction, and the authority reader tests counted-window membership | Existing artifact-binding assumptions; wrong form gives no effect, unsupported path or uncounted declaration grants no key; no old PNAK conversion, no possession or Actor-identity proof | Held: identity and development-profile owners owe exact successor producer/consumer operations, their coordinated change or deadline-bound migration, the published theory, recipe identifier and byte orders, and independent carrier vectors; package ownership is settled by the existing split, but the old digest admission alone does not admit this new carrier |
| Single-unit eligibility | Public issuance, unit and supported-path object of (`rule:devnet:wire-token`), using (`amend:identity:author-asset-recipe`) without change; issuer produces; strict eligibility and derivation fall to pn-act-authentication by the extended split under (`amend:decomposition:act-authentication`), while pn-wallet interprets the decoded transaction and supplied token prevouts | Existing artifact binding with explicit chain-validity premises; contradictory evidence refuses and incomplete evidence is unavailable; an old asset can be independently eligible without importing old authority; no human uniqueness, private custody or value assurance | Held: identity and development-profile owners owe exact successor producer/consumer operations and consuming decisions, their coordinated change or deadline-bound migration, explicit recipe identifiers and byte orders, and retained-check and removed-mark evidence; the existing split settles package responsibilities without admitting new operations |
| Burn asset identifier in context | (`conv:devnet:wire-actor`) assigns the burn's asset identifier only; admission of the actor-document name in acts and frames under (`dec:identity:actor-document-name`) and the link to that identifier are dependencies owed under (`open:identity:chain-actor-binding`), not an actor-document dossier here | Missing or contradictory context cannot authorize reuse; no shortening of an existing signed identity and no global uniqueness inferred from a bare asset identifier | Held with identity, act-and-epoch document and development-profile owners under (`open:identity:chain-actor-binding`) |
| Marker document/reference | (`def:devnet:wire-marker`) designs the literal 32-byte accepted root at position 2 and 32 zero bytes at that position at the authenticated origin; major 1 forbids that zero value at every following place; (`arg:devnet:wire-budget`) derives the 51–59-byte canonical document bound and 53–61-byte script bound | No stale rule or binding strength follows from the 32 bytes at position 2 or their length; the candidate exact-byte root recipe is (`rule:identity:common-root`), with lookup and admission evidence still owed; a zero-root epoch cannot be accepted by a major-1 marker; no migration or truncated reference | Pure construction, reading and supplied-fact assessment admitted as design under (`amend:identity:marker-static-admission`); MD1/MD2 implementation and all external lookup, compatibility, movement and coordinated enablement evidence remain (`open:identity:chain-marker-admission`) |

The following ordered evidence is the design's minimal focused selection, not a claim that a control exists; each completed dossier also records every byte order and exact recipe identifier under (`req:identity:admission-record`).

1. The shared canonical producer and refusing decoder through pn-interchange need published burn/key theories and independent vectors at patch zero and the largest patch, including the worked example, trailing bytes, duplicate keys, noncanonical heads, wrong length of the byte string at position 2, values over 80 bytes, unknown namespaces, unheld majors, unassigned minors and higher-minor unknown keys granting nothing; push evidence covers minimal pushes, the 76–80-byte PUSHDATA1 boundary, trailing opcodes and wrong output asset, amount or nonce.
2. The split eligibility and transaction reader needs initial issuance, one explicit base unit, absent reissuance capability, multiple issuances, confidential amounts as unproved, marked and unmarked existing assets, unchanged derivation vectors, supported paths and their permanent end, and complete-evidence limits returning unavailable.
3. Marker-asset exclusion evidence establishes that the selected network's marker token is no Actor, a key output in its marker transaction gives it no Actor keys, and a burn document naming its asset is attributed to no Actor; an eligible co-moving token retains its own declaration effect.
4. Key interpretation needs invalid-output isolation, declarations shared by eligible issued or co-moving tokens, zero declarations, charged duplicates, unchanged signing-key-hash vectors and refusal to read PNAK1 as a new key document.
5. Actor consumers need the admitted actor-document name of (`dec:identity:actor-document-name`) and its link to the burn's asset identifier, with oracle context evidence under (`rule:identity:actor-document-context`) and (`assum:identity:actor-chain-separation`): the namespace separates test from production and, within a namespace, the token's chain supplies context, with no signed network or run context. Complete token, lineage and authority evidence and operation admission remain owed under (`open:identity:chain-actor-binding`); a matching identifier or prefix alone proves none of them.
6. Burn extraction needs the output's own amount, distinct outpoints, several burns for one Actor, third-party burns, delayed recognition after an intersection, and no inferred run field; source-alone and join-alone cases establish no inferred protocol effect.
7. Pure marker construction, reading and judgement are admitted under (`amend:identity:marker-static-admission`); real construction and reading of one protocol output wait for the held authenticated-context, lookup, origin and construction admissions and their one-output evidence; continuity evidence across the cuts establishes that PNAT2 and PNAK1 are not family members and that no old signature, settled epoch or fixture is converted.

**Open Question (Burn reader placement)** · `open:identity:chain-payload-owner` Owner: decomposition designers under (`dec:decomposition:crates`), with identity and development-profile designers. Should the successor burn document retain the existing split, with canonical decoding in pn-interchange, key carriers and strict eligibility in pn-act-authentication, and transaction construction and decoded-transaction interpretation in pn-wallet, placing burn extraction beside that interpretation, or should pn-records own the burn carrier and pure lineage-attribution reading under (`rule:lineage:burn-reading`), with pn-wallet supplying chain evidence and pn-host consuming the result; the latter candidates follow the ledger/burn-data responsibility in (`dec:decomposition:crates`) and the pure recognition/host-consumer split of (`amend:decomposition:records-marker-recognition`), not an admission of this successor reader. The reading here favors retaining the existing split for extraction; the placement of complete lineage attribution remains for those designers, because (`amend:decomposition:act-authentication`) does not itself assign that consumer. The wallet is not the owner of the strict key scripts or asset derivation. No new package is proposed, because no part has been shown to require one; a package, edge or widened role cannot be inferred from this question. The burn dossier remains held for this placement and its exact operations, while the key and eligibility dossiers retain the settled owners and only their remaining admission holds.

**Open Question (Actor binding admission)** · `open:identity:chain-actor-binding` Owners: identity, act-and-epoch document and development-profile designers. The successor context policy is (`rule:identity:actor-document-context`): no separately bound network or run enters the document, approval or epoch. The test namespace is assigned by (`schema:identity:actor-document`); production allocation remains (`open:identity:actor-document-namespaces`). Pure recipe and public comparison operations are admitted as design by (`amend:identity:actor-static-admission`); generation, token/document association and application selection of successor consumers still require (`open:identity:actor-document-admission`), including evidence for the oracle reading and the limits of (`assum:identity:actor-chain-separation`). The recorded act-hash message of (`def:actepoch:witnessed-act`) receives no extra context preimage. A replay that reproduces the chain identifiers is the same run replayed, not a newly separated context. No existing signed name, run-qualified recipe or settled carrier is shortened or reinterpreted. Development-profile and lineage designers still owe whether development runs share a network lineage; no one-lineage-per-run rule is inferred from (`def:lineage:lineage`) or (`dec:devnet:elements`). Name-succession authority, later-version representation and frame attribution remain (`open:identity:actor-document-succession`).

**Open Question (Marker payload admission completion)** · `open:identity:chain-marker-admission` Owners: identity and publication designers with act-and-epoch document designers for the root, development-profile, wallet and host designers for construction, and oracle designers for authenticated context. (`amend:identity:marker-static-admission`) admits pure payload construction, reading, direct comparison and judgement over supplied facts as design, with no new marker hash identity; (`tab:identity:marker-operations`), (`rule:identity:marker-read-order`), (`rule:identity:marker-judgement`) and (`amend:decomposition:marker-static`) settle operation ownership, results, precedence and edges. MD1/MD2 of (`tab:identity:marker-cuts`) owe implementation, unchanged theory publication, independent vectors, measured allowances and the controls of (`verif:identity:marker-controls`). Identity, publication and act-and-epoch owners still owe the exact compatibility relation between the epoch’s resolved calculation interpretation and the marker’s version-selected standard, its independently selected inputs, evidence and accepting/refusing operation under (`rule:identity:marker-standard`). Publication and oracle owners retain lookup from the literal 32 bytes to the accepted epoch and its complete evidence, external assurance and stale/context rules. Publication owners also retain third-party retrieval of exact marker payload bytes and accepted-epoch support: a claimed absence needs complete applicable evidence and remains distinct from inability to acquire or interpret it; no service is enabled by theory publication. Publication and identity retain dependency analysis under (`open:lineage:marker-placement`) and selection under (`open:lineage:selection`); development-profile, wallet and host retain construction, custody, authenticated movement and invalid-payload classification under (`open:lineage:realization`); publication and oracle retain actual first-seam comparison under (`open:lineage:origin`). The common root’s semantic recipe and pure-operation admission remain (`schema:identity:common-result`), (`rule:identity:common-root`) and (`amend:identity:common-static-admission`); this design changes none of their bytes. A major-1 following marker cannot accept an all-zero root: following_marker has the pure ZeroFollowingRoot refusal, while the host’s response, recovery and independent integrated refusal evidence remain held here with development-profile and host owners. No rehashing, truncation or alternative recipe is authorized. Reader-relative version errors and supplied-fact zero/movement/root errors follow (`def:lineage:marker-error`), missing evidence remains unavailable, and other invalid payloads remain outside that classification. A local pure Valid result proves no chain premise or completed compatibility. No real marker producer, consumer, publication, oracle recognition or coordinated replacement is enabled by this admission.

## Pure marker payload admission · `sec:identity:marker-static`

**Amendment (Pure marker admission)** · `amend:identity:marker-static-admission` Under (`gate:identity:implementation`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), the fixed payload of (`def:devnet:wire-marker`) is admitted as design for the pure operations of (`tab:identity:marker-operations`), not for a real marker movement, external recognition or publication. Hidden fields: none under (`amend:identity:hiding-property`); all payload members and supplied assessment facts are public. Namespace selects the test document role; version selects its interpretation and the following epoch's reading standard; position 2 is the literal preceding accepted root, or the required origin zeros, never a marker identifier, locator, authority map or proof of acceptance. No observer-ignorance property is claimed. The complete canonical value is compared directly, and the supplied root is compared as 32 exact bytes. Under (`alg:identity:adjudication`), adjudication on 2026-11-26 stops at equality already performed on the path: an additional marker-document hash would decide nothing for these consumers under (`crit:identity:benefit`). This is the no-identity outcome of (`case:identity:no-identity`), recorded under (`req:identity:stop-record`), not a new semantic hash or a waiver of an artifact manifest. Revisit only for an independently named consumer that cannot use those comparisons. The namespace/version coordinate names the theory; the chain transaction and handle name an occurrence under (`def:lineage:marker`), whose acquisition remains held.
