No MarkerName digest type, recipe identifier or hash operation is introduced. The epoch-root identity retains its separate semantic admission under (`amend:identity:common-static-admission`); the historical policy identity retains (`amend:identity:marker-policy-admission`).

The pure admission discharges package placement, operation signatures, exact comparisons, refusal precedence, local namespace review and the evidence plan. MD1 and MD2 owe implementation, theory parsing/publication, independent fixtures, measured allowances and source/configuration-matched controls. External assurance, compatibility, lookup, actual movement, first-seam evidence and coordinated application migration remain (`open:identity:chain-marker-admission`). A changed payload, supplied fact or held registry invalidates a cached assessment; no assessment survives that change by default. A changed theory or reading standard obeys the existing major boundary, never reinterprets an old payload. Every historical carrier and signed identity keeps its meaning under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`) and (`conv:actepoch:history`).

**Table (Marker pure operations)** · `tab:identity:marker-operations` The separate successor_marker module in pn-records owns MarkerPayload, DecodedMarker, MarkerPlace, MarkerFacts, MarkerRead and MarkerJudgement. MarkerPayload has a full unsigned version triple and one 32-byte value; its namespace is the fixed designed literal. Its admitted constructed version is major 1, minor 0 with caller-supplied unsigned patch, preserving every patch accepted by the designed theory. DecodedMarker is a separate read-only major-1 view that retains any accepted stamped minor and patch; it is not an input to encode_marker, so tolerated content cannot be lost by an implicit conversion. The constructor never infers place from value. pn-epoch owns the successor_marker canonical adapter, using pn-interchange's sole codec and registry. Neither module owns a chain, store, host callback, secret or live lookup. Public operations below return the stated value or refusal, and no generic signer, digest or accepted-epoch resolver is implied.

| Owner and operation | Inputs | Result and refusal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| records::successor_marker::first_marker | patch: u64 | MarkerPayload at [1, 0, patch] with zero value; infallible fixed storage; does not prove origin |
| records::successor_marker::following_marker | root: 32-byte word, patch: u64 | MarkerPayload at [1, 0, patch], or ZeroFollowingRoot; this local construction refusal is not an observed chain error |
| records::successor_marker::same_marker | two MarkerPayload values | Boolean exact version/value equality, not occurrence equality; different patches may have the same interpretation but different bytes |
| records::successor_marker::compare_marker_root | decoded major-1 payload, separately supplied 32-byte root | Equal or Different, exact byte comparison without lookup, hashing or chain byte-order conversion |
| records::successor_marker::marker_standard | decoded supported major-1 payload | MarkerStandard(namespace o.pn.t.m, major 1); minor and patch do not change this coordinate; no compatibility verdict |
| records::successor_marker::assess_marker | MarkerRead and MarkerFacts under (`rule:identity:marker-judgement`) | Result of MarkerJudgement or MarkerBoundary, exactly as specified there; no external evidence is acquired |
| epoch::successor_marker::registry | no caller input | Held registry acquired from the fixed published initial theory, or RegistryDefinition for invalid owned theory material; no allocation-budget argument, recoverable allocation refusal or fallback definition |
| epoch::successor_marker::encode_marker | constructed MarkerPayload, MarkerLimits | exact canonical bytes or UnavailableBudget(reason, usage); no namespace/version restamping |
| epoch::successor_marker::read_marker | received bytes, supplied held registry, MarkerLimits | MarkerRead under (`rule:identity:marker-read-order`); full strict validation at a held coordinate and tolerant validation above its floor remain distinct |

The adapter returns borrowed canonical bytes with a successful read, retaining the original stamped version and any tolerated content without repairing or re-encoding it. The records-owned MarkerRead is the fixed assessment view; the epoch adapter returns MarkerRead with an optional borrowed byte slice, present only for a successful read. MarkerRead is Decoded(DecodedMarker, Strict or Tolerant), VersionRejected(UnheldMajor or UnassignedMinor, stamped version), UnsupportedMeaning(stamped version, strict/tolerant acceptance), Invalid(reason), NotMarker, or UnavailableBudget(reason, usage). Decoded is available only for major 1 after theory validation; a held later major may pass interchange without granting a major-1 payload interpretation. No constructor for an arbitrary validated payload bypasses read_marker. Constructors and the decoder are the two producer routes; exact comparison is their consumer within the same implementation series.

**Rule (Marker byte reading order)** · `rule:identity:marker-read-order` The marker follows the canonical, header, selection and selected-shape stages of (`rule:identity:document-family-order`), with the explicit chain-payload differences of (`def:devnet:wire-marker`): no application patch-zero refusal, no refusal merely for a successful higher-minor floor read, and the protocol value-length ceiling before canonical validation. A private sibling-style operation-version gate would turn a tolerantly accepted marker into the version error the profile expressly excludes, so it is not used. An encoder accepts only constructed [1, 0, patch] values, while the reader retains the supplied stamp. The 80-byte wire limit is the existing protocol bound of (`conv:devnet:wire-size`), not a measured resource default.

First compare received length with 80: excess is Invalid(PayloadLength); then compare input_bytes: excess is UnavailableBudget(InputBytes, usage). Validate one complete canonical item with the existing codec, no repair or shared reservation callback. DecodeError::CanonicalLimit maps to UnavailableBudget(CanonicalAllocation, usage); DecodeError::CanonicalAllocation maps to UnavailableBudget(Allocation, usage); CanonicalReservation cannot arise; all other canonical failures, including trailing bytes, map to Invalid(Canonical). The records-owned InvalidReason is PayloadLength, Canonical, Envelope or Theory; codec diagnostics remain with the epoch adapter and are not a records dependency or part of judgement precedence. Next require the base envelope: map, correctly typed keys 0 and 1, a namespace label and three unsigned version integers, otherwise Invalid(Envelope). Content-key checking is not part of that header stage. A different namespace is NotMarker, never an error of this marker theory inferred from a similar spelling. Apply the held registry: no held minor for the marker major yields VersionRejected(UnheldMajor); an absent minor at or below its ceiling yields VersionRejected(UnassignedMinor), both before content validation. Rejection::UnheldMajor and Rejection::UnassignedStamp map respectively to those two named version cases, not to malformed input. Otherwise validate the selected strict theory or floor's open companion; Rejection::Unsatisfied maps to Invalid(Theory), not a version rejection; any located diagnostics retain the registry’s ascending-key order in the adapter. For the initial strict theory, the body checks are exact keys 0, 1, 2; byte-string type at 2; then width 32, in that order. Above the initial floor the open companion allows unknown content keys but still requires that same byte-string width. A structurally accepted major other than 1 returns UnsupportedMeaning; a supported major-1 result is Decoded with strict/tolerant mode and the exact patch. Only then materialize fixed typed storage; no variable collection or reservation callback exists. Resource inability in the budgeted canonical validator stops at that point rather than pretending it inspected the input. Document decoding and theory evaluation have no allocation-budget hook; their bounded-input treatment is (`tab:identity:marker-limits`), not an additional UnavailableBudget outcome.

Encoding computes checked output length first, then checks output_bytes, then the canonical encoder's accounted allocation: arithmetic overflow is UnavailableBudget(Representation, usage), an output declaration is OutputBytes, codec Limit/Length is CanonicalAllocation and allocation failure is Allocation. For the designed major/minor, length is 50 + P, where P is the canonical unsigned patch width: 51 at patch zero and 59 at the largest patch, as (`arg:devnet:wire-budget`) derives. No fixed patch-zero total is imposed on other patches. Comparing fixed words and assessing fixed facts need no heap allocation. All operational inability remains different from a demonstrated invalid value or a lineage error.

**Rule (Marker judgement over supplied facts)** · `rule:identity:marker-judgement` MarkerFacts contains marker_movement: Established or Unknown; place: AuthenticatedOrigin, AuthenticatedFollowing or Unknown; movement: Satisfied, Breached or Unknown; and preceding_root: Known(32-byte word) or Unknown. Established means the caller supplies the selected marker-token context; it is not proved by a payload. Satisfied/Breached are the supplied result of the applicable movement rule, including descent from the preceding handle at a following place; the pure function checks no transaction. These closed enums cannot carry simultaneous contradictory values. Facts from different histories or selected contexts are not a valid caller premise; the external evidence producer owns that correspondence under (`open:lineage:realization`). The result is Result<MarkerJudgement, MarkerBoundary>: the judgement has exactly Error(established reasons), Unavailable(missing reasons) and Valid; the outer boundary prevents unassigned invalid-payload policy from becoming a fourth lineage verdict. Error reasons are a fixed record of six boolean flags in the order below; missing reasons are MarkerContext, ReadBudget(reason, usage), Interpretation or a fixed record of Place, Movement and PrecedingRoot flags. The result keeps all established errors or all applicable missing facts in that order, not allocated vectors or whichever fact the implementation happens to encounter first. MarkerBoundary is UnclassifiedPayload(InvalidReason or NotMarker); no interchange type crosses into pn-records. A consumer must carry UnclassifiedPayload unchanged, mapping it to neither Error, Unavailable nor Valid, until (`open:lineage:realization`) decides it.

If marker_movement is Unknown, return Unavailable(MarkerContext) without attributing any payload or movement failure to a marker. Otherwise collect every established error in order: MovementBreach, UnheldMajor, UnassignedMinor, OriginNonzero, FollowingZero, RootMismatch. The first is supplied movement Breached. The two version cases come only from VersionRejected and are relative to the supplied reader state. OriginNonzero requires Decoded and AuthenticatedOrigin with nonzero value. FollowingZero requires Decoded at a proved following place in major 1 and needs no lookup. RootMismatch requires Decoded at that following place, nonzero value and a separately known unequal preceding accepted root. Zero is handled by FollowingZero alone even when a supplied root is also unequal. Any nonempty error set returns Error before missing or unusable evidence about another requirement; manual intervention with no predefined recovery remains (`rule:lineage:halt`). Several errors remain one stopped marker with all independently established reasons.

With no established error, Invalid or NotMarker returns MarkerBoundary::UnclassifiedPayload(reason), retaining (`open:lineage:realization`) rather than deciding whether malformed data on an authenticated marker-token movement is an error. UnavailableBudget returns Unavailable(ReadBudget(reason, usage)); UnsupportedMeaning returns Unavailable(Interpretation). With Decoded, collect missing reasons in order Place, Movement, PrecedingRoot: unknown place, unknown movement, and unknown preceding root only at a following nonzero place. Unknown place does not presume a lookup duty for an origin. An empty missing set is Valid, conditional on the supplied facts and only for the applicable version, movement and value rules; it establishes neither their authenticity nor the following epoch's compatibility. Thus a zero at unknown place is Unavailable, not an origin; nonzero at authenticated origin and zero at proved following place are errors even without lookup; a following matching root with unknown movement is Unavailable, not valid. A successful higher-minor floor read is not a version error; an unassigned minor and unheld major are errors even with missing history. No payload repair, predecessor-standard fallback, skipping or recovery is supplied.

**Rule (Marker standard and compatibility boundary)** · `rule:identity:marker-standard` MarkerStandard is the document's namespace/major coordinate, not a new serialized object, hash or exact compatibility predicate. It denotes the standard for the epoch following that marker, unchanged over minors and patches under (`def:devnet:wire-marker`). Position 2 accepts the preceding epoch, which was judged under the predecessor marker's standard, not this one. The common root reader resolves an internal calculation interpretation, but the records supply no mapping from every resolved interpretation to the marker's required static-verdict version. Identity, act-and-epoch and publication designers retain that exact compatibility relation, its independently selected inputs, accepting/refusing operation and evidence under (`open:identity:chain-marker-admission`). No equality of arbitrary version triples, comparison with the tree's paper tag or automatic compatibility of major 1 is substituted. MD1/MD2 can return the standard coordinate and compare a supplied root without admitting the predicate. A later oracle authority reader may consume that coordinate and the separately established marker place as interface data; no authority window, key selection or authority result is encoded or decided here. The authority design states any further need; this admission offers only the coordinate and the place. Their authenticated acquisition and joining consumers require their own admission.

**Table (Marker resource declarations)** · `tab:identity:marker-limits` MarkerLimits declares finite machine-size input_bytes, output_bytes and canonical_allocation_bytes, each mandatory and with no default. The protocol's 80-byte ceiling precedes input_bytes. The canonical allocation declaration covers only requests charged by the existing borrowed canonical validator and budgeted encoder, including any adapter construction explicitly performed through the encoder's admitted budgeted construction operations; it is not a heap-wide allowance. Canonical validation uses the existing CanonicalUsage and encoding uses EncodingUsage, retaining their named causes and charged usage. Registry parsing and acquisition consume the fixed embedded initial theory and its derived open companion, with no caller-supplied theory text; document materialization and theory evaluation consume at most 80 bytes. These paths are bounded by that fixed theory and input, not metered by a registry or validator allocation declaration: Theory::parse, Registry::acquire, Document::from_canonical_bytes and accept provide no such budget or reservation hook. No recoverable allocation failure is promised for those unbudgeted paths, and no synthetic charge is attributed to them. Another held theory's resource treatment belongs to its publication admission; private version fixtures are finite test material, not an admission of arbitrary caller theories. An invalid embedded theory is RegistryDefinition, never a marker error. The fixed carrier, comparison and judgement have no list ceiling or trial limit. Epoch implementers measure the budgeted codec's charged usage and separately report the fixed theory's parse/acquisition and the bounded materialization/evaluation cost, own CPU, peak memory, wall and load under (`rule:devperf:measurement`), using the 51/59-byte valid forms, the 80-byte tolerant boundary and deep malformed data. Only a charged codec usage supplies minus-one, equal and plus-one allowance operands where representable; allocation-failure injection is confined to those budgeted requests. No service default or measured value is chosen. Budgeted parse, acquisition and evaluation would require a separate interchange admission; that dependency is not required for this fixed-source, protocol-bounded cut because it would add infrastructure without changing these pure marker decisions.

**Rule (Marker theory publication)** · `rule:identity:marker-publication` MD2 publishes the complete marker-only theory source of (`conv:devnet:wire-envelope`), with the patch position written uint under (`alg:xchg:fragment-check`), in packages/epoch/schema/marker-document.cddl. epoch::successor_marker::registry parses that embedded source and acquires namespace o.pn.t.m at major 1, minor 0 through pn-interchange's existing Registry API, following (`rule:identity:common-publication`); no parallel parser, registry format or code-string theory is introduced. marker-registry compares the parsed file's namespace and coordinate and normalized Theory::to_cddl output with the operation's acquired theory. The patch remains every unsigned 64-bit value. The test namespace is already reserved under (`conv:design:chain-test-reservation`), not newly allocated by admission.

The local name review compares the short family o.pn.t.b, o.pn.t.k and o.pn.t.m, differing in their final whole atom, and all assigned names listed in (`rule:identity:actor-publication`), including the common act, witness, epoch, acts and root, Actor, frame, entering state and selected evidence. Those full names begin with the distinct atom org rather than o. Names beneath org.peer-network.test include the historical publication and verified-act carriers; the historical marker-policy recipe pn-records/marker-policy/v1 is a recipe identifier, not a competing namespace or successor recipe. All are exact lowercase ASCII atom sequences; no normalization or textual-prefix match is admitted. This completes comparison of these recorded assignments, not worldwide uniqueness or production allocation. Builder parsing/acquisition and exact correspondence evidence remain owed; no runtime registry artifact is claimed by this documents commit.

**Rule (Marker independent operands)** · `rule:identity:marker-vectors` MD1 records the typed fact cases below in packages/records/tests/successor_marker.rs with literal expected enums, independent of assess_marker. MD2 owns packages/epoch/tests/marker-documents.json and packages/epoch/tests/marker_vectors.py: a Python standard-library-only independent canonical encoder and an explicit expected-result table, importing neither production code nor pn-interchange, produces the expected bytes from these descriptors; Rust construction/encoding is the second route and the strict reader is the comparison consumer. No digest, signer, key, token movement or chain is needed. Let Z be 32 zero bytes and R have byte i at index i for i from 0 through 31. The first payload F is namespace o.pn.t.m, version [1, 0, 0], value Z; the following payload N differs only by value R. Patch neighbours replace only patch by 1, 24 and the largest u64; all are accepted and retain their stamp. Reversing R is a different value, never a byte-order repair. Expected bytes are produced and independently reproduced only in the builder cut; no vector bytes are assigned here.

Every malformed neighbour starts from N unless stated: remove key 0 for Envelope; change version to a two-element array for Envelope; change namespace to o.pn.t.k for NotMarker; remove key 2 for Theory; add key 3 with unsigned zero for strict Theory; replace position 2 by unsigned zero for Theory; shorten R by its last byte for Theory; encode the map head with nonpreferred one-byte argument for Canonical; append unsigned zero for trailing Canonical. The length neighbour is F followed by unsigned-zero items to total 81 bytes, yielding PayloadLength before trailing Canonical; pair that same 81-byte neighbour with input_bytes 51 to expect Invalid(PayloadLength) before InputBytes inability, at the marker-read-precedence site. The 80-byte neighbour is the same construction stopped at 80, yielding Canonical when input_bytes permits it. The deep-shape neighbour replaces only N’s position 2 by sixty nested singleton arrays around unsigned zero; it is canonical within the protocol bound and yields Theory after successful canonical validation. Resource measurements use F, N, the largest-patch N, that deep-shape neighbour and the 80-byte trailing neighbour; only usage charged by the budgeted canonical read or encoder supplies its minus-one, equal and plus-one allowance cases, with all other declarations held at their measured sufficient values. Measure parsing/acquisition of the real initial embedded theory and bounded document materialization/evaluation separately, without fabricated allowance neighbours or injected allocation failures for APIs that do not expose them. Read resource cases use that acquired registry. At marker-resources, inject a failing admitted allocation request separately in the budgeted canonical validator and budgeted encoder, with their other declarations sufficient; registry acquisition, unbudgeted document materialization and theory evaluation are not injection sites. The normal registry holds only the real initial assignment. Change N's major to 2 for UnheldMajor; combine that change with missing key 2 to retain UnheldMajor. Change minor to 1 for tolerant success; add key 3 with unsigned zero to that neighbour for tolerant success; shorten its R for tolerant Theory. Combine wrong namespace with added key 3 for NotMarker and nonpreferred map head with missing key 0 for Canonical. A private registry fixture holds major 1 minors 0 and 2 with identical body theories and pinned matching minor literals, leaving minor 1 absent; N stamped minor 1 then yields UnassignedMinor, including with missing key 2. These test assignments allocate no production theory. A private major-2 fixture with the same body shape makes N stamped major 2 structurally accepted but UnsupportedMeaning, never a decoded major-1 value.

The judgement bases have Established marker context, movement Satisfied and decoded F at AuthenticatedOrigin with preceding_root Unknown, or decoded N at AuthenticatedFollowing with Known(R): both Valid. The payload equality neighbours are F against F, F against N and F against F with only patch changed to 1; the results are true, false and false. Root comparison uses decoded N against R and Z for Equal and Different. Standard comparison uses F, N and each patch neighbour at major 1 for the same MarkerStandard. Change the origin value alone to R for OriginNonzero; change the following value alone to Z for FollowingZero, with preceding_root Unknown to show lookup is unnecessary. Change only Known(R) to Known(Z) with N for RootMismatch. Change only movement to Breached for MovementBreach, to Unknown for Unavailable(Movement), only place to Unknown for Unavailable(Place), and only following preceding_root to Unknown for Unavailable(PrecedingRoot). Use zero with unknown place for Unavailable(Place), not an inferred origin. Substitute each version-rejected read into the following base with all other facts Unknown except Established marker context: the matching version error wins. Substitute tolerant N into the following base for Valid. Substitute invalid Theory into the following base for UnclassifiedPayload; combine it with movement Breached for Error(MovementBreach). Combine FollowingZero with movement Breached and missing root for both errors in declared order. Combine Invalid(Theory) with movement Unknown for UnclassifiedPayload, not a fabricated marker error. Substitute Unknown marker context for Unavailable(MarkerContext), even if supplied payload/version failures exist. Substitute read-budget inability and UnsupportedMeaning separately for their named Unavailable reasons. These operands exercise the policy boundary; they do not authenticate facts or close its unassigned invalid-payload meaning.

**Verification (Pure marker controls)** · `verif:identity:marker-controls` Every row is planned and unexecuted under (`open:identity:chain-marker-admission`); it has one proposition at one owning site under (`rule:testing:site`). Fixed inputs and expected outcomes are (`rule:identity:marker-vectors`); runtime resource neighbours are supplied only after the measurements of (`tab:identity:marker-limits`). No row claims chain evidence, arbitrary histories, completed compatibility or publication enablement.

| Control | Cut and sole site | Proposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| marker-construction | MD1, records tests/successor_marker.rs | First/following constructors enforce the major-1 zero placement policy on supplied intent |
| marker-payload-equality | MD1, records tests/successor_marker.rs | Payload equality retains the whole version and value |
| marker-root-comparison | MD1, records tests/successor_marker.rs | Root comparison is exact word equality |
| marker-error-precedence | MD1, records tests/successor_marker.rs | Every established error and combined-error neighbour wins over missing evidence in declared order |
| marker-unavailable | MD1, records tests/successor_marker.rs | Missing context/place/movement/root and unsupported interpretation produce the specified inability, never origin or validity by default |
| marker-policy-boundary | MD1, records tests/successor_marker.rs | Otherwise invalid payload stays outside the three judgement cases unless an independent error is established |
| marker-standard-coordinate | MD1, records tests/successor_marker.rs | Major selects the successor standard coordinate while minor and patch do not alter it |
| marker-canonical | MD2, epoch tests/marker_documents.rs | Independent F/N/patch bytes round-trip exactly and malformed canonical/body neighbours refuse without repair |
| marker-version-acceptance | MD2, epoch tests/marker_documents.rs | Unheld major, unassigned minor, strict success, tolerant success/failure and unsupported meaning retain their distinct outcomes |
| marker-read-precedence | MD2, epoch tests/marker_documents.rs | Every combined-fault operand has the recorded first cause |
| marker-registry | MD2, epoch tests/marker_documents.rs | Published theory and acquired operation registry correspond exactly |
| marker-resources | MD2, epoch tests/marker_documents.rs | Measured byte and charged-codec allocation neighbours preserve named operational inability, with a failing admitted allocation request injected only in the budgeted canonical validator and encoder at this site |

**Table (Marker implementation cuts)** · `tab:identity:marker-cuts` MD1 and MD2 form one producer/comparison implementation series under (`req:identity:admission-record`); neither may enable an application while the later holds persist. No historical carrier is converted and no new semantic hash is minted.

| Cut and owner | Builds, evidence and purpose | Holds afterwards |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MD1, records | Separate successor_marker typed carriers, first/following constructors, exact comparisons, standard coordinate and assessment over supplied facts; seven focused controls with the fixed typed operands | Codec/publication in MD2; no chain, lookup or oracle recognition |
| MD2, epoch/publication | Canonical adapter, registry/theory source, independent byte corpus, five focused codec controls, measured finite declarations and package/feature evidence under (`amend:decomposition:marker-static`) | Compatibility, authenticated evidence, construction, custody, lookup, first seam and enablement remain held |
| MD3, identity, epoch and publication designers | After the remaining design admission: exact interpretation/standard compatibility operation, accepted-root lookup with complete evidence and dependency analysis | No host response, real movement or authority recognition inferred from a pure result |
| MD4, development-profile, wallet, host and oracle designers | After their own admission: token genesis/custody, one protocol output's supported construction, real movement verification, complete evidence acquisition and first-seam comparison, then coordinated producer/consumer replacement | Production allocation, invalid-payload policy and any undischarged evidence stay with their named owners; manual intervention remains no predefined recovery |

The host response to a zero-root epoch remains part of MD4 under (`open:identity:chain-marker-admission`); following_marker's ZeroFollowingRoot is only the buildable pure refusal. The first seam belongs to (`open:lineage:origin`), dependency and selection to (`open:lineage:marker-placement`) and (`open:lineage:selection`), custody and authenticated context to (`open:lineage:realization`), and burn attribution to (`open:identity:chain-payload-owner`). No obligation of those owners is discharged by finite synthetic facts.

## Actor document representation · `sec:identity:actor-document`

**Amendment (Actor document design boundary)** · `amend:identity:actor-document-design` The representation below designs the naming document of (`dec:identity:actor-document-name`) and (`def:actepoch:actor-document`), not a replacement already admitted for use. It assigns the test namespace and one concrete theory design with a recipe admitted as design for public pure operations by (`amend:identity:actor-static-admission`); registry evidence, honest generation and successor enablement remain held under (`open:identity:actor-document-admission`). Production has only a reserved prefix and no allocated namespace here. No existing token, signed author, frame key, recipe or carrier is converted. The chain payloads of (`conv:devnet:wire-envelope`) and (`conv:devnet:wire-actor`) keep their own owners and bytes. This document never goes on the chain and has no chain-payload byte budget; ordinary representation and caller-resource refusals still apply.

**Schema (Test Actor document theory)** · `schema:identity:actor-document` Assign org.peer-network.test.actor as a repository-owned test namespace beside the reservations of (`amend:design:test-publication-profile`), for major 1 and minor 0 with an unsigned 64-bit patch. The role follows the test designation, as in the test-only family of (`conv:devnet:wire-envelope`). The concrete CDDL below fixes the namespace literal; the body is the ordered pair of the asset identifier and the blinder, each exactly 32 bytes. This assignment selects the test name and theory design, not a published registry object or an implemented identity. The local assigned-name review and publication plan are (`rule:identity:actor-publication`); actual registry entries and implementation evidence remain (`open:identity:actor-document-admission`), and production allocation remains (`open:identity:actor-document-namespaces`). The application uses patch zero under (`rule:identity:actor-document-opening`); the theory and interchange structural acceptance leave patch free.

```cddl
actor-document = { 0: "org.peer-network.test.actor", 1: [1, 0, patch], 2: [asset, blinder] }
patch = 0..18446744073709551615
asset = bstr .size 32
blinder = bstr .size 32
```

This is the three-member map family of (`conv:devnet:wire-envelope`) restricted to this body, with a full namespace rather than a chain-budget abbreviation. The instantiated theory pins keys 0 and 1 and has only content key 2, within (`gram:interchange:assignable-fragment`); patch stays free under (`inv:interchange:patch-identity`). Changing the body type requires a new major under (`law:interchange:major-boundary`), not an extension member or an additive change inside the pair. The concrete theory design has not been parser-checked and is not a published registry object or an independently implemented decoder.

**Rule (Actor document canonical bytes)** · `rule:identity:actor-document-bytes` The name input is the single complete canonical CBOR encoding of that map through pn-interchange under (`lang:interchange:data-language`) and (`req:xchg:determinism`): keys 0, 1 and 2 in encoded order; the namespace as its exact text; the version as a three-element unsigned array; and the body as a two-element array of byte strings. Preferred integer and length heads, definite lengths, unique keys and absence of trailing bytes are required of received bytes, not repaired by re-encoding. The asset bytes are exactly the display-order binary bytes of (`conv:devnet:wire-actor`); no second reversal or textual hexadecimal enters the body. All envelope bytes, patch and both complete byte strings enter the hash. No alternative serialization, Unicode rewriting, signature, external run value, chain framing or OP_RETURN wrapper enters it. The generic reader's higher-minor tolerance under (`def:interchange:open-companion`) remains intact; a tolerant read without the exact known Actor prefix/theory row is insufficient for an Actor association, not a redefinition of interchange acceptance.

**Table (Actor prefix convention)** · `tab:identity:actor-prefixes` The test row assigns the test namespace of (`schema:identity:actor-document`); the real-network prefix of (`conv:actepoch:actor-prefix`) is reserved without namespace text or a concrete theory. The version selected by a row is the theory coordinate (major, minor), shared across its unsigned patches: patch does not define another theory or prefix, although changing its encoded value changes the document and name. This follows (`inv:interchange:patch-identity`) and preserves the full version triple in the hash. A later defining version needs its own distinct assigned row; no allocation rule for subsequent rows is inferred. The table belongs to this design, never to an epoch document, and the three bytes mean only the row they select.

| Prefix, in byte order | Namespace | Theory coordinate | Patch |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 00 00 00 | Assigned: org.peer-network.test.actor | Major 1, minor 0 | Any unsigned 64-bit patch |
| 00 00 01 | Reserved: no namespace text; production allocation under (`open:identity:actor-document-namespaces`) | First real-network version; coordinate unassigned | No production theory assigned |

**Amendment (Actor name adjudication)** · `amend:identity:actor-document-adjudication` Under (`alg:identity:adjudication`), the proposed object is one complete validated naming document, not its asset alone. The receiving host and oracle need to compare a separately received document with the fixed-width name in an authored act or frame; that document is absent from the epoch set, so no parent identity, local typed comparison or standing review supplies the binding across that distribution and signature boundary. A raw asset identifier supplies token equality but neither binds the document and its version nor separates its token denotation from the closure’s naming value. This passes (`crit:identity:benefit`) for a new independently compared naming value, not a no-identity stop or semantic token identity. (`amend:identity:actor-static-admission`) defines the complete canonical naming value as the semantic object and admits its pure comparison operations as design under (`case:identity:artifact`)'s explicit canonical-object clause. The blinder belongs to that naming value; it does not add a fact about the token. No manifest is invented, no artifact-class requirement is waived, and no implementation or oracle acceptance follows before its separate evidence. This is not a generated-file freshness digest.

**Definition (Actor document name recipe)** · `def:identity:actor-document-recipe` The recipe identifier admitted as design for the pure operations of (`amend:identity:actor-static-admission`) is `pn-act-authentication/actor-document/v1`. For a canonical document D of (`schema:identity:actor-document`), let B be its entire received canonical byte sequence; the proposed name is the 32 output bytes of SHA-256(B), in digest byte order, without truncation, reversal, hexadecimal text or a second hash. The prefix test compares its first three bytes with (`tab:identity:actor-prefixes`). Domain separation is the exclusive Actor-document namespace and version encoded inside B, not an additional preamble: no other role may be assigned those namespaces, and a consumer resolves the recipe through its held Actor row. The recipe identifier names this projection and is not another byte string prepended to B. The full namespace and typed envelope already separate the object, while an extra frame would cease to hash the document's exact bytes. SHA-256 uses the existing standalone primitive division of (`rule:crypto:operation-scope`) and implementing line of (`amend:identity:sha-two-line`), not a new library choice. Binding assumes collision and second-preimage resistance for this prefix-constrained domain; fixing an output prefix narrows that domain, so no unrestricted-output collision strength is claimed. Layer separation, with no hidden-field claim, is (`rule:identity:actor-document-hiding`). The test literal is fixed by (`schema:identity:actor-document`); production allocation and the evidence needed for identity admission remain held. This pure-operation admission assigns no instance digest or vector and enables no Actor identity in an application.

**Table (Actor document admission dossier)** · `tab:identity:actor-document-dossier` This dossier under (`req:identity:admission-record`) is admitted as design for public pure operations by (`amend:identity:actor-static-admission`), with implementation evidence and honest generation separately owed. The public fixed-size carrier and pure recipe fall to pn-act-authentication by (`amend:decomposition:act-authentication`) and (`dec:design:authentication-owner`), using pn-interchange for the one codec; the exact pure operations and unchanged edges are (`tab:identity:actor-static-operations`) and (`amend:decomposition:actor-static`); no private operation is enabled.

| Item | Designed contract and remaining admission |
| --- | --- |
| Object and assurance | Exact canonical document including namespace, all version members, asset and blinder; semantic computational equality of the complete naming-document value, not semantic token identity, chain truth, authority or authenticity |
| Producer | Actor through its client; pn-client owns intent and orchestration under (`dec:design:authentication-owner`), while public construction/hash validation uses the shared recipe; ordinary public-material generation and its candidate-source operation remain held under (`open:identity:actor-generation-admission`), not inferred from the public subset |
| Consumers and decisions | Host receipt recomputes the supplied name before retaining and publishing the exact accepted document; oracle associates it with a proven eligible token in the selected network, refuses a competing document outside an admitted succession flow and resolves burn-to-frame names; successor act/frame consumers compare opaque names without opening documents |
| Lifecycle and cut | These are designed consumers, not present operations: the implementation owner must deliver producer, public validation, receipt and oracle association together with the successor act/frame consumers in one coordinated change, with the deadline-bound migration of (`tab:identity:actor-cuts`) completed before external use |
| Staleness | Any byte change, including patch or blinder, changes the identified naming value; any projection, primitive, canonicalization or role change requires a new recipe identifier under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`); a changed prefix table row cannot reinterpret an existing name |
| Migration | No unadmitted regrinding, restamping or namespace rewrite of an accepted token document; (`amend:identity:actor-document-succession`) gives the later-version direction of retaining old and new names together, without assigning a member or flow now; (`open:identity:actor-document-succession`) holds the later version, frame attribution and oracle flow admission; a hash cannot recover a missing document |
| Public and hidden fields | Hidden fields: none. The published document reveals asset, namespace, version and blinder; the name carries prefix class and equality while the closure alone supplies no token-binding operand under (`rule:identity:actor-document-hiding`); no observer secrecy or anonymity is claimed |
| Non-claims | No proof of entropy, uniqueness of a person, asset eligibility, ownership, signing authority, network/run binding, chain finality, availability, deletion or computational hardness from a matching hash or prefix; exact bytes decide detected collisions and idempotence |
| Independent evidence owed | Canonical document/name vectors produced by code and independently reproduced; wrong-coordinate, width, noncanonical and prefix refusals; every member-change check, prefix-constrained binding review, bounded candidate-source and search-stop evidence and the controls of (`verif:identity:actor-document-controls`); no expected value is handwritten here |

**Rule (Opening and validity)** · `rule:identity:actor-document-opening` A receiver applies (`rule:identity:actor-read-order`): complete canonical validation precedes held namespace/theory selection, the selected structural body precedes the application patch, and recomputation precedes prefix and supplied-name comparison. A missing or unknown row makes Actor association unavailable; malformed bytes, wrong shape/width, a mismatching known prefix or expected name refuse that association. No prefix infers the complete namespace without the held table, and no prefix replaces recomputation. Interchange structural acceptance and these application checks are separate. For the test theory at major 1 and minor 0, an Actor producer writes patch 0 and an Actor-association reader requires that application patch after structural acceptance; another structurally valid patch is not admitted for this Actor application. This does not change (`def:interchange:acceptance`) or (`inv:interchange:patch-identity`): all unsigned patches share the same theory, and the CDDL remains unchanged. The application stamp, namespace and theory coordinate are fixed, so the asset and blinder determine the exact canonical document and its name without another unrecorded producer choice; received bytes are never restamped or repaired. The exact public operation order is (`rule:identity:actor-read-order`); token eligibility, selected network, uniqueness and authority remain oracle checks. The epoch static checker instead accepts the supplied 32-byte name as opaque: it neither opens this document nor consults this table, tests a prefix or fetches an asset. Resource limits give an explicit inability rather than a false validity verdict; no protocol byte budget is inferred from local limits.

**Procedure (Blinder search)** · `alg:identity:actor-document-search` The Actor's client orchestrates a bounded search with the asset, test namespace and version [1, 0, 0] fixed under (`rule:identity:actor-document-opening`); only the 32-byte blinder varies, and each candidate is encoded canonically and hashed until the assigned prefix matches. The procedure follows (`rule:identity:actor-document-hiding`) and the candidate-start policy of (`open:identity:actor-generation-admission`). A counter may index the admitted candidate sequence; its exact initialization and expansion remain that admission's duty, with no secrecy or secret-handling owner implied. Candidate repeats waste trials and must not be credited as fresh independent trials in the probability model; there is no privacy-based cross-Actor seed-reuse refusal. Under the independent uniform-hash model, each distinct trial has probability 2^-24 of the assigned three-byte prefix and the expected trial count is its reciprocal; no finite success guarantee or measured performance follows. The prefix test establishes only that prefix, not the origin, randomness or independence of the candidates. Measured cost, finite trial/deadline limits, cancellation, checked resource accounting and explicit exhaustion/source/codec failure remain owed under (`rule:devperf:measurement`). Failed or cancelled searches publish no completed Actor name, while an idempotent receipt retry reuses the completed document. The pure public search_step remains in authentication; client orchestration and an ordinary candidate-source operation are a separate held AD3 cut under (`open:identity:actor-generation-admission`), with no secret-home consumer proposed.

**Rule (Actor naming layer separation)** · `rule:identity:actor-document-hiding` Hidden fields: none under (`amend:identity:hiding-property`); the complete Actor document, including its blinder and asset, is public and published under (`dec:identity:actor-document-name`). No operand of the closure binds a name to a token, and no static check computes or uses that binding; other public data, such as gross amounts against burns, may still link them. This is an input boundary, not computational secrecy or a prohibition on deriving a binding from other public data. Candidate-start policy is (`open:identity:actor-generation-admission`); no entropy floor or hidden generator state is claimed. Fresh candidate variation serves prefix search, not concealment, and its probability account must not count repeated candidates as independent attempts. Different assets remain distinct members even if a candidate blinder is reused; the oracle's one accepted association and exact-byte collision checks, not seed secrecy, resolve competing documents. No ban on logging or distributing public candidates, no erasure promise, secret custody or confidentiality cleanup duty is imposed by this rule. Ordinary resource release, bounded search and source-failure handling remain operational duties. This classification changes no observation operation or recorded field under (`rule:observe:privacy`). The prefix test cannot certify honest generation; binding still rests on the recipe's prefix-constrained collision and second-preimage assumptions, whose review remains owed. Fixed vectors establish neither those assumptions nor oracle acceptance.

**Rule (Receipt and one accepted document per token)** · `rule:identity:actor-document-receipt` The host treats a newly offered document as untrusted, applies (`rule:identity:actor-document-opening`), and asks the oracle to establish eligible token identity and the network-scoped accepted association. The oracle uses complete association evidence for that selected network: equal asset and identical document is an idempotent retry; equal asset and any different complete document offered outside a succession flow the oracle admits is refused, including a different blinder, patch, version or namespace; equal name with different bytes is a collision/conflict refusal, never an equality shortcut. A host's not having seen an asset is not evidence that the network has no association. Missing authoritative association evidence is unavailable, not permission to install a competing name. Before accepting a new association the owning design must establish who can authorize it from the token and how the network's one accepted association is retained and recovered; durable compare-and-install must preserve the same answer across concurrent offers and crashes. This is oracle-side state/evidence outside every epoch root, not a new committed map or a first-writer-wins authority rule. The concrete admission and reconciliation of that evidence remain (`open:identity:actor-document-admission`). A different document for an accepted asset is accepted only through such a flow, which replaces the one accepted association and retains the earlier name beside the new under (`amend:identity:actor-document-succession`); no flow is designed or admitted here, so every such offer is refused now, and the flows remain (`open:identity:actor-document-succession`).

**Rule (Burn to Actor attribution)** · `rule:identity:actor-document-attribution` The oracle retains the eligibility and ancestry rules of (`rule:devnet:wire-token`) and (`rule:lineage:burn-reading`), including exclusion of the network's marker asset. It reads the burn's raw display-order asset bytes under (`conv:devnet:wire-actor`), opens the accepted Actor document, compares those asset bytes exactly and credits the lineage-recognized gross amount to that document's recomputed name. The namespace distinguishes test from production; within a namespace the token's chain supplies the context under (`rule:identity:actor-document-context`) and (`assum:identity:actor-chain-separation`). No extra network/run field or approval context supplies that distinction. Complete chain evidence with no such token supplies neither burn attribution nor an authorized key for that Actor; incomplete chain evidence is unavailable, not proof of foreignness or a zero amount. A missing document or ambiguous/conflicting association likewise makes attribution unavailable or refused, never another Actor's amount. No amount, chain proof or key map is added to the Actor document or epoch. Extraction and complete lineage-attribution placement stay with (`open:identity:chain-payload-owner`); this design does not reassign another package's reader.

**Rule (Successor name consumers)** · `rule:identity:actor-document-consumers` The successor act author and frame key carry exactly the recipe's 32 name bytes under (`def:actepoch:act`) and (`def:actepoch:frame`). Equality and ordering use all bytes, bytewise lexicographically; the prefix may group names by their assigned kind but never replaces the remaining bytes in comparison. Equal-width CBOR byte-string keys therefore have the same order under (`lang:interchange:data-language`); duplicate frame names remain invalid. Static validation reads no prefix or Actor opening and no Actor-to-key relation; the witnessed act records its verification key, and oracle authority uses the selected network's counted declarations under (`rule:lineage:authority`). No existing pn-devnet-v1/run/target, signing key, approval preimage or account identity is shortened or relabelled. The successor uses no separately signed network/run context under (`rule:identity:actor-document-context`); (`open:identity:chain-actor-binding`) retains operation admission and evidence, not the alternative context policy. A later succession carries the old and new names as names of unchanged width under (`amend:identity:actor-document-succession`); neither their pairing nor a new Actor-document member is assigned here.

**Verification (Actor document controls)** · `verif:identity:actor-document-controls` These controls are planned and unexecuted, one proposition at one owning site under (`rule:testing:site`); cuts and exact operands are (`tab:identity:actor-cuts`) and (`rule:identity:actor-publication`). Actual assertions and independent evidence must be registered before an executed claim. Existing fixtures witness only their admitted editions. Later authority, generation and integration controls cannot be claimed from pure byte examples.

| Control | Cut and sole site | Proposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| actor-document-canonical | AD1, packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | Valid public values round-trip through their exact canonical bytes |
| actor-document-malformed | AD1, packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | Each isolated malformed operand receives its named refusal |
| actor-document-read-order | AD1, packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | Combined defects receive the first applicable refusal in the declared order |
| actor-document-name | AD1, packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | The recipe agrees with independent complete-preimage/name vectors |
| actor-document-comparison | AD1, packages/act-authentication/tests/public.rs | Supplied-name comparison recomputes the name |
| actor-document-version | AD1, packages/act-authentication/tests/public.rs | Strict application selection preserves the separate generic patch and minor verdicts |
| actor-document-registry | AD1, packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | Published assigned theory and the code registry correspond |
| actor-document-api | AD1, packages/act-authentication/src/actor.rs source review | The public surface is confined to the admitted operation table; no executed absence claim from examples |
| actor-document-search-step | AD1, packages/act-authentication/tests/public.rs | Each supplied public candidate is classified by its recomputed prefix |
| actor-document-prefix | AD2, packages/act-authentication/tests/public.rs | Opening requires the assigned prefix of the recomputed name |
| actor-document-opening-name | AD2, packages/act-authentication/tests/public.rs | Opening compares the supplied expected name after prefix validation |
| actor-document-network | AD2, packages/act-authentication/tests/public.rs | A reserved production prefix supplies no held opening theory |
| actor-document-generation-stop | AD3, pn-client actor-generation operation tests, concrete path fixed by its operation admission | Cancellation, exhaustion or source/codec failure yields no completed document |
| actor-document-unique-token | AD4, packages/host/src/storage/tests.rs | Atomic accepted association preserves one document per token across retry and recovery outside a succession flow |
| actor-document-oracle-context | AD4, oracle adapter site fixed by (`open:identity:chain-payload-owner`) | Only complete chain evidence can establish absence of an eligible token |
| actor-document-replay | AD4, oracle adapter site fixed by (`open:identity:chain-payload-owner`) | Reproduced issuance is treated as replay under the recorded context policy |
| actor-document-burn-name | AD4, burn-reader site fixed by (`open:identity:chain-payload-owner`) | Eligible burn bytes are credited through the accepted document's name |
| actor-document-retrieval-by-name | AD4, interface query-operation test site fixed by the interface owner's admission under (`dec:api:schema`) | A held requested name returns exactly the document bytes accepted by this host's receipt |
| actor-document-retrieval-outcome | AD4, interface query-operation test site fixed by the interface owner's admission under (`dec:api:schema`) | An unheld name yields explicit absence, distinct from operational inability |
| actor-document-static-opacity | AD5, packages/verification/tests/recognition.rs | Static validation never opens an Actor document or tests its prefix |

The planned actor-document-generation-custody selector is retired: no secret-custody or confidentiality-cleanup proposition remains for this public material. No executed claim is withdrawn, and ordinary resource release belongs to the bounded generation operation. The limit of (`assum:identity:actor-chain-separation`) remains an assumption with its stated failures, not a testable guarantee of foreign-chain rejection. The held sites above belong to operations not admitted here; their owners must name the actual source site before any builder implements or registers those controls.

**Open Question (Actor document admission completion)** · `open:identity:actor-document-admission` Owners: identity and authentication designers for pure comparison; client and authentication owners for bounded public-material generation; host and development-profile designers for first-association authority, retention and recovery; burn-reader owners under (`open:identity:chain-payload-owner`). (`amend:identity:actor-static-admission`) discharges the pure-operation semantic class, including the canonical naming value's treatment of the blinder and patch, without waiving an artifact manifest. (`tab:identity:actor-static-operations`), (`rule:identity:actor-read-order`), (`rule:identity:actor-publication`) and (`amend:decomposition:actor-static`) settle pure operation placement, refusals, publication plan and local confusable review. Actual registry publication, independent vectors, measured resource declarations and focused source/configuration-matched controls are builder duties in AD1 and AD2 of (`tab:identity:actor-cuts`). Prefix-constrained binding review and bounded public-material generation operation/dependency evidence remain (`open:identity:actor-generation-admission`); public fixed fixtures establish neither. Host and interface owners also owe publication and exact-name retrieval under (`conv:actepoch:interface`) before application enablement; no live service follows from a published theory. Application enablement stays behind (`gate:identity:implementation`) until the coordinated producer and consumer cut. First-admission authority and complete recoverable conflict evidence remain held: a host's empty cache proves no network-wide absence, and no first-writer-wins authority or new committed epoch member is admitted. Oracle context respects (`assum:identity:actor-chain-separation`) without a signed run context. Production allocation remains (`open:identity:actor-document-namespaces`); later-version continuity, frame attribution and flows remain (`open:identity:actor-document-succession`). No succession flow is admitted, so every second offer outside such a flow is refused. The naming decision's obligation stays held until these application duties and evidence are discharged.

**Open Question (Production Actor namespace)** · `open:identity:actor-document-namespaces` Owner: the production allocation authority whose agreement remains (`open:vision:publication-agreement`), with identity and act-and-epoch document designers for its eventual theory. The test name is org.peer-network.test.actor under (`schema:identity:actor-document`); its choice is settled and its registry publication and confusable-name review remain (`open:identity:actor-document-admission`). Production has the reserved first-version prefix 00 00 01 and no namespace text or concrete theory here. A repository test deployment cannot allocate the production name. Its authority, full namespace and registry assignment must be settled through the publication agreement, not inferred from the test spelling or from the reserved prefix.

**Assumption (Asset identifiers separate chain contexts)** · `assum:identity:actor-chain-separation` The successor relies on the assumption that contexts treated as different chains do not recognize the same issuance-derived asset identifier as an eligible Actor token, except for replay of the same run. (`amend:identity:author-asset-recipe`) fixes derivation from the issuance prevout and contract field, and (`conv:devnet:wire-actor`) fixes its opaque display-order use; neither proves non-recurrence on arbitrary histories, and the recipe has no additional history or run input. Rebuilding a test chain with the same parameters, keys and transactions reproduces its identifiers and is the same run replayed. Shared or reproduced issuance inputs, forks that retain an issuance, or a derivation collision can defeat separation between histories treated as different contexts; no rejection is guaranteed by an extra signed context, since none exists in this successor. When this assumption does not hold, the design supplies no cross-history separation claim; oracle recognition still requires the actual token, lineage and authority evidence. This is a limited deployment premise, not a theorem of the chain or a paper conformance claim.

**Rule (Actor context through the token)** · `rule:identity:actor-document-context` Nothing beyond the Actor document binds the successor name or approval to a network or run. The namespace separates test from production; within a namespace the chain-derived asset identifier resolves the name only on a chain where its eligible token exists, subject to (`assum:identity:actor-chain-separation`). No run member enters the document and no extra network/run context enters the approval or epoch. With complete evidence of a chain lacking that token, the oracle finds neither its burn nor an authorized signing key; missing evidence is unavailable instead. The static check reads the name opaquely and does not decide foreignness. This avoids an extra context admission for the distinction supplied by the document and oracle reading, without claiming separation where the assumption fails. The existing act-hash message and every admitted historical run-qualified carrier keep their meanings; the rule describes the designed successor only.

**Amendment (Actor name succession direction)** · `amend:identity:actor-document-succession` A later Actor-document version may quote the earlier document; the new name is then carried together with the old, so the pair exposes continuity while each name retains its 32-byte shape. This is a direction for a later version, not an added member, encoding, prefix assignment or theory in the present two-member body. Whether a change is admitted belongs to the oracle through its allowed flows, with one accepted document for a token at a time and the earlier name retained beside the new; the reasons, flows and evidence are oracle material, never a closure verdict. No such flow is designed or admitted here. An ordinary competing offer is refused under (`rule:identity:actor-document-receipt`); the later version, frame attribution and permitted flows remain (`open:identity:actor-document-succession`). This permits a later version and prefix without letting competing offers create two accepted documents, and leaves the closure outside the succession decision.

**Open Question (Actor succession admission)** · `open:identity:actor-document-succession` Owners: identity and act-and-epoch document designers for the later version and frame; host and development-profile designers for allowed oracle flows and retained association evidence. The direction of (`amend:identity:actor-document-succession`) still owes the later version's reference to the earlier document, with its member, bytes and theory; which name a frame keys and how a burn under the old name is credited after a change; who authorizes a change from the token, its effective transition, replay and recovery; and evidence that exactly one document is accepted at any time. A flow replaces the accepted association and retains the earlier name beside the new. No such flow is designed or admitted, so every second document is refused until this hold is discharged. No member, byte, flow or frame-credit rule is assigned here, and the closure decides neither succession nor its authority.

**Amendment (Pure Actor-document admission)** · `amend:identity:actor-static-admission` Under (`gate:identity:implementation`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), the recipe `pn-act-authentication/actor-document/v1` is admitted as design for the bounded deterministic operations of (`tab:identity:actor-static-operations`), with the complete patch-zero canonical naming-document value explicitly defined as the semantic object under (`case:identity:artifact`) and (`case:identity:semantic`). It is not the semantic identity of the token: the asset and blinder together define the naming value, and replacing the blinder changes that value even when the asset stays equal. The blinder is essential to the naming subject, its layer separation and prefix search, not incidental serialization metadata; application patch zero removes the interchange's presentation choice. Completeness covers namespace, version and both full members; canonical encoding supplies one reviewed projection, the namespace supplies role separation, the recipe identifier fixes the operation, and comparison assumes SHA-256 collision and second-preimage resistance in the prefix-constrained domain. Recomputability is from the disclosed complete document; neither recomputability from a name alone nor unrestricted-output collision strength is claimed. This meets the semantic column of (`tab:identity:class-properties`) without inventing an artifact manifest or changing that table. A separately distributed release artifact would still owe its artifact manifest. All existing recipes and carriers retain their meanings under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`).

Hidden fields: none under (`amend:identity:hiding-property`), with the distinct layer-separation property of (`rule:identity:actor-document-hiding`). This admission supplies computational comparison of public supplied values, not assurance about candidate origin or oracle acceptance. The semantic class and complete-byte recipe remain unchanged: publication makes their inputs independently available and adds no member or projection. Bounded generation still owes its ordinary source operation, measured limits and binding review under (`open:identity:actor-generation-admission`); application enablement and exact-byte publication await the coordinated cut of (`tab:identity:actor-cuts`). No secret-home custody, entropy-secrecy or hiding review is a prerequisite for these public values. No independent digest of the blinder or asset is admitted.

**Table (Actor pure operations)** · `tab:identity:actor-static-operations` pn-act-authentication owns actor::ActorDocument, the role-specific actor::ActorDocumentName, their canonical adapters and the recipe. A typed value contains the two fixed words and fixes namespace and application version [1, 0, 0]; it exposes immutable member accessors and no restamping or generic-role conversion. These are proposed public names admitted as design, not APIs asserted to exist. Document construction accepts explicitly supplied public words; it does not generate an honest blinder. A candidate value and its computed name can fail the prefix; computing that candidate digest is part of search, not assurance for a valid Actor name. Only a successful opening establishes the document validity predicate, and even it is not an accepted Actor association. Equal names never substitute for comparing available complete bytes when resolving an observed conflict.

| Operation and owner | Inputs and result | Refusals and assurance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| actor::ActorDocument::new, pn-act-authentication | asset and blinder, each a fixed 32-byte public value, to ActorDocument | Infallible fixed-shape construction; no entropy, eligibility or prefix claim |
| ActorDocument::encode, pn-act-authentication | value and ActorLimits to canonical bytes | UnavailableBudget only; encode through pn-interchange; no alternate bytes |
| ActorDocument::decode_strict, pn-act-authentication | received bytes and ActorLimits to value | The ordered structural/application outcomes of (`rule:identity:actor-read-order`); no prefix or oracle check |
| ActorDocument::name, pn-act-authentication | value and ActorLimits to ActorDocumentName | Encode and SHA-256 over exactly those bytes; UnavailableBudget; no prefix-validity assertion |
| ActorDocument::matches_name, pn-act-authentication | value, separately supplied fixed-size name and ActorLimits to comparison success | UnavailableBudget before NameMismatch; recompute, never accept a supplied digest as evidence of itself |
| actor::open, pn-act-authentication | bytes, expected name and ActorLimits to opened value and recomputed name | Strict read, then prefix, then NameMismatch in that order; a public opening proves neither entropy nor oracle acceptance |
| actor::registry, pn-act-authentication | no caller input to the existing interchange Registry | RegistryDefinition for defective owned theory material; not a received-document refusal |
| actor::search_step, pn-act-authentication | one public candidate value and ActorLimits to Match(value, name) or Continue | UnavailableBudget; computes the recipe and compares the assigned prefix, without randomness, iteration or publication |
| generate_actor_document, pn-client orchestration with the authentication public search_step | asset, ordinary external candidate source still owed to its owners, finite generation declaration and cancellation to completed public document/name | Planned AD3, held under (`open:identity:actor-generation-admission`); no secret-home consumer or enabled producer follows from the pure cuts |

ActorDocument::asset() and ActorDocument::blinder() borrow their corresponding 32-byte members without allocation. ActorDocumentName::from_bytes and as_bytes carry a fixed-width opaque name without checking a prefix; equality and ordering compare all 32 bytes lexicographically. They are passive operations with no identity, allocation, oracle or hiding claim of their own. Public pure inputs and outputs may be copied because they are explicitly disclosed document material. Candidates and generator inputs are public material; selecting an honest candidate-source operation is still outside that pure-input contract. A caller cannot turn a public constructor into evidence of honest generation.

**Rule (Envelope family application order)** · `rule:identity:document-family-order` The common, Actor and frame strict operations select namespace and held major/minor after complete canonical validation and the header shape, then validate the entire selected body's shape before checking the application patch and before typed-storage allowances. A higher minor's generic floor remains an interchange success where (`def:interchange:acceptance`) permits it, but is not a known strict application operation; content-key validation therefore follows operation-version selection. Canonical validation's own allocation or input-budget inability stops immediately: shape-first does not promise to inspect bytes without the resources its parser needs. Once that pass succeeds, a borrowed shape walk allocates no typed collection; variable-length member checks precede the collection allowance. All declared-budget, representational-overflow and allocation failures have one application class, UnavailableBudget, with a diagnostic reason and charged usage, never a document-invalid verdict. Reasons retain InputBytes, OutputBytes, Representation, CanonicalAllocation, Pairs, Entries, CollectionAllocation and Allocation as applicable; they are not different validity classes. These strict readers pass no shared reservation callback, so CanonicalReservation cannot arise. The frame retains its caller-support and missing-byte checks before received-byte validation, and its Shape, Edition and Patch names for the corresponding family stages; those names do not change which defect wins. Combined malformed-body/nonzero-patch inputs therefore report body shape, and a malformed member in an otherwise over-allowance list reports the member before typed-storage inability. Patch-first and early collection limits could reject sooner, but make sibling document readers disagree about the same envelope defects; the complete canonical pass already bounds the shape walk's input. The frame joins because checking its extra content keys before selection would give the same envelope defects a different precedence inside one epoch package. Removing its shared callback makes the validator allowance and allocator failure the same two possible canonical inability reasons as its siblings; it sacrifices shared-meter admission for this pure read, not bounded allocation. This convention changes no interchange verdict, theory or historical decoder.

**Rule (Actor strict read and opening order)** · `rule:identity:actor-read-order` First compare the received length with the declared input_bytes, then complete pn-interchange canonical validation with the declared canonical_allocation_bytes. Interchange CanonicalLimit maps to UnavailableBudget (CanonicalAllocation) and CanonicalAllocation to UnavailableBudget (Allocation); no shared meter is passed, so CanonicalReservation cannot arise. Every other canonical-validation error maps to Canonical; its internal diagnostic offset does not define an application class. Next require an unsigned-key map with namespace text at 0 and a three-unsigned-integer array at 1 (Envelope), compare the exact namespace (Namespace), select major 1/minor 0 (OperationVersion), require exactly content key 2 and an array body (Body), require two members (Count), and check asset then blinder, byte-string type before 32-byte width (MemberType, MemberWidth). Then require patch zero (OperationPatch). Fixed typed storage needs no variable collection allocation. For open, recompute the hash of the complete received canonical bytes, refuse a nonmatching test prefix (Prefix), then compare the separately supplied name (NameMismatch). Unknown namespace or coordinate is unavailable interpretation for association, not a claim of malformed interchange; the strict operation still reports its named selection refusal. No missing production row is inferred from a supplied name's prefix. Construction fixes the one admitted row; search_step returns Continue rather than an opening error for a nonmatching prefix. No token lookup, duplicate-token decision, signature, key authority or entropy test occurs in these operations.

Encoding, name and matches_name use checked encoded-length arithmetic first, then output_bytes, then the canonical encoder's allocation accounting; arithmetic overflow maps to UnavailableBudget with reason Representation; EncodingError::Limit and EncodingError::Length after the output-length check map to CanonicalAllocation, and EncodingError::Allocation to Allocation. No shared reservation callback is passed, so EncodingError::Reservation cannot arise. The canonical allocation declaration charges adapter construction and encoder work together. No re-encoding repairs received bytes. The patch-zero Actor document length is 106 bytes: one map head, three one-byte keys, the two-byte head and 27 bytes of namespace text, four bytes for [1, 0, 0], one body-array head and two 34-byte byte strings. This is a theory consequence, not a throughput or resource measurement, and no fixed total is asserted for the generic patch-free theory.

**Table (Actor operation resource declarations)** · `tab:identity:actor-limits` No deployment default, trial allowance or elapsed-time figure is admitted. ActorLimits has mandatory finite machine-size unsigned fields input_bytes, output_bytes and canonical_allocation_bytes, with no default; omission does not mean unlimited. The implementer records actual charged allocations, peak memory, own CPU and wall with load under (`rule:devperf:measurement`), on the exact valid shape, deep malformed input and each refusal path; below/at/above declarations are derived from that measurement. A caller's smaller allowance is inability, not invalidity. Fixed two-word storage and name comparison add no variable collection or search state.

| Declaration | Owner and measurement owed | Result when unavailable |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pure byte/allocation declarations | Authentication implementer measures the existing codec and hash adapters, including wrong-last-member and complete canonical refusal | UnavailableBudget with charged usage; no fabricated name |
| max_trials and deadline for generation | Client and authentication owners measure prefix-search trial cost, cancellation latency, ordinary source acquisition and resource release before any service default | Exhausted, Cancelled, Source or Codec as the first encountered operational stop; no completed document on failure |
| Application opening/receipt allowance | Host owner derives its service allowance from pure measurements and its separately admitted association work | Unavailable evidence is not a new accepted association |

**Open Question (Bounded Actor generation admission)** · `open:identity:actor-generation-admission` Owners: client and authentication designers, with cryptographic review of the unchanged prefix-constrained binding assumptions. AD3 remains a separate public-material generation cut, not an extension silently selected by AD1 or AD2. No secret value or consumer of pn-secret-home is proposed: the blinder, candidates and any sequence seed need no secret custody. The owners owe the ordinary candidate-source operation and its dependency admission, exact initialization/expansion satisfying (`alg:identity:actor-document-search`), the finite declarations and measured source/search/release costs, cancellation and exhaustion evidence, and the concrete generation-stop site. Fresh OS acquisition is an ordinary-source proposal, not an admitted dependency; an externally supplied public seed and reviewed deterministic expansion is an alternative that needs its own operation contract. No builder chooses either route from this hold. The client checks cancellation, deadline and trial ceiling before each acquisition, charges each attempted trial once and propagates source or codec failure without a completed document; only a prefix match can complete. Ordinary release of resources is required, with no confidentiality erasure or protected-temporary review. The exact source-failure mapping and source operation are settled before its builder starts. The layer boundary is (`rule:identity:actor-document-hiding`). A common public seed is permitted for AD3: with the chain's asset identifiers and the admitted deterministic expansion, a reader can compute the corresponding names. No per-Actor unpredictability, seed non-reuse or delayed seed publication is required. The alternative would require an unshared per-Actor start not computable from public chain or closure data and publish its seed no earlier than its document; that would add start-provenance, reuse and publication-order duties without changing the static input boundary or preventing links from other public data. The common-seed policy avoids those duties and makes the absence of observer secrecy explicit; it selects neither a dependency, a randomness source nor an expansion operation. The owners must still admit the exact source and sequence and justify the search's distinct-trial probability model; this hold asks for no entropy-secrecy or observer-hiding proof. Existing public construction and search_step remain buildable in AD1 and public opening in AD2. Host receipt, oracle acceptance and publication are separate duties, not consequences of successful generation.

**Rule (Actor theory publication and operands)** · `rule:identity:actor-publication` AD1 publishes the unchanged complete theory of (`schema:identity:actor-document`) as packages/act-authentication/schema/actor-document.cddl. actor::registry parses that exact embedded file and acquires its assigned coordinate through the existing pn-interchange Registry API, using no duplicate code-string theory and no new registry format. The actor-document-registry control compares the file's parsed namespace and coordinate and normalized Theory::to_cddl output with actor::registry; parser/acquisition failure is RegistryDefinition. The local comparison covers the complete assigned test set recorded here: beneath org.peer-network.test, act, act.witness, actor, api-control-register, api-control-schema, api-node-register, api-node-schema, authority, epoch, epoch.acts, epoch.evidence, epoch.frame, epoch.root, epoch.state, publication, publication.author-authority and verified-act. These are lowercase ASCII, distinct sequences of atoms, with no case-folding or numeric-letter aliases; actor and act differ in the text of their last atom, and actor is not beneath act.
