Beneath is atom-sequence extension, never textual prefix, under (`schema:identity:common-document-parameters`); byte equality selects the assigned name, with no normalization. The comparison also distinguishes the unassigned control spellings absent, carriers, fixture, items and orphan and the deliberately wrong epoch.frames; their occurrence in a control does not allocate them, and .json path suffixes do not allocate namespace children. This discharges this local recorded-name comparison, not external or production allocation. The builder supplies the parser and exact-assignment evidence; production remains (`open:identity:actor-document-namespaces`).

AD1 owns packages/act-authentication/tests/actor-documents.json and tests/actor_vectors.py. The latter uses Python standard-library SHA-256 and an explicitly written independent encoder, importing neither production code nor its codec; the Rust producer is the other route. Let W(i) denote 32 copies of byte i, public fixture words and never honest-generation evidence. Base a has asset byte i at index i and blinder byte 32 + i at index i, for i from 0 through 31, namespace org.peer-network.test.actor and version [1, 0, 0]; b changes only asset to W(3), and c changes only blinder to W(4). The nonuniform base makes reversal of either word observable; reversing a supplied asset is a different public value, not a structural refusal or an alternative chain byte order. Expected bytes and hashes are generated by those two routes in AD1 and retained with recipe and operand descriptors in the JSON; this design computes none. A public bounded candidate sequence uses a's asset and blinder SHA-256 of the ASCII text actor-document-public-fixture followed by the trial index as an unsigned 64-bit big-endian integer, beginning at zero. The independent script selects the least index whose document hash has the test prefix, and the least index whose hash does not; it records the indices and search declaration with the generated corpus. This is a reproducible fixture procedure, not a production blinder source. The AD2 fixture cut declares a trial ceiling of at least two to the power 27, and measures the time and memory needed for that declaration before running the search; no time or service default follows from this probability floor. Under the independent uniform-hash model of (`alg:identity:actor-document-search`), p = 2^-24, the expected trials are 2^24, and the miss probability after N trials is (1 - 2^-24)^N, less than exp(-N / 2^24); at N = 2^27 it is below exp(-8). The actual least index is fixed by the operands, so this model supplies no guarantee. Exhaustion, or inability to resource that floor, returns the operand label and measured declaration to the identity designer and stops AD2; neither operands nor the ceiling are silently changed. No successful vector is promised by a finite bound. Its deterministic public inputs carry no hiding claim.

Every malformed neighbour starts from a: remove the last body member for Count; replace the first member by unsigned integer 0 for MemberType; remove the last byte of the asset for MemberWidth; encode the three-entry map head with a nonpreferred one-byte argument for Canonical; append one unsigned zero for trailing Canonical. The body-as-integer-zero case yields Body, missing header key 0 yields Envelope, and namespace org.peer-network.test.act yields Namespace. Change only patch to one for OperationPatch and only minor to one for OperationVersion. Add content key 3 with integer zero to the minor-one case for OperationVersion and to a for Body. Combine patch one with the shortened asset for MemberWidth, and nonpreferred map head with missing last member for Canonical. The opening-success vector uses the selected matching candidate and its independent name; the wrong-prefix vector uses the selected nonmatching candidate with its own true name; the wrong-name vector flips bit zero of byte index 31 of the matching candidate's expected name, leaving document bytes unchanged. Combine the nonmatching candidate and that changed supplied name to show Prefix before NameMismatch. A reserved-production name is formed by replacing only the first three bytes of that supplied name with 00 00 01, without inventing a production namespace or theory; it cannot select an opening row. Interchange separately accepts patch one structurally while the application refuses it; a held higher-minor floor is exercised only through the existing generic reader. Namespace separation is the exact preimage difference, never a claim of disjoint hash outputs. All runtime-budget boundary operands are charged-usage minus one, equal and plus one where representable, supplied after measurement, not arbitrary constants.

**Table (Actor-document implementation cuts)** · `tab:identity:actor-cuts` These cuts are a deadline-bound migration under (`req:identity:admission-record`): each pure producer lands with its comparison consumer and independent evidence, and no application selects the successor until the final coordinated cut. Earlier pure work may remain unused if a later hold persists; no old identity is silently converted. No execution status follows from the design.

| Cut and owner | Builds and evidence | Holds afterwards |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AD1, authentication | Public ActorDocument/ActorDocumentName, construction, codec, name and comparison, registry and search_step; independent a/b/c and malformed vectors, focused canonical/name/registry/version/API controls | Opening-success prefix fixture and opening may land in AD2; no generation, authority, oracle or application |
| AD2, authentication | Pure open and its matching/nonmatching fixture corpus under the measured fixture search declaration; prefix and expected-name controls | No production search; a public opening is no accepted association |
| AD3, client and authentication owners | Only after (`open:identity:actor-generation-admission`) settles the ordinary source operation: bounded public-material generation, generation-stop evidence, measured limits and reviewed dependencies; no secret-home consumer | No token authorization or network-wide compare-and-install |
| AD4, host, interface, development-profile and burn-reader owners | First-association authority, recoverable conflict evidence and concurrent durable association, eligible burn-to-name attribution, exact-byte public Actor retrieval with the by-name and outcome controls, and separate oracle context controls after their own admissions | Production allocation and succession retain their own holds; no inference from a host's empty cache |
| AD5, successor document and application owners | Coordinated producer and act/frame comparison consumers with static-opacity integration and explicit enablement evidence | Undischarged source, title, marker, lineage and production duties remain under their own holds; no courtesy or oracle truth is claimed by byte equality |

## Common document representation · `sec:identity:common-documents`

**Amendment (Common document design boundary)** · `amend:identity:common-document-design` The common documents of (`def:actepoch:act`), (`def:actepoch:witnessed-act`), (`def:actepoch:epoch`) and (`def:actepoch:result`), with the acts subject of (`def:actepoch:acts`), have the representation design below. Their content counts, order and 32-byte widths are unchanged. The act and witnessed-act representations, their test theories and pure first-cut operations are built with independent unsigned vectors and focused controls; the epoch, root and acts representations remain designed and unbuilt. (`amend:identity:common-static-admission`) admits these pure static operations as design, not enabled application identities or evidence for an implemented static check. Test namespace assignment is (`schema:identity:common-document-parameters`), production allocation remains (`open:vision:publication-agreement`), and registry publication and admission remain (`open:identity:common-document-admission`). Every admitted and historical identity, signed message, vector and carrier keeps its exact meaning under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`) and (`conv:actepoch:history`); no existing signed object is converted by shortening it to these members.

**Convention (Common envelope and body)** · `conv:identity:common-document-envelope` Each of the five documents uses the map family of (`schema:identity:actor-document`): namespace text at key 0, the version triple at key 1, and the entire ordered body at key 2. The four common bodies are fixed arrays; the acts body is an array of pairs. A body array preserves the concept's positions without a second field-number convention and keeps one envelope reader for these and the chain family. A map with one content key per member would repeat key bytes and require a separate mapping from keys to the concept's order. A new member is a new major under either form: an added position changes key 2's type, and a required new key is not additive under (`inv:interchange:minor-inclusion`). Here changing a body type is a major change under (`law:interchange:major-boundary`); a later major changes the type at key 2 without changing the envelope grammar. No optional, null, constant or extension content member is added. Envelope constants are representation, not extra semantic members under (`inv:actepoch:independent-members`).

**Schema (Common document theory parameters)** · `schema:identity:common-document-parameters` The five test namespaces are org.peer-network.test.act for the act, org.peer-network.test.act.witness for its witness, org.peer-network.test.epoch for the epoch, org.peer-network.test.epoch.acts for its acts list and org.peer-network.test.epoch.root for its root. They are assigned under the repository test reservations of (`amend:design:test-publication-profile`); no production namespace is assigned. Beneath means that the sequence of atoms under (`gram:interchange:label-grammar`) extends the other name’s sequence, not that its text merely begins with the other text: org.peer-network.test.actor is not beneath org.peer-network.test.act. Each concrete theory below targets major 1, minor 0, with the literal type name uint at the patch position under (`alg:xchg:fragment-check`), denoting every unsigned 64-bit value, and word = bstr .size 32. Application production and complete successor validation use patch zero so the same members have no unrecorded producer patch choice; another patch is a distinct hashed representation and is not restamped. This operation restriction does not change (`inv:interchange:patch-identity`): the structural theory leaves every unsigned patch accepted. The act and witnessed-act concrete theories are parser-checked and published as test registry assignments, with within-reservation name review and independent unsigned vectors; the epoch, root and acts theories and registry assignments remain held under (`open:identity:common-document-admission`), and production allocation remains (`open:vision:publication-agreement`).

Each fenced source below is a complete theory submitted independently, with its top-level map first and its own word definition; the five sources are not concatenated. The act and witnessed-act sources retain an unused patch range definition as part of their owned publication text, but key 1 uses uint directly, as the assignable fragment requires. The arrow key notation and the colon notation denote the same closed-map entries; neither changes the canonical document bytes. A reference alias in one theory does not import the body definition of another.

**Schema (Common act theory)** · `schema:identity:common-act` The seven positions are those of (`def:actepoch:act`), without a host fact, seal or signature. The sequence is an exact 32-byte member; its numeric interpretation, range and relation to historical sequence representations belong to the typed-act admission, not to an implicit integer conversion here. Author is the opaque Actor name of (`rule:identity:actor-document-consumers`). The other five members name the type and subject documents in the static set.

```cddl
act-document = { 0 => "org.peer-network.test.act", 1 => [1, 0, uint], 2 => [author, sequence, act-type, typed-document, parents-document, content-document, dependency-document] }
author = word
sequence = word
act-type = word
typed-document = word
parents-document = word
content-document = word
dependency-document = word
patch = 0..18446744073709551615
word = bstr .size 32
```

**Schema (Witnessed act theory)** · `schema:identity:common-witnessed-act` The four positions of (`def:actepoch:witnessed-act`) carry the act hash, the BIP-340 x-only public verification key and the first and second 32-byte halves of its 64-byte signature, in that order. The halves concatenate in signature byte order; they are neither two signatures nor references to documents. The document's assigned namespace and version select this signature operation under (`rule:identity:common-act-approval`), without an algorithm member. The witness is beside the act; hashing it does not introduce a self-reference into the message it signs.

```cddl
witnessed-act-document = { 0 => "org.peer-network.test.act.witness", 1 => [1, 0, uint], 2 => [act-hash, verification-key, signature-first, signature-second] }
act-hash = word
verification-key = word
signature-first = word
signature-second = word
patch = 0..18446744073709551615
word = bstr .size 32
```

**Schema (Common epoch theory)** · `schema:identity:common-epoch` The two positions of (`def:actepoch:epoch`) are the acts and frame references. Neither the marker, predecessor, host, counter, signature nor an external locator is present. The frame's own theory and amount remain with its owner under (`open:actepoch:marker-frame`); a 32-byte reference does not choose them.

```cddl
epoch-document = { 0 => "org.peer-network.test.epoch", 1 => [1, 0, uint], 2 => [acts-document, frame-document] }
acts-document = word
frame-document = word
word = bstr .size 32
```

**Schema (Epoch root theory)** · `schema:identity:common-result` The three positions of (`def:actepoch:result`) are the epoch, entering-state and selected-input/evidence references. Its identity is the candidate accepted root, not a hash of computed outputs, a marker or a predecessor. The referenced state and evidence documents still need their own theories and binding admissions; their interpretation is not supplied by these names.

```cddl
epoch-root-document = { 0 => "org.peer-network.test.epoch.root", 1 => [1, 0, uint], 2 => [epoch-document, entering-state-document, selected-evidence-document] }
epoch-document = word
entering-state-document = word
selected-evidence-document = word
word = bstr .size 32
```

**Schema (Acts list theory)** · `schema:identity:common-acts` The subject document of (`def:actepoch:acts`) has one complete finite array of pairs, preserving the host's accepted order. Each pair is act first and witness second; both are 32-byte names. The empty array is a real document. There is no sorting, count member, separate position field, chunk wrapper or inferred absence. The schema alone does not establish pair equality, uniqueness by act name or identifier, or acceptance: (`rule:identity:common-document-reading`) supplies those checks. This variable-length subject is not a fifth fixed-count common document.

```cddl
acts-document = { 0 => "org.peer-network.test.epoch.acts", 1 => [1, 0, uint], 2 => [* [act-hash, witnessed-act-hash]] }
act-hash = word
witnessed-act-hash = word
word = bstr .size 32
```

**Rule (Common canonical bytes and lengths)** · `rule:identity:common-document-bytes` Each concrete theory uses the single canonical encoding of (`lang:interchange:data-language`) and (`req:xchg:determinism`): definite map and arrays, ordered unique keys 0, 1, 2, exact namespace UTF-8, preferred unsigned heads for the version, and byte strings of length 32. Received bytes must already have that spelling and complete consumption; noncanonical input is refused, never repaired and then signed or hashed. Let N be the allocated namespace's UTF-8 byte length, h(x) the preferred CBOR head length for an unsigned argument x, and P the encoded patch length. Here h(x) is 1, 2, 3, 5 or 9 for x below 24, 256, 65536, 4294967296 or 18446744073709551616 respectively. For a fixed body with k members, the length is N + h(N) + 8 + P + 34k bytes: the map head, three keys, version-array head, major and minor each take one byte, the body-array head takes one byte, and each member takes a two-byte byte-string head plus 32 bytes. At application patch zero P = 1: the act is N + h(N) + 247, the witnessed act N + h(N) + 145, the epoch N + h(N) + 77, and the root N + h(N) + 111 bytes, using each document's own N. For n pairs the acts document is N + h(N) + 7 + P + h(n) + 69n bytes: each pair adds one array head and two 34-byte byte strings. At patch zero this is N + h(N) + 8 + h(n) + 69n. These are encoding arithmetic, not measured costs, a chosen resource ceiling or concrete byte examples. With the assigned ASCII namespaces at application patch zero, N is respectively 25, 33, 27, 32 and 32 for act, witness, epoch, root and acts list, and h(N) = 2. The totals are therefore 274, 180, 106 and 145 bytes for the four fixed bodies; the acts list is 42 + h(n) + 69n bytes, hence 43 bytes when empty. These are patch-zero totals, not fixed total encoded lengths across patches under (`conv:actepoch:fixed-width`). Checked size arithmetic, finite caller limits and allocation refusals remain implementation obligations; no old carrier's limits are silently reused.

**Table (Common identity adjudications)** · `tab:identity:common-document-adjudications` Each row follows (`alg:identity:adjudication`) separately: validate the entire typed canonical object first, identify the named later comparison, and test whether direct equality or an existing parent binding supplies it. These five boundaries are part of the successor design, not existing producers or consumers. Each row passes (`crit:identity:benefit`) for a new independently compared document value. The candidate artifact classification is resolved by the explicitly defined semantic object and assurance treatment of (`amend:identity:common-static-admission`), not by reusing a historical identity or inferring semantic assurance from canonical bytes alone. No identity is minted for a member merely because it is 32 bytes. The semantic treatment applies only to these typed pure comparisons; independently distributed release artifacts still owe (`case:identity:artifact`). No manifest format is invented; the Actor naming value has its separate pure-operation admission under (`amend:identity:actor-static-admission`) and its separate generation and association holds.

| Object | Named comparison and benefit | Why existing equality does not supply this boundary | Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Act | Actor approval and host/static comparison of the exact act with the witness and acts pair | The author/sequence/type identity names the act but does not bind its exact representation; no epoch parent exists when approval is made | Semantic document-value identity implemented as a pure first-cut operation; application selection remains held |
| Witnessed act | Resolve and check the exact separately supplied witness named by the acts pair | The signed act hash does not bind the recorded key and signature bytes; choosing a different valid signature changes this artifact | Semantic document-value identity implemented as a pure first-cut operation; application selection remains held |
| Epoch | Resolve the exact acts/frame pair named by the root's epoch reference | The root also binds state and evidence; its later identity cannot replace the separate reference required to resolve this component | Semantic document-value identity admitted as design, unbuilt |
| Epoch root | Match a supplied complete root document to the marker's carried accepted root | Neither a path nor a preceding epoch binds this independently supplied three-reference set | Semantic document-value identity admitted as design, unbuilt |
| Acts list | Resolve the complete accepted order named by the epoch | Individual act and witness identities do not bind order, completeness or the pair association; the epoch carries this reference before opening the list | Semantic document-value identity admitted as design, unbuilt |

**Definition (Common document identity recipes)** · `def:identity:common-document-recipes` The five recipe identifiers below, admitted as design for pure operations by (`amend:identity:common-static-admission`), each name SHA-256(B), where B is the entire received canonical byte sequence of the corresponding concrete theory, including namespace, full version and complete body. The output is exactly 32 bytes in digest order, without truncation, reversal, textual hexadecimal or a second hash. The recipe identifier is metadata selecting the operation, not another hash-input member or preamble. Separation is the five distinct namespace literals within B, as in (`def:identity:actor-document-recipe`); equal complete encodings cannot belong to two of these distinct namespaces. SHA-256 binding still assumes collision and second-preimage resistance, not disjoint output ranges or a proof that hashes are unique. No output prefix search is performed for these documents. The primitive and implementation division are (`rule:crypto:operation-scope`) and (`amend:identity:sha-two-line`), with no new primitive or cryptographic library selection; the direct package edges are (`amend:decomposition:common-static`). Each complete recipe is immutable under this pure-operation admission; the act and witnessed-act registry assignments and independent unsigned vectors now provide first-cut implementation evidence, while epoch, root and acts publication and evidence remain owed. No instance digest is assigned.

| Document | Recipe identifier | Hash input |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Act | pn-act-authentication/common-act/v1 | Complete canonical act-document bytes |
| Witnessed act | pn-act-authentication/witnessed-act/v1 | Complete canonical witnessed-act-document bytes |
| Epoch | pn-epoch/epoch-document/v1 | Complete canonical epoch-document bytes |
| Epoch root | pn-epoch/epoch-root-document/v1 | Complete canonical epoch-root-document bytes |
| Acts list | pn-epoch/acts-document/v1 | Complete canonical acts-document bytes |

**Table (Common document admission dossiers)** · `tab:identity:common-document-dossiers` Under (`req:identity:admission-record`), the following rows and their common conditions are admitted design dossiers for the bounded pure operations of (`tab:identity:common-static-operations`); only the first-cut act and witnessed-act pure operations are implemented. Ownership follows the public authentication and epoch responsibilities of (`amend:decomposition:act-authentication`) and (`dec:decomposition:crates`), with pn-interchange as the only codec and pn-client owning user intent; exact operations are (`tab:identity:common-static-operations`) and planned dependency edges are (`amend:decomposition:common-static`); no edge is inferred from a recipe string. The implementation deadline is before any successor producer, signer, consumer or marker publication is enabled: the coordinated cut supplies the producer and its comparison consumer together or refuses enablement. All five claim only computational equality of the complete canonical document value under the hash assumptions; none proves semantic validity, availability, origin, entropy, authority, chronology, chain acceptance or a truthful calculation. A hash hides no small-domain or public input merely by hashing it; no additional hiding is claimed. Their namespace, version, lengths and member values are public representation, with Actor asset hiding and private subject openings remaining their own owners' conditional claims.

| Identity | Producer and proposed owning site | Consumer and accepting/refusing decision | Staleness, migration and independent vectors owed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Common act | Actor client invokes a typed public construction/hash operation in pn-act-authentication | Complete-act signer recomputes the act hash from canonical bytes; host and static checker reject disagreement with witness or pair | Any byte change requires recomputation and fresh approval; independently reproduce whole preimages and hashes, each-member and envelope changes, and wrong-shape refusals |
| Witnessed act | Actor client constructs the witness through pn-act-authentication after complete-act approval | Host/static checker resolves the named witness, recomputes its identity and rejects wrong act association or invalid signature | Changed act hash, key or signature makes this witness name stale; no conversion of a historical three-signature carrier; vectors cover both halves, key parsing and valid/invalid approval |
| Epoch | Host composes the accepted acts/frame references through the successor pn-epoch carrier | Root/static reader resolves and compares the epoch identity before using its acts/frame | Either reference or any envelope change makes the name stale; vectors cover swapped references, missing resolution and the empty acts document without an empty-frame shortcut |
| Epoch root | Host composes epoch, entering-state and selected-evidence references through the successor pn-epoch carrier | Publication/oracle reader compares the supplied root hash to the marker's literal bytes; static reader checks the resolved input set under its selected interpretation | Any member or envelope change makes the root stale; marker bytes cannot migrate an old root; vectors cover position swaps, changed state/evidence and major-1 refusal of an all-zero accepted root without manufacturing a zero preimage |
| Acts list | Host builds one complete accepted order through the successor pn-epoch carrier | Epoch/static reader recomputes the list identity, resolves both names of each pair and rejects duplicates or a mismatched witness act member | Insertion, removal, reordering or either pair member changes the name; vectors cover empty/single/multiple pairs, preserved order and pair equality |

Across all rows, a missing required document is unavailable evidence, not a matching hash or an empty substitute; conflicting bytes under one name refuse. Before a replacement edition is enabled, retained original inputs are rebuilt under newly admitted recipes with explicit consumer selection; missing inputs cannot be recovered from a residue. Old recipes and signed objects retain their meanings under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`). The semantic assurance treatment, pure operation selection and refusal precedence are admitted by (`amend:identity:common-static-admission`) and (`rule:identity:common-read-order`). The first-cut act and witnessed-act theories and independent unsigned reproduction are built; the epoch, root and acts cuts still owe their theories and reproduction. Measured production limits remain owed under (`tab:identity:common-limits`), and coordinated enablement remains held. No present fixture witnesses these future consumers.

**Rule (Complete-act approval operation)** · `rule:identity:common-act-approval` The candidate operation pn-act-authentication/complete-act-approval/v1 signs the exact 32 output bytes of the common-act recipe of (`def:identity:common-document-recipes`) using the BIP-340 operation selected for (`schema:identity:common-witnessed-act`). The signer constructs or validates the complete canonical act and recomputes that message inside the admitted operation; a generic caller-chosen digest entry point is not admitted. The base operation's own challenge hashing remains its BIP-340 algorithm, not a second application frame; no additional outer hash, recipe text, host, network or run enters the message. This is the same fixed-length message facility described by (`amend:identity:proposal-signature`), with a new subject and operation, not that historical proposal's recipe or signature. The namespace and version inside the hashed act select its representation; signature unforgeability and the act hash's binding assumptions supply coverage of those exact bytes. Coverage of referenced typed bodies and private commitments additionally needs their own admitted binding recipes and checks; a signature over a root alone establishes none of their missing premises. The public verifier parses the 32-byte x-only key, concatenates the signature halves in order, recomputes the act hash, compares it with the witness's first member and the pair's act name, and verifies the signature under that recorded key. Invalid key, invalid signature or disagreement refuses approval. Static validation establishes this recorded-key relation only. Whether that key speaks for the Actor in the epoch belongs to the oracle under (`rule:lineage:authority`) and (`rule:identity:actor-document-context`), with the limit of (`assum:identity:actor-chain-separation`); no additional context or Actor-to-key map is committed. No host seal, receipt, ordering promise or completed-set membership follows.

The historical proposal and approval messages under these Actor keys are SHA-256(F(r,tag,fields)): F begins with the ASCII recipe identifier followed by a zero byte under (`amend:identity:act-framing`), so its first byte is below 128. Every canonical act preimage B begins with the three-entry map head 163 (decimal). The preimage sets are disjoint. Substituting one message for the other at a verifier that recomputes its message therefore requires either SHA-256(B) = SHA-256(F) for distinct preimages, contrary to collision resistance, or a signature valid for a different message, contrary to the signature assumption. This applies in both directions and does not claim disjoint digest ranges. The public proposal check without openings under (`amend:identity:proposal-signature`) cannot distinguish P from an arbitrary 32-byte value: it can see a complete-act approval as a valid signature over an unopenable P, but gains no proposal relation without an F-preimage opening. The designed courtesy request and every later message under these keys must stay outside the act-document preimage family under (`amend:identity:authentication-recipe-gate`); no unexamined canonical-document message class is admitted.

This operation's adjudication is provenance, scoped to recorded-key authentication, under (`req:identity:admission-record`), not another digest identity; its pure message is built, recorded-key verification remains admitted as design by (`amend:identity:common-static-admission`), and its signer remains held: the Actor client produces it and the host and static verifier consume it to refuse an unapproved exact act. An act hash or typed equality alone supplies no recorded-key approval. Its assurance is signature authenticity conditional on key-use policy and binding, not Actor authority or historical truth. Any act byte or selected key change makes approval inapplicable; a new scheme or message interpretation needs a new document version and operation admission. BIP-340 permits distinct valid signatures for one message, so retries may reuse the selected witness while another signature names another witness artifact. Independent approval vectors, changed-byte and wrong-key controls, signer-side operation restriction and migration before successor enablement remain held under (`open:identity:common-document-admission`); every historical proposal, seal and approval operation is unchanged.

**Rule (Common document reading and resolution)** · `rule:identity:common-document-reading` Shape is checked before following references: the selected document has its known namespace/version row, exact body count and widths and canonical full-consumption bytes. Interchange acceptance remains exactly (`def:interchange:acceptance`), including higher-minor tolerant reading; a tolerant read alone does not authorize this successor operation. Complete common-document validation requires the exact admitted operation row and application patch zero, without rejecting another patch as structurally invalid interchange. No unassigned row, unknown scheme or unsupported operation is inferred from a nearby name. All reference members are opaque 32-byte names until resolved to the required subject inside the static set and checked against that subject's own admitted recipe; no locator is inferred from their bytes. The Actor name is the explicit outward denotation and is never opened or prefix-tested by the static check. Sequence, verification key and signature halves are not document references. For each acts pair, the resolved act's name equals the pair's first member and the resolved witness's first member; the witness's own name equals the pair's second member. A repeated act name is refused by the acts-list codec before resolution. After resolution the static check refuses a repeated author/sequence slot, even when the type or act hash differs; this is the local restriction of (`def:actepoch:act`), stronger than network-v0.37.4 ``itm:graph:act-authentication-requirements-identity-uniqueness`` (closure), which permits nonce reuse across families; earlier slot and placement facts are supplied by entering state under (`rule:actepoch:checking`). This local refusal is conditional on those supplied facts; the declared-run check separately refuses predecessor identity collisions under (`rule:actepoch:parent-connection`). No tie-break admits a colliding selected epoch. The complete order is retained, never sorted or deduplicated as a repair. Missing required resolution cannot be supplied by a parent, marker, chain query or host cache outside the declared set. Optional private openings keep their conditional treatment under (`rule:actepoch:checking`), not an excuse for missing required public support.

**Rule (Accepted root and marker compatibility)** · `rule:identity:common-root` The accepted-root candidate is the epoch-root-document recipe’s 32-byte SHA-256 output, committing the three-reference set transitively through its admitted member recipes. It has no marker, predecessor or external locator input. SHA-256 supplies no exclusion of the all-zero output; this design neither proves an accessible zero preimage nor assumes one impossible. The recipe stays SHA-256 of the exact canonical bytes, without exclusion, regrinding, rehashing or a second recipe. Under major 1 of (`def:devnet:wire-marker`), an epoch whose root is all zero cannot be accepted by a marker: the zero value is required at the authenticated origin and forbidden at every following place under (`def:lineage:marker-error`). The origin is identified by its authenticated place, never by its value. The refusal requires one comparison and does not alter any epoch bytes or hash output; it avoids inventing a replacement recipe and needs no premise about the feasibility of targeting that value. What a host does on encountering such an epoch, its operation and refusal evidence remain owed to (`open:identity:chain-marker-admission`); no recovery is designed here. A later marker major may state its own reservation. Lookup, exact publication and marker admission remain that hold. Namespace separation inside the complete root preimage needs no extra chain-specific frame and proves no chain binding by itself.

The public static checker resolves the selected interpretation version and constants as (`rule:actepoch:checking`) requires, inside the set or in the fixed identified public source; this is not a new common-document member, an external interpretation locator or a marker supplied as a calculation input. The oracle independently applies the predecessor marker's version-selected standard to the next accepted epoch under (`def:devnet:wire-marker`), and must establish that the resolved interpretation and resulting static verdict are supported by that standard before accepting the root. Encoding version, calculation interpretation and marker standard are different selections, not integers to equate by spelling. An unsupported or unavailable correspondence supplies no recognized acceptance and is never repaired by changing the epoch bytes. The exact compatibility relation, evidence and operation belong to the marker/root admission and (`open:actepoch:source-selection`); this rule does not invent a version map, admit an interpreted source or make the static checker read the marker.

**Verification (Common document controls)** · `verif:identity:common-document-controls` The act, witnessed-act and registry first-cut controls are executed; later-cut controls remain designed and unexecuted. Their sole assertion sites and minimal independent evidence are assigned by (`rule:identity:common-publication`) and their cuts by (`tab:identity:common-cuts`); a named site does not assert a present test, broaden a package or register an executed claim. Independent expected bytes and hashes are produced by code and independently reproduced at admission, never filled in by this design. The codec and claim owners register actual assertions before any execution status is claimed.

| Control name | Proposed site | Proposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| common-act-canonical | packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | Exact act bytes round-trip; wrong seven-member count, any wrong width, noncanonical head/order or trailing bytes refuse without repair, with the combined shape-before-OperationPatch and selection-before-content-key operands of (`rule:identity:common-publication`) |
| common-witness-canonical | packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | Exact witness bytes round-trip; wrong four-member count, key/half width or noncanonical spelling refuses |
| common-epoch-canonical | packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs in the successor cut | Exact epoch bytes round-trip; wrong two-member count, reference width or noncanonical spelling refuses |
| common-root-canonical | packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs in the successor cut | Exact root bytes round-trip; wrong three-member count, reference width or noncanonical spelling refuses |
| common-acts-canonical | packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs in the successor cut | Empty and nonempty lists round-trip in original order; wrong pair count or width, repeated act names and noncanonical spelling refuse, with member shape before UnavailableBudget (Pairs), shape before OperationPatch and selection before content-key checks under (`rule:identity:common-publication`) |
| common-document-separation | packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs, with development-only authentication evidence | Each of the five independently reproduced preimages includes its namespace and every envelope/body byte |
| common-document-role-refusal | packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs | Each canonical document refuses at another role's strict reader with Namespace |
| common-operation-version-boundary | packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs | Interchange's patch-independent and higher-minor-floor acceptance does not authorize the strict patch-zero major-1/minor-0 operation |
| common-auth-registry | packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | The two published theory assignments equal the objects and coordinates returned by common::registry |
| common-epoch-registry | packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs | The three published theory assignments equal the objects and coordinates returned by common::registry |
| common-act-approval | packages/act-authentication/tests/public.rs in CD3 | Recorded-key approval verification accepts the valid recomputed-act witness and refuses changed-act, wrong-key, malformed-key and changed-signature cases |
| common-act-approval-api | Source review of packages/act-authentication/src/common.rs and its public re-exports in CD1 | The common module exposes only the operations and public types admitted by (`tab:identity:common-static-operations`) and (`rule:identity:common-publication`), with the verifier added in CD3 and no generic digest-signing operation |
| common-act-approval-cross-role | packages/act-authentication/tests/public.rs in CD3 | Under one fixture key, historical proposal and approval signatures refuse at the recomputing complete-act verifier and complete-act signatures refuse at the recomputing historical verifiers |
| common-acts-pair | packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs in CD2 | The conditional comparator accepts the three matching supplied recomputed values and refuses the first mismatching act name, witness name or witness act member |
| common-static-slot | packages/verification/tests/recognition.rs in CD4 | After resolution a repeated author/sequence slot refuses even with another type or act hash |
| common-static-opacity | packages/verification/tests/recognition.rs in the successor cut | Fixed-shape reading follows no references before shape validation; complete static validation uses only supplied internal resolutions, never opens an Actor document or tests its name prefix, and makes no marker or network fetch |

**Open Question (Common test namespace allocation, closed)** · `open:identity:common-document-namespaces` The five test names are assigned by (`schema:identity:common-document-parameters`) and reserved by (`amend:design:test-publication-profile`); no namespace-text choice remains for them. The act and witnessed-act registry assignments and within-reservation name review are evidenced by CD1; epoch, root and acts publication remain (`open:identity:common-document-admission`). Production allocation remains with the publication-agreement owners under (`open:vision:publication-agreement`); no production name or registry evidence is supplied here. The retained complete act-envelope design has no namespace reservation under (`open:identity:act-envelope-allocation`).

**Open Question (Common document admission completion)** · `open:identity:common-document-admission` Owners: identity and act-and-epoch document designers for theories, recipes, operation selection and binding evidence; authentication/client owners for complete-act approval; epoch/host owners for carriers and static consumers; publication and development-profile owners for root carriage and lookup. The exact-byte hash family, patch-zero application spelling and BIP-340 act-hash message are designed here. Position 2 holds one ordered array under (`conv:identity:common-document-envelope`), and the five test namespace literals are assigned by (`schema:identity:common-document-parameters`); neither choice remains open. This keeps one family reader, the concept’s positions as the only mapping and no per-member keys. The semantic assurance treatment, pure operation names, owners, refusal ordering, registry publication plan and within-reservation confusable-name review are discharged as design by (`amend:identity:common-static-admission`), (`tab:identity:common-static-operations`), (`rule:identity:common-read-order`) and (`rule:identity:common-publication`). CD1 supplies the act and witnessed-act theories, unsigned independent vectors and focused canonical and registry controls. Its usage measurement is retained as evidence without a production allowance; the later cuts still owe their theories, vectors, measured usage and controls under (`tab:identity:common-cuts`). The existing cross-role argument is design evidence; CD3 remains held until the identity/authentication owners admit the independent signature producer's pinned source revision, home and public test operands, and supply independent signature and key-use review with actual operation evidence. No reference signer is selected by CD1 or delegated to its builder, and private signing remains held. The two new registry edges to sha2 and serde_json are proposed, not admitted; CD2 requires their dated dependency admission under (`amend:decomposition:common-static`), with a differing hash line returned to (`amend:identity:sha-two-line`)'s owner. No arbitrary service ceiling is admitted: production allowances require (`tab:identity:common-limits`). Coordinated application producer/consumer migration and enablement remain held under (`gate:identity:implementation`). This hold supplies the complete-act approval’s operation, independent vectors and verification-material admission for the conformance hold (`open:identity:act-realization-admission`), rather than creating a second conformance owner. The referenced subjects’ binding recipes are also required before complete-body coverage is claimed. The entering-state and selected-evidence names, envelope and role structures are designed by (`conv:identity:state-evidence-envelope`) and (`schema:identity:state-evidence-parameters`); their complete theories and binding recipes remain held until subject key/operand types and relations are admitted. The frame, type, parent, content and dependency document theories, sequence interpretation and public interface are owed by their respective owners under (`gate:actepoch:design`), (`open:actepoch:state-provenance`), (`open:actepoch:marker-frame`), (`open:actepoch:source-selection`) and (`open:actepoch:accountability-occurrences`), not designed by opaque references. Root lookup, version-standard compatibility and publication evidence remain (`open:identity:chain-marker-admission`). No root, recipe candidate or signature design establishes oracle truth, a courtesy standard or a present implementation.

**Convention (State and evidence envelope design)** · `conv:identity:state-evidence-envelope` Entering state and selected evidence are subject documents under (`def:actepoch:document-grade`), not additional common documents. Their envelope follows (`conv:identity:common-document-envelope`): canonical map key 0 is exact namespace text, key 1 the version triple and key 2 the whole body, with no additional top-level content key. Their bodies may use the plain maps, lists, integers, ratios and meaningful absence allowed by (`inv:actepoch:independent-members`); the common fixed-count 32-byte-member restriction does not apply. Independently retrieved or retained public parts and removable parts remain separate under (`inv:actepoch:part-separation`). Canonical encoding, duplicate-key and trailing-byte refusal follow (`lang:interchange:data-language`) and (`req:xchg:determinism`). This admits design of the names, envelope and semantic role structure only. Complete theories, exact nested key/operand types, limits, refusal ordering, identity dossiers, operations and implementation remain held under (`open:identity:common-document-admission`), (`open:actepoch:state-provenance`) and (`open:actepoch:source-selection`); an unresolved required subject prevents complete input admission. No common document member, recipe or byte is changed.

**Schema (State and evidence test parameters)** · `schema:identity:state-evidence-parameters` The reserved test names are org.peer-network.test.epoch.state for accumulated entering facts and org.peer-network.test.epoch.evidence for independent selections and supporting observations, at major 1 and minor 0. Patch is an unsigned 64-bit integer structurally; application production and complete validation use patch zero, without restamping received bytes. The names use the dotted-atom grammar of (`gram:interchange:label-grammar`) beneath (`amend:design:test-publication-profile`); no production allocation or external uniqueness is claimed. State distinguishes inputs from computed results, and evidence includes choices without promising a proof or external source. The common-family major-boundary rule (`law:interchange:major-boundary`) governs changed body types. Exact lowercase ASCII dotted atoms distinguish both names from the test API, publication, publication-author, verified, authority, act, witnessed-act, actor, epoch, acts-list, root and frame profiles and from API schema/register names; unsupported names or majors never select a similar profile. Registry publication and complete theory/codec evidence remain held.

**Schema (Entering-state role structure)** · `schema:identity:entering-state` The designed body is a closed role-keyed map with inline coverage under (`rule:actepoch:state-domain-coverage`), one mandatory typed title-subject reference and conditionally required ledger, semantic standing-basis, schedule, spent-slot, public-support and determination-state references under (`def:actepoch:state-subjects`). Each selected role resolves to one named subject; a required role cannot be omitted, null or replaced by a zero-hash sentinel. A required empty subject is a real empty document. The manifest has an entry for each admitted key domain of every role, declaring inapplicability for the domains of an inapplicable role; global totals are full entries. Role names are semantic slots, not assigned nested key bytes: exact keys, types and complete CDDL await the subject admissions. A flat body would couple independently retained subjects; a fixed array of 32-byte members would unnecessarily externalize small coverage records. The map keeps those records inline while preserving independent subject references. This is designed and unbuilt.

**Schema (Selected-evidence role structure)** · `schema:identity:selected-evidence` The designed body is a closed map with selections, attempted-check support and observations under (`def:actepoch:evidence`). Selections associate admitted operand slots and supported subjects with small typed values or references to independently retrieved defining objects. Attempt support is ordered by the required attempted-rung sequence and binds each calculation instance, procedure/version, point and independent support. Observations associate admitted observation roles and supported subjects with typed observation documents. Empty groups are allowed only when unused; they supply no default for a required operand. Exact role-key bytes, operand slots, attempt descriptors, observation types and limits remain subject admissions under (`open:actepoch:source-selection`), not an extensible untyped string/value bag. Bindings point to independently fixed epoch, state or act inputs, not the epoch root or evidence itself. Previous standing, copied bounds/verdicts, title certificates, winning timelines, optional time assertions, Actor documents, authority/source histories and external interpretation locators are not members. Recorded witnesses remain in the acts branch; optional private openings keep their admitted separate associations. This is designed and unbuilt.

**Amendment (Pure common-document admission)** · `amend:identity:common-static-admission` Under (`gate:identity:implementation`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), the five recipes of (`def:identity:common-document-recipes`) are admitted as design for the pure producers and comparison consumers below. Each complete canonical document value, including its envelope and ordered opaque members, is explicitly the semantic object under (`case:identity:artifact`) and (`case:identity:semantic`); its meaning here is that value, not the resolved act, calculated epoch or chain occurrence. This selects semantic computational equality rather than the candidate artifact class: there is no release-path or manifest consumer in these operations, and inventing a manifest would add a distribution subject that none reads. The canonical encoding is the reviewed projection, the namespace separates roles, the recipe identifier selects the exact operation, and SHA-256 collision and second-preimage resistance condition comparison. B includes the patch, which interchange treats as presentation; freedom from incidental content holds because every admitted operation encodes, reads and names only patch-zero values and refuses another patch (OperationPatch), so one value has one name; completeness is the whole document and recomputability rests on public inputs. The unchanged five recipes therefore meet the semantic column of (`tab:identity:class-properties`); they do not acquire artifact distribution, provenance or release assurance. Each declares hidden fields: none. No nonce or salt is introduced, and neither an opaque reference nor its hash claims hiding of its referent. Existing artifact candidates for other documents are unaffected. Release packaging, if introduced, separately owes (`case:identity:artifact`).

This admission discharges the assurance treatment, exact operation selection, package placement and refusal contracts for pure common-document work; it admits no execution claim. The benefit and staleness of each dossier remain: a fixed-width independently supplied reference is compared with the complete supplied value, and any byte change invalidates its previous name. No member digest is added. The complete-act approval retains its distinct provenance class, scoped to recorded-key authentication, under (`rule:identity:common-act-approval`), with hidden fields: none in the public message or witness; custody of a signing secret is not a hidden field of that object. Only message construction and verification under an explicitly supplied recorded public key are admitted as design here. Signing and production selection stay held. Independent encoding, name and signature vectors and finite implementation witnesses must accompany the corresponding pure producer and consumer in the same cut, following the pure-operation boundary of (`amend:identity:formation-three`) and (`amend:identity:closure-components`). Missing evidence prevents that cut's admission; a design statement is not a substitute for it.

**Table (Common static operations)** · `tab:identity:common-static-operations` The owning package owns its typed value, canonical-document adapter and recipe together. All adapters use pn-interchange's canonical codec; none writes a second CBOR codec. A module named common is selected explicitly and never substitutes for a historical module. The table's operation names are public names relative to that module; the input members retain the theories' names and order. A document name is a distinct role type containing 32 bytes, with byte equality and unsigned lexicographic comparison; ordering conveys no chronology, authority or equivalence between roles.

| Package; typed value and name | Constructor and inputs | Encoding, reading and identity operations |
| --- | --- | --- |
| pn-act-authentication; ActDocument, ActDocumentName | ActDocument::new(author, sequence, act_type, typed_document, parents_document, content_document, dependency_document), seven 32-byte values | ActDocument::encode, ActDocument::decode_strict, ActDocument::name, ActDocument::matches_name |
| pn-act-authentication; WitnessedActDocument, WitnessedActDocumentName | WitnessedActDocument::new(act, key, signature_first, signature_second), four 32-byte values | WitnessedActDocument::encode, WitnessedActDocument::decode_strict, WitnessedActDocument::name, WitnessedActDocument::matches_name |
| pn-epoch; EpochDocument, EpochDocumentName | EpochDocument::new(acts, frame), two 32-byte references | EpochDocument::encode, EpochDocument::decode_strict, EpochDocument::name, EpochDocument::matches_name |
| pn-epoch; EpochRootDocument, EpochRootDocumentName | EpochRootDocument::new(epoch, entering_state, selected_evidence), three 32-byte references | EpochRootDocument::encode, EpochRootDocument::decode_strict, EpochRootDocument::name, EpochRootDocument::matches_name |
| pn-epoch; ActsDocument, ActsDocumentName | ActsDocument::new(ordered_pairs, limits), each pair two 32-byte names | ActsDocument::encode, ActsDocument::decode_strict, ActsDocument::name, ActsDocument::matches_name |

Each name type exposes from_bytes([u8; 32]) and as_bytes(), and same-role equality and unsigned lexicographic ordering; from_bytes asserts no existence or validity of a referent. Each fixed value exposes read-only accessors with its constructor argument names, and ActsDocument exposes pairs() in retained order; mutation cannot bypass its constructor. These accessors and name constructors are infallible and perform no lookup. Each owning module supplies its own Limits value with mandatory finite machine-size unsigned fields input_bytes, output_bytes, canonical_allocation_bytes, max_pairs and collection_allocation_bytes, with no default; the last two are used only for lists. No shared package is added merely for this caller declaration. Fixed constructors return the typed patch-zero value without reading a referent; fixed-size arguments make count and width errors unrepresentable there. ActsDocument::new takes a finite borrowed slice of fixed-size pairs and returns an immutable owned list or UnavailableBudget or DuplicateActName, in that order. It checks max_pairs, encoded output length and the cumulative collection-allocation ceiling in that order, with arithmetic overflow reported as UnavailableBudget (Representation) at the calculation that overflows, before reserving the owned list and duplicate index. It checks duplicate first names by increasing list position after allocation succeeds. pairs() borrows the retained slice; no unbudgeted public copying operation is admitted. It neither sorts the list nor rejects a repeated witness name by itself. encode receives a typed value and explicit finite limits and returns its canonical bytes or the ordered resource refusal, never an altered value. encode, name and matches_name first compute the theory's output length with checked arithmetic (UnavailableBudget (Representation) on overflow), then refuse UnavailableBudget (OutputBytes), then UnavailableBudget (CanonicalAllocation) or UnavailableBudget (Allocation) at the first failed reservation. No shared reservation callback can refuse: the canonical encoder's EncodingError::Limit maps to UnavailableBudget (CanonicalAllocation), EncodingError::Allocation to UnavailableBudget (Allocation), and its EncodingError::Length after the independent output-length check maps to UnavailableBudget (CanonicalAllocation); EncodingError::Reservation cannot arise. The canonical allocation ceiling charges the adapter's construction and encoder work; it is not substituted for the separately checked output ceiling. decode_strict receives the complete byte slice and the same limit declaration and returns a patch-zero typed value or the ordered refusal below. name receives a typed value and limits, canonically encodes it and returns the corresponding role name or UnavailableBudget. matches_name receives that value, an independently supplied name of the same role and limits, recomputes and returns equal or different, or UnavailableBudget; it never accepts a supplier's cached hash. The exact same bytes form the hash preimage; no caller path, selected chain, authority or context enters. Role names expose bytes for the designed carriers but no cross-role implicit conversion. Complete typed equality remains available and is not replaced by hash comparison.

pn-act-authentication additionally owns common::act_approval_message(act, limits), returning the recomputed 32-byte message or UnavailableBudget, and common::verify_act_approval(act, witness, pair_act_name, limits), returning RecordedKeyApproval or the ordered refusal. It first constructs the message, compares the pair's act name, then the witness's act member, then parses the recorded key, then verifies the concatenated signature halves: PairActName, WitnessActName, Key, Signature are the first applicable refusal classes after resource refusal. RecordedKeyApproval retains the recomputed act name and exact recorded key in read-only fields as an ephemeral comparison result; it has no serialization or independent identity. It attests only this supplied-key relation, not Actor authority or complete-body validity. No private-key argument or generic digest-signing operation is exposed by this cut. pn-epoch owns common::match_acts_pair(pair, recomputed_act_name, recomputed_witness_name, witnessed_act_member): it compares those supplied recomputed values in act-name, witness-name, witness-act-member order and returns equal or PairActName, PairWitnessName or WitnessActName. It is a pure conditional comparison, not a resolver or proof that its caller recomputed anything. The owning integration must obtain those values from the admitted operations before claiming resolved pair equality. Neither operation checks spent slots, counted parents, title, signatures' authority or epoch completeness.

**Rule (Common strict-read refusal order)** · `rule:identity:common-read-order` The strict application operations have one order for the same bytes, selected role and finite limit declaration. The family convention is (`rule:identity:document-family-order`). First compare received byte length to the declaration (UnavailableBudget (InputBytes)); then use pn-interchange's complete canonical validation. Interchange's CanonicalLimit maps to UnavailableBudget (CanonicalAllocation) and its CanonicalAllocation to UnavailableBudget (Allocation); no shared meter is passed, so CanonicalReservation cannot arise; every other canonical-validation refusal maps to Canonical. These operational stops are not a document-invalid verdict. No provisional decoder event is accepted before complete canonical success. A different allocation outcome can stop an implementation earlier; it cannot turn a refused document into an accepted one. The canonical error's internal offset is diagnostic, not another application refusal class. A complete canonical pass precedes all application checks, so a later malformed byte outranks an earlier shape or namespace error. No re-encoding repairs supplied bytes.

After canonical success, require an unsigned-key map containing keys 0 and 1 (Envelope); require namespace text and a three-unsigned-integer version array (Envelope); compare namespace to the operation's literal (Namespace); require held major 1 and minor 0 (OperationVersion); require exactly content key 2 beyond keys 0 and 1, with an array value (Body); require its fixed length for the four common roots (Count). Finally check each body member in order, type before width (MemberType, MemberWidth). For the acts list, after Body check each pair in list order: require an array (PairType), length two (PairCount), and first then second byte-string type and 32-byte width (MemberType, MemberWidth). After every member passes shape, require patch zero (OperationPatch); for the acts list then compare its length with max_pairs (UnavailableBudget (Pairs)), acquire the bounded typed storage, and refuse the earliest repeated first name (DuplicateActName). A duplicate whose later occurrence has the least list index is first; no sorting or deduplication repairs it. Checked arithmetic and the cumulative collection-allocation ceiling cover the owned list and duplicate index before their allocation; overflow is UnavailableBudget (Representation), an exceeded ceiling is UnavailableBudget (CollectionAllocation) and failed allocation is UnavailableBudget (Allocation). These are the entire shape/read refusals; no point parsing, signature check, referent fetch or hash-prefix check belongs to decode_strict.

| Theory | Order after the common envelope/version stages |
| --- | --- |
| Act | Body, Count seven, MemberType then MemberWidth for author, sequence, type, typed document, parents, content, dependencies |
| Witnessed act | Body, Count four, MemberType then MemberWidth for act name, key, first signature half, second signature half |
| Epoch | Body, Count two, MemberType then MemberWidth for acts then frame |
| Epoch root | Body, Count three, MemberType then MemberWidth for epoch, entering state, selected evidence |
| Acts list | Body, PairType/PairCount/MemberType/MemberWidth in pair order, OperationPatch, UnavailableBudget (Pairs), storage UnavailableBudget (CollectionAllocation)/UnavailableBudget (Allocation), DuplicateActName |

For each fixed theory the table's shape stages are followed by OperationPatch. UnavailableBudget is one inability class with diagnostic reasons and charged usage under (`rule:identity:document-family-order`). This is selection of a strict application operation, not a new interchange verdict. (`def:interchange:acceptance`) still accepts a structurally valid nonzero patch and may read a higher minor through its floor; such a result is not a patch-zero major-1/minor-0 operation input. No rejection here is attributed to interchange merely because the application operation is unavailable. A separately selected historical operation retains its own verdicts. Reference resolution, missing evidence, slot freshness and semantic validation follow this boundary and cannot be represented by successful decoding alone.

**Table (Common operation limits)** · `tab:identity:common-limits` No deployment default or measured ceiling is assigned. Each call declares finite input/output byte and canonical allocation ceilings; list operations additionally declare max_pairs and cumulative collection-allocation bytes for the owned list and duplicate index together. The canonical validator's existing borrowed path and accounting are used, with no content allocation on an untrusted advertised count. Fixed-array construction needs no variable allocation. For fixed patch-zero outputs, 274, 180, 106 and 145 bytes are consequences of the theories, not performance measurements; the list's 42 + h(n) + 69n length is likewise arithmetic. Allocation ceilings count requested bytes, not elapsed time. The implementation must report charged usage on refusal and success, check arithmetic before reserving, and never infer an unbounded allowance from omission. A smaller caller allowance supplies an operational unavailable result, not a claim that the theory rejects the document.

| Declaration | Measurement owed and owner | Admission boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Fixed-document input/output and canonical allocation budgets | Authentication and epoch implementers measure exact valid shapes, malformed deep inputs and refusal paths, recording allocations, peak memory, own CPU and wall with load under (`rule:devperf:measurement`) | The pure operations require caller declarations; no production default before these results are reviewed |
| max_pairs and list input/output budget | Epoch implementer measures empty, increasing ordered lists, repeated names near each end, malformed last pairs and independent-name comparison; report allocation and comparison growth, including the duplicate index | No arbitrary epoch cardinality is made a protocol condition; the service owner chooses its finite allowance from these measurements and states unavailable on exhaustion |
| Duplicate-index and canonical allocation budgets | Epoch implementer measures the chosen deterministic index with no quadratic all-pairs scan, and the existing interchange borrowed validator | Refusal order is independent of index traversal; only the earliest repeated input position is reported |
| Approval call budget | Authentication implementer measures message construction, malformed key, signature refusal and success separately | No signing or service throughput limit is inferred from byte sizes or finite vectors |

**Rule (Common theory publication and vector evidence)** · `rule:identity:common-publication` The first pure cut publishes the concrete major-1/minor-0 theories in packages/act-authentication/schema/common-act.cddl and common-witnessed-act.cddl; the epoch cut publishes packages/epoch/schema/common-epoch.cddl, common-root.cddl and common-acts.cddl. Each publishes the complete fenced source of its schema, with its own word definition and uint written directly at key 1’s third position under (`alg:xchg:fragment-check`); the unused patch range retained in the act and witnessed-act sources is not the patch-position type. The patch remains free as in (`schema:identity:common-document-parameters`). They are the owned source artifacts for those five registry assignments, not a new registry format. Each package's common::registry() takes no caller input and returns the existing pn-interchange Registry built from those exact embedded files, or RegistryDefinition if its owned material cannot be parsed or acquired; that failure is a publication/implementation defect, not a supplied-document refusal. It must not duplicate theories in code strings. The package-specific common-auth-registry and common-epoch-registry controls parse the files, acquire the assignments through the existing registry API and compare their namespaces, coordinates and theory objects with those returned by common::registry. The separate common-operation-version-boundary control exercises patch independence and the higher-minor floor against strict application selection; canonical round trips do not claim registry correspondence. This supplies the code/registry comparison without a new checker. Publication and parser evidence arrive with the implementation; these paths are not claimed to exist merely by this design.

The namespace reservation and confusable-name review are decided for this assigned set: each literal is lowercase ASCII, its atom hierarchy is explicit, act differs from actor by a complete atom, and witness, acts and root occupy separate atoms under their subjects. No normalization, case folding or string-prefix dispatch is permitted. This review concerns the repository's own assigned test tree, not a claim about all external allocations or production authority. The implementation must compare the actual published literals with (`schema:identity:common-document-parameters`) and the reservation table before claiming assignment evidence; production allocation remains (`open:vision:publication-agreement`).

Each package's tests/common-documents.json retains public input members, the exact canonical preimage, recipe identifier, expected name and ordered refusal descriptors, with no instance filled in by this design. Expected encodings and SHA-256 outputs are generated and independently reproduced by tests/common_vectors.py in that package using Python's standard library and an explicitly written encoder, without importing production code or its codec. The Rust producer is the second route and must match those bytes and names; generating expectations through the Rust producer alone is not independent. CD1 and CD2 publish no signed-witness expectations and introduce no reference signer. CD3 must supply signed-witness evidence only after its identity/authentication owners admit the independent signature producer's source revision and home for the public test secret, key and auxiliary-randomness operands of row index 0 of packages/act-authentication/tests/bip340.csv under (`open:identity:common-document-admission`); this is a held admission, not a choice delegated to its builder. The existing independent route ci/authentication_bip340.py verifies and does not sign; neither it nor the unsigned vector script is represented as a signer. Production verification uses the existing admitted library. Neither fixture keys nor signature operands establish entropy, custody, authority or a usable signing service. A committed digest receives its existing hash-citation reader registration in the vector-producing cut; no digest is assigned here.

The operand descriptors are fixed here so the first cut does not choose a corpus. Let W(i) be the 32-byte word whose every byte is integer i; this is a public deterministic fixture operand, never a blinder or a name-validity claim. Case act-a uses W(1) through W(7) in member order; act-b changes only its last member to W(8). CD1's witness-a and witness-b contain the corresponding independently computed act name, W(9) as key and W(10), W(11) as signature halves. These words have no signature-validity claim; signed witnesses enter with the verifier in CD3, after its independent signature-evidence admission. The three list cases are empty, the act-a/witness-a pair, and that pair followed by act-b/witness-b. epoch-a names the two-pair list and W(13) as the opaque frame reference; root-a names epoch-a, W(14) and W(15). These descriptors determine the five families’ preimages and names once the fixed algorithms are run; no preimage, signature or digest is computed by this design. The fixed body counts and the literal namespace already determine their lengths.

For each fixed theory, remove its last member for Count, shorten its first word by one byte for MemberWidth, replace that word by the unsigned integer 0 for MemberType, and encode the three-entry map head in the nonpreferred one-byte-argument form for Canonical; also combine the Count and MemberWidth mutations and the Canonical and Count mutations to distinguish the stated precedence. Combine patch one with the shortened first word to require MemberWidth before OperationPatch. For the two-pair list, declare max_pairs as one and shorten the second pair's last word by one byte: MemberWidth precedes UnavailableBudget (Pairs); with that word restored the same declaration yields UnavailableBudget (Pairs). Change only patch to one for OperationPatch and only minor to one for OperationVersion, while separately recording interchange's patch-independent and tolerant verdicts. Add unsigned content key 3 with integer value 0 to the minor-one case for OperationVersion, and to the minor-zero case for Body, so unavailable operation selection precedes content-key validation. For each role, add that key to its already specified wrong-role namespace neighbour for Namespace. Each role's existing canonical control owns these combined-fault assertions at its sole site; the list's canonical control also owns its Pairs/shape allowance neighbours. For the list, use the byte string W(1) as the first pair for PairType, a one-member first pair for PairCount, the same type/width mutations on its first act name, and append its first pair to the two-pair list for DuplicateActName; combine that duplicate with a last pair holding only its first member to show shape precedes duplication. Reverse the valid two-pair order as another valid list with its own expected name, never as a repaired list. For namespace separation, feed each of the five canonical cases to every other role's strict reader and expect Namespace; no assertion of mathematically disjoint hash ranges is made.

The pair comparator uses act-a and witness-a, then substitutes W(20) separately for recomputed_act_name, recomputed_witness_name and witnessed_act_member to exercise PairActName, PairWitnessName and WitnessActName respectively; combined substitutions establish first-cause precedence. In CD3, after its evidence admission, approval uses a separately named signed-witness-a for act-a, never the unsigned CD1 witness-a; then it uses act-b with that signed witness while updating the pair and witness act-name operands to act-b so that the failure reaches Signature rather than association. The wrong-key case substitutes row index 1's public key from bip340.csv; the malformed-key case uses a word of maximum byte values, outside the curve field. The changed-signature case replaces its scalar half by the BIP-340 group order encoded by the independent script, which must refuse its range. Pair and witness name substitutions separately reach the earlier association refusals. For CD3 cross-role coverage, its held evidence admission must supply signatures of the independently recomputed historical proposal and approval message descriptors from the first case of tests/v3-golden.json with the same public fixture key, then test both directions with the recomputing verifiers as (`rule:identity:common-act-approval`) specifies; the unopenable supplied-proposal case retains its stated non-claim. The CD1 and CD2 corpus is fixed without a signature producer; the CD3 signature corpus remains held with its evidence admission. Budget-boundary descriptors derive from each implementation's measured charged usage and recorded declaration, with below/at/above cases; their numeric operands wait for measurement. Later static-opacity evidence needs the integrated reader and is not substituted by these byte fixtures.

| Control | First-cut independent evidence and sole assertion site | Later evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| common-act-canonical | One exact act, one changed member and all stated combined malformed cases, including MemberWidth before OperationPatch and Namespace/OperationVersion before additional content-key Body; packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | Broader adversarial corpus only where implementation risks warrant it |
| common-witness-canonical | One exact witness and wrong count/width/noncanonical cases, with the stated combined count/width, canonical/count, shape/patch and selection/content-key operands; packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | No key-authority claim from these bytes |
| common-epoch-canonical | One exact acts/frame pair and count/width/noncanonical refusals, with the stated combined count/width, canonical/count, shape/patch and selection/content-key operands; packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs | Referent resolution is later integration |
| common-root-canonical | One exact three-reference value and count/width/noncanonical refusals, with the stated combined count/width, canonical/count, shape/patch and selection/content-key operands; packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs | Major-1 zero-root publication refusal belongs to marker admission, without a fabricated zero preimage |
| common-acts-canonical | Empty, single and two-pair values, retained order, malformed pair and duplicate-first-name precedence, MemberWidth before UnavailableBudget (Pairs), restored width yielding UnavailableBudget (Pairs), shape before OperationPatch and selection before content-key checks; packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs | Measured large-list and exhausted-budget cases before a service allowance |
| common-document-separation | CD2 exact independent preimages for all five roles; packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs, with pn-act-authentication as a development-only dependency | No assertion of disjoint hash ranges |
| common-document-role-refusal | CD2 every canonical case supplied to each other role's reader; packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs | No resolution or authority claim |
| common-operation-version-boundary | CD2 patch-one and minor-one cases through interchange and the corresponding strict readers; packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs | No change to interchange acceptance |
| common-auth-registry | CD1 parses the two source files, acquires their assignments and compares registry objects and coordinates; packages/act-authentication/tests/encoding.rs | No registry publication claim before this evidence |
| common-epoch-registry | CD2 does the same for the three epoch source files; packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs | No second registry format |
| common-act-approval | CD3 independently admitted signed witness and changed-act, wrong-key, malformed-key and changed-signature outcomes; packages/act-authentication/tests/public.rs | Signing custody and full subject binding stay held |
| common-act-approval-api | CD1 source review of the complete common-module declarations and public re-exports against the operation table, renewed for the CD3 verifier addition; packages/act-authentication/src/common.rs is the sole review site | This absence proposition is source-review evidence, not an executed claim inferred from passing API examples; no new census or checker is introduced |
| common-act-approval-cross-role | CD3 independent signature corpus admitted before the cut, tested in both recomputing directions; packages/act-authentication/tests/public.rs | Unopenable historical proposals retain their non-claim |
| common-acts-pair | CD2 matching triple and one substitution for each ordered comparison; packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs | No resolved-slot claim |
