| common-static-slot | CD4 two resolved acts sharing author and sequence with a different type or act hash; packages/verification/tests/recognition.rs | Entering facts and counted-parent evidence remain integration duties |
| common-static-opacity | Later complete static reader with inaccessible Actor documents and explicitly supplied required references; packages/verification/tests/recognition.rs | This entire control waits for that reader; pure codecs establish only that their own calls perform no resolution |

The corresponding existing control under (`verif:identity:common-document-controls`) retains one proposition at the named site under (`rule:testing:site`); the table refines its cut and evidence, not its execution status. The builder registers actual assertions and their exact selectors before claiming execution under (`rule:testing:coverage`). Resource boundary checks accompany the operation they bound, with measured declarations and below/at/above cases; no random test count or broad unrelated suite substitutes for those cases.

**Table (Common-document implementation cuts)** · `tab:identity:common-cuts` Each identity cut supplies its producer and comparison consumer together; CD3's approval verifier instead uses independently produced fixture signatures after its evidence admission, while its signer arrives in CD5 before any enablement within the deadline-bound migration recorded here. Each cut keeps public main green and is separately selected. This is the deadline-bound migration of (`req:identity:admission-record`): none of the successor surfaces may be selected by an application until the coordinated enablement cut; failure to finish a later cut leaves earlier pure operations unused, never silently selected in place of old carriers.

| Cut and owner | Creates and serves | Still closed afterwards |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CD1, authentication — built | Test theory files and registry, typed act/witness values, canonical adapters, names and comparison consumers, unsigned independent vectors, canonical and registry controls and the restricted-API control; pure act-message construction | Signer, live host and oracle selection; no complete-act acceptance or referenced-body validity |
| CD2, epoch | A distinct common module containing epoch/root/acts values, theory registry, adapters, names, comparison consumers and conditional pair comparator, with independent vectors and focused controls; requires separate dated admission of the proposed registry edges under (`amend:decomposition:common-static`) before adding them, with protected fixture-selection evidence | Present epoch carriers and algorithms unchanged; no calculation, resolver, marker lookup or publication |
| CD3, authentication | Pure recorded-key approval verifier and independently reproduced signature/cross-role controls only after the signature-evidence admission, with the library and key-use review for this operation | Private signer and complete-body coverage under (`open:identity:act-realization-admission`); verification material for production remains an owner duty |
| CD4, epoch and verification | Measured service allowances and complete supplied-set integration only after frame, typed parts, counted parents, entering state, selected evidence and source designs are admitted; register pair integration, slot freshness and opacity assertions | (`open:actepoch:state-provenance`), (`open:actepoch:source-selection`) and (`open:actepoch:title-admission`) retain every undischarged subject duty; a codec does not discharge them |
| CD5, client, host and publication | Coordinated producer/signer/consumer enablement after their own authority, custody, source, subject and publication admissions | Marker lookup, zero-root host response, standard compatibility and oracle evidence remain (`open:identity:chain-marker-admission`) until that owner supplies them; no interface or marker operation is authorized by CD1–CD3 |

## Frame document representation · `sec:identity:frame-document`

**Amendment (Frame document design boundary)** · `amend:identity:frame-document-design` The successor frame of (`def:actepoch:frame`) has its records-owned passive carrier and exact integer adapter implemented with focused evidence; the epoch codec, name and comparison operations remain admitted as design and unbuilt, while external production, epoch use and oracle recognition remain held under (`open:identity:frame-document-admission`). Its amounts are exact counts of the burnt asset's minimal units, with the unit fixed by the selected network and no unit or asset member in the frame. The test network counts its test asset's minimal units; a production network counts its selected real asset's minimal units. Positive whole-number destructions under (`model:devnet:burn`) and (`rule:devnet:burn-map`) sum without a fractional scale or rounding, so the oracle's comparison under (`def:lineage:gross`) is integer equality. The paper's network-v0.37.4 ``def:comparator:imported-frame`` and ``rem:transport:frame-amount-granularity`` fix neither a machine format nor a deployment scale; this exact integer subset gives no permission to round a supplied total. Only gross input has this representation; charges, residual balances and other burn-denominated quantities are exact values in that same network unit, possibly fractional, in their own representations, while prices keep their own denominations. Their equations remain (`def:actepoch:ledger`). The admission below fixes operation placement, the recipe identifier, the conditional comparison and the publication route; no implemented codec, identity value or executed claim is supplied by this design.

Under (`gate:identity:implementation`) and (`req:identity:admission-record`), the pure cut follows the bounded producer/comparison form of (`amend:identity:formation-three`) and (`amend:identity:closure-components`): producer and independently supplied-name comparison arrive in the same cut, before either successor producer or consumer is enabled. pn-records owns a new private-field Frame value containing an ordered association of opaque 32-byte Actor names and unsigned 32-byte GrossAmount values; neither is the historical BurnAmount or a checked epoch. pn-records also owns the passive FrameName carrier; the epoch document's frame member is that FrameName without changing its 32-byte representation. pn-epoch owns FrameDocument and a separate frame module for the assigned theory, canonical bytes and pn-epoch/frame-document/v1, beside its common epoch/root/acts adapters under (`amend:decomposition:common-static`); pn-records retains the exact numerical adapter through its existing numerical owner. A third-party static checker under (`rule:actepoch:checking`) reads frame bytes through pn-epoch and its records, graph and numerical closure plus the admitted interchange edge, without linking pn-host, its PostgreSQL client/runtime or the secret-handling root pn-credential-derivation for that read. The host alternative would bring its whole normal closure, including those dependencies and authentication, and make an installed checker protected under (`amend:decomposition:protected-package-resolution`); its benefits are continuity with historical payload codecs and an existing direct SHA-256 edge, not a requirement of (`sig:design:host`), which leaves new payload codecs to their respective admissions. Epoch placement is selected so public-record checking does not acquire a store transport or secret-handling root merely to read this document, consistent with (`rule:testing:public-record-conformance`); placing it in records would instead add a codec edge to the passive carrier owner. Host consumers can reach the frame through their existing host-to-epoch edge; epoch gains no host edge. The SHA-256 route is the direct normal workspace sha2 edge proposed for pn-epoch by (`amend:decomposition:common-static`), with defaults disabled and its stated zeroize selection, not a new records hashing wrapper or a new primitive. That edge and the development-only serde_json edge for the independent vector reader remain proposed until the common admission's dated stable-release, source, licence, compiler and pin review is supplied under (`rule:decomposition:stable-dependencies`); the admitted normal pn-epoch-to-pn-interchange path edge supplies the codec and registry. No additional edge, package, feature or node edge is admitted here, no manifest is changed, and the existing protected and feature-off selections must pass the common admission's package-scoped comparison before those edges land. A records-only preparation cut may build the passive carriers, constructor and exact adapter with their focused evidence before those registry-edge reviews; the coordinated epoch codec, name producer, comparison, publication and vector controls wait for those reviews and their edge admission. No private material or hidden field enters any of these operations. Their public values and outcomes receive the standing observation form under (`rule:observe:spans`), without a new metric or content exception.

The designed public operations are Frame::from_entries, taking an already ordered finite slice of fixed-width pairs and explicit entry/allocation allowances and returning Frame or Order, Duplicate or UnavailableBudget; Frame::entries, returning a borrowed immutable slice without a refusal; encode_frame, taking Frame and explicit construction/encoding allowances and returning the patch-zero canonical bytes or UnavailableBudget; read_frame, taking independently selected support and optional borrowed bytes with read allowances and returning a validated FrameDocument or the first cause below; frame_name, taking only that validated FrameDocument and returning the typed FrameName under the intrinsic hash bound; compare_frame, taking independently selected support, an expected typed FrameName, optional bytes and read allowances and returning the validated document on equality or the read cause followed by NameMismatch; and NumericContext::frame_integer, taking GrossAmount and the caller's existing exact context and returning Computed with the exact integer value and denominator one or numerical resource inability. FrameDocument has private fields, retained original bytes and a borrowed Frame view; only read_frame constructs it. A producer obtains its FrameDocument by passing encode_frame's bytes to read_frame with its own read allowances; no other constructor exists. FrameName is a distinct fixed-width role, not an Actor name, generic digest or authority proof. The constructor first checks entry count and allocation arithmetic against its allowances, then all descending adjacent names before equal adjacent names, then allocates and copies the already checked slice; it never sorts or deduplicates. An allocation failure returns UnavailableBudget with reason Allocation, the same reason read_frame reports. GrossAmount::from_be_bytes and FrameName::from_bytes each take one fixed-width 32-byte array and return the corresponding carrier without refusal; the former interprets every bit pattern, while the latter records an untrusted expected reference and establishes no binding. GrossAmount::bytes, FrameName::bytes, FrameDocument::bytes and FrameDocument::frame return immutable borrowed views without refusal; they add no validation or identity recipe. The pure comparison binds supplied bytes only, not the authenticity of the expected name or its selection from an epoch. No lookup, chain query, state transition or restoration is part of these operations.

For the same explicit support and allowances, read_frame and compare_frame follow (`rule:identity:document-family-order`) and refuse in this order: UnsupportedEdition for a caller selection other than the admitted frame operation; MissingFrame for absent bytes; UnavailableBudget (InputBytes) for the complete input-byte allowance or UnavailableBudget (Representation) for checked size arithmetic; Canonical for a strict pn-interchange full-consumption refusal, with CanonicalLimit returned immediately as UnavailableBudget (CanonicalAllocation) and CanonicalAllocation as UnavailableBudget (Allocation). No shared reservation callback is passed, so CanonicalReservation cannot arise. After complete canonical success, Shape refuses a non-map or non-unsigned-key envelope, missing header keys 0 or 1, a non-text namespace or a version not three unsigned integers; Edition then refuses namespace, major or minor unequal to org.peer-network.test.epoch.frame, 1, 0. Only then Shape refuses missing or additional content keys, a body other than the assigned map, or a key or amount not a byte string of exactly 32 bytes. Patch then refuses a nonzero patch; UnavailableBudget (Entries) refuses the entry allowance, UnavailableBudget (CollectionAllocation) the cumulative typed-storage ceiling and UnavailableBudget (Allocation) its allocator failure; only compare_frame after successful read and name computation can return NameMismatch. All checks of a stage complete before the next; header checks use keys 0 then 1, and body checks use encoded order, key before amount. Duplicate and misordered received map keys are Canonical, not repaired or delayed to typed construction. Strict canonical validation uses the existing borrowed validator and its allocation ledger; its child events remain provisional until full success. A bounded second traversal checks the already validated fixed schema without a second permissive decoder. Shape and patch walks allocate no typed collection, so entry and typed-storage inability follow Patch. Every inability retains its reason and charged usage; allocator failure is inability where encountered, never evidence of bad content. This is an operation-level order, not a reordering of interchange diagnostic subcauses. A structurally accepted higher minor yields Edition before content-key and body checks without changing generic interchange acceptance.

The caller declares finite complete input bytes, canonical-validator allocation bytes, body entries, cumulative typed-construction allocation bytes, and output encoding bytes and allocation bytes in the existing interchange units. There is no production default or implicit test ceiling. Input bytes are checked before parsing; the borrowed validator checks its own declared allocation limit before allocation without a shared reservation callback, and entry/typed-storage checks follow complete body shape and patch and precede constructing the Frame. Construction and encoding each use a separate ledger, neither silently sharing the reader's; their allowances and owned-constructor order are unchanged by the reader convention; the builder publishes which existing interchange limits are passed to each bounded construction and encoder call and carries their usage on success and inability. The input-byte bound also bounds heads, nesting, comparisons and the finite validation and materialization walks; no independent depth or solver policy is invented for this fixed schema. The numerical adapter uses the caller's explicit ExactLimits and ComparisonLimits, shares that NumericContext's cumulative budget across calls and retains its inability cause and usage; it creates no default context. Before the code cut lands, the owning builder measures zero, one and multiple entries, map-head transitions, the selected finite entry ceiling and its neighbour, and zero and maximal amounts, recording time, allocation and operation units and last-refused/first-passing allowance neighbours under (`rule:devperf:measurement`). It selects finite workload allowances from those measurements, not from the consensus sum bound. The intrinsic SHA-256 whole-octet input bound is two to the power 61 minus one bytes; read and encode cannot admit a document beyond it. This is a primitive applicability bound, not an amount refusal or a chosen workload size. Hashing visits the retained bytes once after validation and needs no separate digest-input copy.

The exact adapter expands the big-endian unsigned integer without sign extension or a machine-width intermediate, using the existing exact numerical owner and a denominator of one; resource inability propagates without a floating-point conversion, rounding or substitution of a historical burn. The builder supplies independently authored zero, largest-value and interior literals 256, two to the power 64, and two to the power 128 plus one, with a passing resource neighbour and a refusing neighbour derived from measured usage. The adapter is not a ledger or an implementation of (`rule:actepoch:frame-checking`); it leaves title-specific state and representation open under (`open:actepoch:title-admission`), and entering-state and ledger documents under (`open:actepoch:state-provenance`). The basis is network-v0.37.4 and no owner-map premise is encoded.

The first implementation cut publishes the exact CDDL of (`schema:identity:frame-document`) as packages/epoch/src/frame/frame.cddl and includes that single source in the separately selected epoch frame operation through the existing pn-interchange theory and registry APIs. The separate source and frame-document-registry control keep this independently selected subject outside common::registry(), whose embedded epoch, root and acts theories are fixed by (`rule:identity:common-publication`); this preserves that registry’s composition without a new registry format or parser. The assignment is the namespace/major/minor named by that schema, with no patch-specific theory. The epoch package's separate frame-document-registry control parses it through pn-interchange::Theory::parse, acquires it through Registry::acquire, compares its normalized theory with an independently transcribed schema literal and exercises the frame code against that held theory. The committed CDDL and this recorded assignment are the published repository material; the runtime Registry is a reader's held copy, not the publication authority. Parsing, acquisition and the exact correspondence control are builder evidence, not discharged by this paragraph. The test allocation's atoms org, peer-network, test, epoch, frame are distinct from the reserved sibling atoms acts and root and the act family's names; the test allocation has no case-folding or numeric-letter alias, and byte equality alone selects it. This discharges the local comparison with those recorded sibling allocations, not a global confusable-name search or a production allocation. Every sibling uses the same assignment-by-coordinate, immutable CDDL and existing parse/acquire semantics; a consolidated publication carrier is the interchange/publication owners' work and must not redefine the frame theory.

Independent canonical preimages and expected names belong in packages/epoch/tests/frame-document-vectors.json, with a standard-library-only Python producer beside them using its own CBOR emission and hashlib SHA-256, never the Rust codec, constructors or their output. Its inputs are separately authored symbolic maps and explicit byte-string widths; an implementation reviewer checks the preimages against the theory and the expected digest against that second route. The existing hash-citation family registry records the vector family, writing program and reader when the bytes land, by extension of its existing vector audit rather than a second checker. The canonical vector inputs are fixed symbolically: the empty frame; one all-zero Actor name with amount zero; one all-one-bit Actor name with the largest amount; and a two-entry frame whose first Actor has one zero octet followed by thirty-one all-one-bit octets and amount one, and whose second Actor has one octet of value one followed by thirty-one zero octets and amount 256. Every version, name-mismatch, malformed-input and combined-fault neighbour starts from that two-entry frame. The version neighbour sets patch one; the name-mismatch neighbour flips the least significant bit of octet 31, counting from zero, of its independently expected name, never of the candidate bytes. The separate refusal neighbours use namespace org.peer-network.test.epoch.frames, major two, minor one, the first amount with its last octet removed, the first Actor name replaced by unsigned integer zero, an added envelope key 3 with unsigned integer value zero, the body map head in its nonpreferred two-octet form 184, 2 in decimal, and one trailing octet zero. Additional malformed neighbours use an indefinite body map containing the same two pairs and its break, a three-entry body repeating the first pair immediately after itself, the two body pairs in reversed encoded order, an envelope array containing namespace/version/body in that order, missing envelope key 2, namespace replaced by unsigned integer zero, version [1, 0], body replaced by an empty array, the first Actor name with its last octet removed, and the first amount replaced by unsigned integer one. Except for the explicitly noncanonical, indefinite, duplicate, misordered and trailing forms, each mutation is encoded canonically without another change; unchanged fields retain the two-entry case's operands. Their first causes follow the stated read order: namespace/major/minor mutations are Edition, nonpreferred/indefinite/duplicate/misordered/trailing forms are Canonical, and the listed type, width and envelope/body mutations are Shape. The four combined faults use respectively that nonpreferred body-map head plus the wrong namespace, that wrong namespace plus the shortened first amount, that shortened first amount plus patch one, and patch one plus the specified expected-name bit flip; they return Canonical, Edition, Shape and Patch respectively. UnsupportedEdition uses selection major two with the unchanged bytes; MissingFrame uses admitted selection with absent bytes. Additional combined neighbours start from the same two-entry frame: wrong namespace plus envelope key 3 with unsigned zero yields Edition; minor one plus missing key 2 yields Edition; the shortened first amount with a declared entry ceiling of one yields Shape, while restoring that amount with the same ceiling yields UnavailableBudget (Entries); patch one with that ceiling and otherwise valid shape yields Patch. The existing frame-document-precedence control owns all these combined faults and allowance neighbours at packages/epoch/src/frame.rs, and frame-document-refusal owns single malformed neighbours there. Resource neighbours use measured charged usage minus one, equal and plus one where representable, with all other allowances sufficient: input-byte failure is InputBytes, validator ceiling is CanonicalAllocation, entry ceiling is Entries and typed-storage ceiling is CollectionAllocation, all within UnavailableBudget. The measured resource neighbours belong to the separate frame-document-inability control at packages/epoch/src/frame.rs, whose proposition is that each declared allowance refuses one below its measured charge with its stated reason and passes at and above it. Allocation is not a usage-derived allowance neighbour: injected allocator-failure evidence is held with the epoch measurement owner, who must admit its concrete injection route and site before that case is built or claimed; this cut does not choose or build an injection mechanism. There is no reservation-callback neighbour because this reader passes none. Checked arithmetic overflow remains Representation. No measured production allowance is assigned by the entry ceiling of one used solely to isolate this precedence control. These are symbolic input specifications, not computed example bytes or digests. It includes combined faults to fix first-cause precedence and explicit resource neighbours separately from malformed input. The numerical owner's literals are authored independently of the adapter; source-to-frame controls use independently selected recognized destruction identities, including an alias, and never infer chain recognition from codec vectors. The first cut's focused evidence is the round-trip, name, malformed-input, precedence, registry and version controls at epoch plus exact-input at records; gross, static-opacity and epoch-reference binding stay held for their owning successor cuts. Larger generated corpora and workload calibration beyond the selected first-cut allowance are later evidence, not an invented executed claim.

**Schema (Frame document theory form)** · `schema:identity:frame-document` The designed envelope has namespace at key 0, version at key 1 and only body key 2, under (`schema:interchange:global`) and (`gram:interchange:assignable-fragment`). The body is a map from 32-byte Actor names to 32-byte amounts. A map expresses the unique-key association directly and inherits canonical key order from (`lang:interchange:data-language`); an ordered pair list would need separate uniqueness and ordering predicates without adding information. The concrete test theory below uses org.peer-network.test.epoch.frame at major 1, minor 0, with an unsigned 64-bit patch. Its allocation is beneath the repository's org.peer-network.test reservation by the dotted atoms of (`gram:interchange:label-grammar`), with epoch and then frame as successive atoms, not by raw string prefix. This locates the referred document beneath its referring epoch's test name under (`schema:identity:common-epoch`) and (`schema:identity:common-document-parameters`). No production name is assigned; the local sibling-name review and repository publication route are admitted under (`amend:identity:frame-document-design`), while theory parsing, publication and executable correspondence remain builder evidence under (`open:identity:frame-document-admission`). No example bytes are assigned.

```cddl
frame-document = { 0: "org.peer-network.test.epoch.frame", 1: [1, 0, patch], 2: frame }
patch = 0..18446744073709551615
frame = { * actor-name => gross-amount }
actor-name = bstr .size 32
gross-amount = bstr .size 32
```

**Rule (Frame canonical bytes)** · `rule:identity:frame-document-bytes` Encode the complete envelope and nested map through pn-interchange: preferred heads, definite lengths, unique keys and bytewise-lexicographic order of encoded keys, with no trailing bytes or repair. All Actor keys have the same byte-string head and width, so their encoded-key order is their full 32-byte order under (`rule:identity:actor-document-consumers`). Refuse a noncanonical spelling, a repeated or misordered key, any other key or amount width, another envelope shape, or a value outside the selected theory. No sorting, deduplication, padding, truncation or numeric CBOR substitution repairs received bytes. Zero entries and an empty map are structurally representable; whether a supplied frame has the required Actor domain is a separate closure/oracle question under (`rule:identity:frame-document-domain`). Patch does not change the theory under (`inv:interchange:patch-identity`), but remains part of the identified bytes. For the test theory at major 1 and minor 0, a frame producer writes patch 0 and the successor epoch reader requires that patch after complete body shape and before entry/typed-storage checks under (`rule:identity:document-family-order`); another structurally valid patch is not admitted for this application, so one frame content has one name. This application rule does not change generic structural acceptance or higher-minor tolerance under (`def:interchange:acceptance`), which does not establish knowledge of an unheld frame theory for closure.

**Definition (Frame amount interpretation)** · `def:identity:frame-amount` For the 32 bytes b indexed from 0 to 31, the amount is the unsigned integer sum of b[i] times 256 to the power 31 minus i. The first byte is most significant, zero is 32 zero bytes, and the domain is every integer from zero through two to the power 256 minus one. Every string of exactly 32 bytes denotes one amount; there is no reserved amount, sign, exponent, fraction, NaN or amount-level sentinel. Bytewise order equals numeric order, allowing the same ordering comparison without another canonical spelling. This is not a CBOR uint, whose ground domain is narrower under (`ntn:interchange:ground-terms`). The fixed network unit supplies the interpretation and is not a per-entry scale. A correctly sized amount can still fail gross-progression, domain or oracle equality checks; structural validity is not evidence of destruction.

**Rule (Frame source and arithmetic boundary)** · `rule:identity:frame-amount-boundary` The oracle reads the explicit positive whole-number output amount in the selected burnt asset's minimal units under (`def:devnet:wire-burn`) and (`rule:lineage:burn-reading`), attributes it through the accepted Actor document and sums distinct recognized burns under (`def:lineage:gross`). Conversion into the frame amount is the identity on that integer, with no repricing, decimal scaling, fraction or rounding. The static check instead decodes the supplied integer and performs (`rule:actepoch:frame-checking`), without a chain read, Actor opening or claim about source validity. Its numerical adapter must embed that integer exactly as a rational with denominator one before using the calculation's exact arithmetic, and report resource inability rather than approximate it. The current pn-records NumericContext::literal and BurnAmount carriers in packages/records/src/numerics.rs and the exact rational ledger arithmetic in packages/epoch/src/ledger.rs are existing places for this interpretation, not an implemented successor adapter; the publication and numerical owners owe the conversion, caller resource declarations and independent boundary evidence. The current carrier's unit string is internal to that historical interface and is not an extra frame member.

**Argument (Frame sum range)** · `arg:identity:frame-amount-range` The selected Elements release under (`dec:devnet:release`) supplies a finite output-count bound without a lifetime issuance or physical-capacity premise. At elements-29.4.1rc1, [CBlockHeader](https://github.com/ElementsProject/elements/blob/elements-29.4.1rc1/src/primitives/block.h) stores nTime as an unsigned 32-bit integer, [GetMedianTimePast](https://github.com/ElementsProject/elements/blob/elements-29.4.1rc1/src/chain.h) takes the median of the last eleven headers, and [ContextualCheckBlockHeader](https://github.com/ElementsProject/elements/blob/elements-29.4.1rc1/src/validation.cpp) requires each next timestamp to exceed that median. These are premises about a chain valid under those rules, not about arbitrary stored headers. Once eleven headers exist, replacing the oldest timestamp by one above the median cannot decrease the median; after eleven further headers all entries exceed the starting median, so it increases by at least one. With at most two to the power 32 possible median values, including the initial eleven headers and a final partial window gives fewer than eleven plus eleven times two to the power 32 headers, hence fewer than two to the power 36 blocks. For non-genesis blocks, CheckBlock in that validation.cpp limits non-witness serialized block bytes times WITNESS_SCALE_FACTOR to MAX_BLOCK_WEIGHT; [the constants](https://github.com/ElementsProject/elements/blob/elements-29.4.1rc1/src/consensus/consensus.h) are four and four million, giving at most one million non-witness bytes per block, fewer than two to the power 20. [CConfidentialValue](https://github.com/ElementsProject/elements/blob/elements-29.4.1rc1/src/primitives/confidential.h) encodes an explicit value in one prefix byte and eight big-endian value bytes, consistent with vendor/elements/src/confidential/value.rs and packages/wallet/src/decoder.rs; the oracle recognizes only explicit amounts under (`rule:identity:frame-amount-boundary`), so each recognized amount is at most two to the power 64 minus one, and each distinct output occupies at least nine of those serialized bytes. Genesis bypasses that validation but [the genesis constructors](https://github.com/ElementsProject/elements/blob/elements-29.4.1rc1/src/kernel/chainparams.cpp) create one output and call [AppendInitialIssuance](https://github.com/ElementsProject/elements/blob/elements-29.4.1rc1/src/issuance.cpp) with at most one asset output and one reissuance-token output, giving at most three outputs, also below two to the power 20. Even counting one output per non-witness byte in every later block is a conservative upper bound: fewer than two to the power 36 blocks times fewer than two to the power 20 outputs per block gives fewer than two to the power 56 output occurrences in one valid chain. The recognized set under (`def:lineage:gross`) is a subset of those distinct occurrences, with aliases counted once, so cumulative recognized burns are less than two to the power 120 minimal units, independently of issuance or reissuance. This establishes representability for that selected format and validation rules; each tag change under (`dec:devnet:release`) re-establishes these six premises at the new tag in its dependency review before the deployment declares that tag; a different chain or changed rules need their own source-bound proof before admission, and deployment provenance and oracle recognition retain their existing holds. Nothing wraps, saturates, truncates, discards burns or rounds, and an out-of-domain publication refusal is not the design. The conditional arithmetic is the final general step: for an eight-byte output bound and N less than two to the power 192 distinct recognized outputs, the sum is at most N times two to the power 64 minus one, at most the product of two to the power 192 minus one and two to the power 64 minus one, strictly less than two to the power 256; the selected chain's count is already less than two to the power 56.

**Rule (Frame Actor domain)** · `rule:identity:frame-document-domain` Every funded Actor remains in the supplied frame with its cumulative gross total over the lineage accepted by the epoch, including an Actor with no current act or with zero residual balance. Missing required entries are not zero. An Actor first funded in this epoch has an explicit entry for its recognized cumulative gross, even when it performs no act; its transition uses the explicit initial state required by (`rule:actepoch:frame-checking`), not an absent-entry convention that could conceal omitted history. The frame alone neither proves that funding is first nor supplies the initial-state document. Static validation compares the supplied domain and amounts with entering state and checks the exact transition; the oracle separately compares the complete recognized Actor domain and integer totals. An empty frame is sufficient only when those checks establish an empty required domain. Unknown Actor prefixes do not authorize static opening or filtering. Actor succession and old/new-name attribution remain (`open:identity:actor-document-succession`), not a second name for the same contribution chosen by this map.

**Amendment (Frame identity adjudication)** · `amend:identity:frame-document-adjudication` The proposed object under (`alg:identity:adjudication`) is the complete validated canonical frame document, independently supplied to the epoch reader and named by the epoch's second member under (`def:actepoch:epoch`) and (`schema:identity:common-epoch`). The consumer must bind those supplied bytes to that fixed-width reference before using their amounts. An epoch-root commitment under (`schema:identity:common-result`) and (`rule:identity:common-root`) does not itself establish that the separately supplied frame is its named object; complete typed comparison supplies no compact public reference for a variable map, and an Actor identifier or a local serial cannot name the entire frame. The benefit under (`crit:identity:benefit`) is independently comparing this complete supplied document to a compact reference, not a freshness digest or a digest of each amount. The walk admits a semantic identity under (`case:identity:semantic`): the complete validated canonical frame document is the reviewed canonical byte encoding defined as the semantic object; the namespace and version inside the bytes separate the role, the recipe identifier selects the operation, and patch zero under (`rule:identity:frame-document-bytes`) keeps one content to one name. This meets the exception stated in (`case:identity:artifact`) and the semantic column of (`tab:identity:class-properties`), rather than adding a second artifact-domain-separation mechanism. Completeness covers the whole validated document; determinism and recomputability rest on its public canonical bytes; collision and second-preimage resistance condition comparison; exclusion of incidental presentation is the admitted patch-zero spelling. Hidden fields: none. The admission is design for the separately selected producer and supplied-FrameName comparison arriving together, with no chain truth, authenticity of the expected name or external enablement. An independently distributed release artifact still owes (`case:identity:artifact`); no release manifest is invented for this pure semantic comparison.

**Definition (Frame document identity recipe)** · `def:identity:frame-document-recipe` The design-admitted recipe is SHA-256 of the entire received canonical frame-document bytes, producing all 32 digest bytes in digest byte order without truncation, reversal, a second hash, an added preamble or hexadecimal text. Namespace, complete version including patch, all keys and amounts participate. The recipe identifier is pn-epoch/frame-document/v1, owned by the separately selected epoch frame operation under (`amend:identity:frame-document-design`) and (`amend:decomposition:common-static`); its producer and supplied-name comparison consumer arrive together in the first pure cut. The identifier is operation-selection metadata and enters no hashed byte; changing its package component changes neither the complete-document preimage nor any resulting name, and adds no preamble. The test namespace org.peer-network.test.epoch.frame and version inside the byte sequence identify the designed frame role; publication and executable correspondence remain owed by the first pure cut. No prefix grinding, blinder, nonce, salt or hidden field exists; no hiding or authenticity is claimed. Binding assumes collision and second-preimage resistance and uses the existing SHA-256 line of (`amend:identity:sha-two-line`) under (`rule:crypto:operation-scope`), not a new dependency choice. This is a design admission under (`gate:identity:implementation`), with no digest or vector computed here and no executed or enabled consumer claim.

**Table (Frame identity admission dossier)** · `tab:identity:frame-document-dossier` The following completes the pure-operation design admission under (`req:identity:admission-record`); implementation evidence and external enablement remain held.

| Item | Designed contract and hold |
| --- | --- |
| Object and class | Whole canonical frame document at org.peer-network.test.epoch.frame, major 1, minor 0, application patch 0, semantic computational equality under (`amend:identity:frame-document-adjudication`); no hidden fields; theory publication and strict-validation evidence remain owed |
| Owner and producer | pn-epoch codec/recipe pn-epoch/frame-document/v1 using pn-interchange and the reviewed direct sha2 route; pn-records owns Frame, GrossAmount, FrameName and exact conversion; later epoch assembly and independent oracle recognition remain held |
| Consumer and decision | Pure compare_frame consumes an independently supplied typed expected name and candidate bytes, accepts only the validated matching document and otherwise returns the ordered cause; the later epoch reader resolves its second reference under (`schema:identity:common-epoch`). Unavailable bytes are not a zero frame; equal reference with different complete retained bytes is a conflict, not permission to overwrite |
| Cut | A records-only preparation cut may deliver the passive carrier and exact adapter first; after the proposed registry-edge reviews and admission, the coordinated epoch cut delivers codec, name producer and supplied-name comparison together before any successor use; reference resolution, static checking and oracle enablement remain separate held cuts, with no enabled producer awaiting a future consumer |
| Staleness | Any byte change, including patch, changes the named preimage; only patch-zero documents are admitted operation inputs; projection, primitive, canonicalization or role changes require a new recipe identifier under (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`) |
| Migration and unit | No historical carrier, unit or identity is reinterpreted; a changed asset/unit must have a separately declared deployment context or a newly admitted document interpretation/version, never a new reading of already published amounts; context selection and its unambiguous historical association remain the deployment/publication owners' admission |
| Non-claims | No chain truth, completeness, finality, Actor authority, uniqueness of humans, mathematical closure, settlement, numerical conformance, hiding or authenticated publication follows from hash equality |
| Evidence | Design discharges placement, identifier, local sibling-name review, refusal order and semantic assurance; the records adapter has focused evidence under (`verif:identity:frame-document-controls`); builder owes published theory and correspondence, independent canonical preimages/names, refusal controls and measured codec allowances; oracle and deployment owners retain application of chain premises, attribution and complete recognized-source evidence |

**Verification (Frame document controls)** · `verif:identity:frame-document-controls` The records exact-input selector is implemented and executed; the other nine remain designed and unexecuted: the six epoch selectors for the first pure cut and the epoch-reference, gross and static-opacity selectors for their successor cuts, each with one proposition per site under (`rule:testing:site`), not minted claims. The first pure cut owns the round-trip, name, malformed-input, precedence, registry and version controls at distinct packages/epoch/src/frame.rs private assertion sites and exact-input at packages/records/src/numerics.rs; independently authored literals/preimages and selected recognized sets supply expected values, never the constructor or encoder under test. The implementation must register its actual assertions and independent fixtures before claiming them.

| Selector | Proposed site | Single proposition and selected inputs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| frame-document-round-trip | packages/epoch/src/frame.rs private round-trip control | The four selected canonical frames, including zero and the largest amount, round-trip with exact independently specified bytes and map values |
| frame-document-name | packages/epoch/src/frame.rs private name control | Names of the four independent canonical preimages agree with frame_name, and compare_frame accepts the independently supplied matching FrameName and returns NameMismatch for the specified bit-flip neighbour |
| frame-document-refusal | packages/epoch/src/frame.rs private refusal control | Each specified single malformed-input neighbour refuses with its recorded first cause under the admitted read order |
| frame-document-precedence | packages/epoch/src/frame.rs private precedence control | Each specified combined fault or ceiling-one precedence neighbour returns the recorded first cause, including Edition before additional/missing content keys, Shape before Patch or Entries, and Patch before Entries, at this sole precedence site |
| frame-document-inability | packages/epoch/src/frame.rs private inability control in the epoch codec cut | Each declared allowance refuses one below its measured charge with its stated reason and passes at and above it; allocator injection remains held with the measurement owner |
| frame-document-registry | packages/epoch/src/frame.rs private registry control | The published frame theory parsed and acquired by the operation equals the independently transcribed normalized assigned theory |
| frame-document-epoch-reference | packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs in the successor reader cut | The resolved frame is accepted only when its recomputed FrameName equals the epoch document's supplied frame member under (`schema:identity:common-epoch`); a mismatch refuses before its amounts are used |
| frame-document-gross | packages/records/tests/public/burn.rs, after successor attribution placement | The independently selected recognized set projects to the exact integer frame totals: two distinct burns for one Actor add and an alias of one is counted once |
| frame-document-exact-input | packages/records/src/numerics.rs private exact-input control | Conversion of independently authored integer totals, including zero and the largest frame amount, into the calculation's exact values with denominator one preserves those totals; a passing allowance neighbour and a refusing resource-inability neighbour show that inability is not approximation |
| frame-document-version | packages/epoch/src/frame.rs private version control; later epoch integration retains its own hold | The major-1 minor-0 producer writes patch zero, and its pure application reader accepts that independently authored neighbour but refuses another structurally accepted patch after complete body shape and before entry/typed-storage checks under (`rule:identity:document-family-order`) |
| frame-document-static-opacity | packages/epoch/tests/inputs.rs in the successor cut | The selected supplied frame transitions are checked without opening Actor names: arbitrary opaque names, a newly funded Actor and a retained inactive Actor pass with the independent entering state, while a missing required entry or decreasing gross refuses; a resolver witness establishes no Actor-opening call |

**Open Question (Frame admission completion)** · `open:identity:frame-document-admission` Owners: identity/publication designers for theory publication, recipe/role binding and independent vectors; publication/decomposition and numerical designers for operation placement, the identifier's package component and exact conversion; development-profile and oracle designers for the selected chain premises, attribution and complete evidence. The concrete test theory uses org.peer-network.test.epoch.frame under the repository's test reservation; no production allocation is made, which remains (`open:vision:publication-agreement`). The map form and integer byte order are unchanged. The pure-operation design discharges the dossier, package/identifier placement, local sibling-name review and the semantic assurance of (`amend:identity:frame-document-adjudication`); the records-only preparation cut implements the passive carriers, ordered constructor and exact integer adapter with its exact-input control and measured resource neighbour; the later coordinated producer/comparison cut still owes theory parsing and repository publication, exact registry/code correspondence, independent vectors, measured codec limits and the round-trip, name, malformed-input, precedence, registry and version controls. The epoch codec and vector reader also wait for the proposed sha2 and serde_json edges and their dated review under (`amend:decomposition:common-static`); a records-only preparation cut may precede that review. Gross attribution remains held with the oracle owner; static-opacity and epoch-reference binding remain held with the epoch owner, whose frame-name operation makes the latter site reachable without a host edge. External producer/consumer enablement, release artifact binding and production allocation remain held; a finite passing control cannot close them. The source-bound proof of (`arg:identity:frame-amount-range`) establishes the range for chains valid under the selected Elements format and validation rules; selecting another chain or changing those rules requires a corresponding proof before admission, with no representational refusal in its place. Source provenance and recognition evidence remain with the development-profile and oracle owners. Neighbouring designs retain their own owners: entering-state and ledger documents under (`open:actepoch:state-provenance`), oracle lookup and recognition evidence under (`open:identity:chain-payload-owner`) and (`open:lineage:realization`), epoch-root representation and recipe under (`schema:identity:common-result`) and (`rule:identity:common-root`), with pure-operation design admission under (`amend:identity:common-static-admission`) and remaining publication, implementation and enablement evidence under (`open:identity:common-document-admission`), marker lookup, compatibility and the major-1 zero-root refusal operation under (`open:identity:chain-marker-admission`), and the act and witnessed-act first-cut pure operations built beside the later common documents designed under (`amend:identity:common-document-design`), with remaining implementation and enablement evidence under (`open:identity:common-document-admission`). The current BurnAmount, historical publication codec and every admitted recipe keep their meanings under (`conv:actepoch:history`) and (`rule:identity:recipe-permanence`); no present publication is restamped or reinterpreted.
